# FixMy.Energy Portal — Project Context

## What This Is
A single-file admin + field portal for a solar diagnostic / battery retrofit / new solar business.
All logic, HTML, CSS, and JS lives in **`portal.html`**. There is no build step — Netlify deploys the repo as-is.

## Live Site
Deployed on Netlify from the `main` branch. Active feature branch: `claude/fixmy-energy-setup-R9lDn`.

## Key Files
- `portal.html` — the entire portal (admin, ops, setter, tech, customer views)
- `index.html` — public-facing landing page
- `sign.html` — customer-facing Sign & Pay page (Stripe Elements + agreement signature)
- `netlify/functions/claude-vision.js` — proxies Anthropic API calls (photo AI categorization; called by whichever function is running the sync, not from upload anymore)
- `netlify/functions/lib/quoya.js` — shared Quoya photo-categorization logic (fetch pending job_photos rows, call Anthropic vision directly, save results) used by both functions below
- `netlify/functions/quoya-sync.js` — manual "Sync with Quoya" button, scoped to one lead, synchronous
- `netlify/functions/quoya-sync-background.js` — nightly full-sweep categorization of all pending photos (scheduled in netlify.toml)
- `netlify/functions/regrid-lookup.js` — proxies Regrid parcel lookup (keeps API key server-side)
- `netlify/functions/ghl-calendar.js` — books GHL calendar appointments + upserts contacts
- `netlify/functions/ghl-diag-agreement.js` — upserts GHL contact + adds `send-diag-agreement` tag
- `netlify/functions/ghl-inbound.js` — receives inbound GHL webhook payloads
- `netlify/functions/ghl-status-update.js` — receives GHL status webhooks, updates Supabase
- `netlify/functions/sign-init.js` — validates sign_token, creates Stripe PaymentIntent server-side
- `netlify/functions/sign-complete.js` — verifies payment success, marks invoice paid + agreement signed
- `.claude/settings.local.json` — gitignored; holds Netlify PAT env var + Supabase MCP permissions

## Supabase
- Project URL: `https://kbtobyoumvbcxfbugsid.supabase.co`
- Main table: `customers` — holds all leads and jobs for both FixMy and New Solar
- Other tables: `team_members`, `rep_agreements`, `marketing_expenses`
- MCP is configured — use it to run SQL directly instead of asking user to copy/paste
- Anon key is in portal.html as `SUPA_KEY` (safe — protected by RLS)
- Service role key is `SUPA_SERVICE_KEY` Netlify env var (server-side only, never in client code)

## Portal Architecture
- `supabase.createClient(SUPA_URL, SUPA_KEY)` — client pattern used everywhere
- `adminCustomers` — global in-memory array of all customer/lead records
- `lead_category` field: `'fixmy'` | `'new_solar'` — drives which pipeline renders
- `sold_type` field: `null` = lead, `'diagnostic'` | `'battery_retrofit'` | `'monitoring'` | `'new_solar'` = job
- `isNSEdit` / `isNSSave` flags gate New Solar-specific fields in editor/save

## Business Lines & Accounting SOPs (2026-07-07, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
These are Standard Operating Procedures. Do not re-ask how the business lines work.

### 1. FixMy.Energy (internal line name; customer-facing brand is Solar Review)
- **Services:** Diagnostics, Inverter Swaps, Warranty Replacements, Batteries.
- **Revenue:** Solar Review charges the CUSTOMER directly for diagnostics, inverter swaps, warranty replacements, and battery sales **when batteries are sold in-house**. Going forward, **Cosmic charges the customer for battery sales** (battery revenue shifts out of Solar Review's books when Cosmic sells/charges).
- **Accounting requirements:**
  - Track revenue (payments ledger — `payments` table).
  - Track operations costs of doing business per job ("sub sheet"): pending vs completed payments to ops/partners.
  - Rep commissions on sold jobs for ALL sales reps EXCEPT Dennis (Dennis-sold = no commission).
  - Future: monthly net must break out into equity distributions (design for it; don't block on it).
  - Reports must be real, easy to follow for accounting + payroll. Automate as much as possible.

### Commission math (2026-07-07, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
- **FixMy rep commission:** `Revenue − Sub-Sheet Costs = Net Revenue`; rep earns **40% of Net Revenue**. Dennis-sold jobs (rep_id `tech4`) = no commission. Commission finalizes only after job costs are entered — costs first, then commission.
- **Dialer/Setter split (2026-07-27, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE):** the **40%-of-net rep-pay pool is FIXED — company/equity margin is identical no matter who closes.** Rep pay is directed to whoever set/closed: **same person sets & closes → 40% to them; a distinct setter (dialer) → 20% setter / 20% closer** (still 40% total). **No owner/Dennis carve-out on the split** — zeroing a closer's share would change the margin, which must stay constant (this SUPERSEDES the old "Dennis-sold = no commission" behavior *inside the split math*; Dennis's own draw vs. distribution is a payout/distribution matter, not a margin one). Dialer = the setter role (reuses `customers.setter_name`); a Tech can dial, a setter-only can't close. The dialer's "Booked!" (`bbDialCommit`) stamps `setter_name` = the dialing rep; admin reassigning a different closer (inline Tech dropdown on the card, or the editor's Setter/Dialer + Assigned Rep selects) is what triggers the split. Computed + locked in from the FixMy job editor's accounting panel: **`edRecordFixmySplit(cid)`** recomputes net authoritatively at click time and inserts a `rep_commission` (closer, 20%/40%) and a **`setter_commission`** (setter, 20%; new `commissions.kind`, no migration — `kind` is free text) row, both `sold`→`paid`. Both book to P&L **COGS "Incentives - Sales"** (`finComputePnl`/`finMonthlySeries`) and show per-payee in the Finance **Commissions** sub-tab (`kindLbl.setter_commission='setter split'`). Worked examples (verified): PW3 $13,950−$12,500 = $1,450 net → pool $580 (same person) or $290 setter + $290 closer (split); net $4,000 → $800/$800; Diagnostic $699−$300 = $399 → $159.60 or $79.80/$79.80.
- **Top Tier / New Solar sold commissions:** entered MANUALLY per deal when the provider confirms.
- **Manager overrides (TT + NS):** paid TO Dennis — they are 1099 income to **Solar Review Corp**, not payouts. Track as receivables: override sold (owed to SRC) vs received.
- **Travel reimbursements:** Top Tier only, manual entries, owed to the rep.
- Data model: `job_costs` (per-job pending/paid cost line items) + `commissions` (kind: rep_commission | override | travel_reimbursement; status: sold | paid) tables — migration `20260707_accounting.sql`.

### 2. Top Tier — ⚠️ RELATIONSHIP ENDED 2026-08-20, per Dennis (AUTHORITATIVE)
No longer doing business with Top Tier. **All 35 `lead_category='top_tier'` customer rows were archived** (`archived=true`) on 2026-08-20 — none were sold jobs (`sold_type` was null on all of them), so there was no in-flight commission/accounting cleanup needed. Restorable from Admin → 🗄 Archived if that ever changes.
- **The "⭐ Top Tier" button was removed from the New Lead form** (`portal.html`, the shared admin+setter "Business Line" picker) so a new Top Tier lead can no longer be created. `nlSetCat('top_tier')` is now unreachable from the UI but was left defined rather than deleted — it degrades safely (`document.getElementById('nlCatTT')` returns null, already guarded).
- **Top Tier category tab hidden from admin Leads (fixed 2026-08-25, per Dennis — the tab reappearing read as a regression):** the admin Leads screen's `⭐ Top Tier (0)` pill rendered unconditionally, so it stayed visible forever even at a permanent 0 count — the sales-rep dashboard's equivalent tab was already conditional (`if (ttList.length) …`), so reps never saw this, but admin did. The admin tab now uses the same `ttLeads.length ?` guard, so it disappears the moment the count is 0 (which it is, permanently, post-archival) and reappears automatically if an archived Top Tier lead is ever restored — no code change needed either way if that happens.
- **Still deliberately left in place:** the lead editor's Business Line dropdown option (only relevant if an archived TT lead is ever manually restored), and all Finance/Commissions code that renders historical Top Tier override/commission entries (`finRenderCommissions`, deposit classification "Top Tier" button, P&L labels) — this is **past financial data that still needs to report correctly**, not new pipeline usage. Same reasoning as never deleting a Business Line's accounting history when a line goes dormant.
- **Model (historical, kept for reference):** sales rep + manager override payscale ONLY. Solar Review did NOT charge the customer — Top Tier charged the customer.
- **Accounting requirements (historical):** track sold commissions, paid commissions, and travel reimbursements. Nothing else.

### 3. New Solar (customer/rep-facing name: "Axia/QCell" — see below, 2026-08-21)
- **Model:** direct partnership with **Axia/QCell** as the installer going forward. **Trio is no longer part of the business strategy** (per Dennis, 2026-08-21) — dropped with zero code/data cleanup needed, since "Trio" was always pure documentation prose, never a tracked `lead_category`, `assigned_ops`, or any other coded entity (confirmed by grep — the only real installer-partner identity in code is the `ops4`/"Axia" Ops Partner used for FixMy-side job routing, which is a separate concept from this business line). Sales rep + manager override payscale ONLY. Solar Review does NOT charge the customer — the solar provider charges the customer.
- **Accounting requirements:** track sold commissions and paid commissions. Nothing else historically — **now being built out further, see "Axia/QCell pipeline rework" below.**

## Business Lines
### FixMy.Energy (diagnostic / battery retrofit)
10-step pipeline via `step` field (was 9 steps until 2026-08-25 — see "Jobs Tab: Commission Dashboard" below for why "Installed" was inserted as its own step rather than a separate field):
1. Eval Booked → 2. Photos → 3. Diagnostic → 4. Analysis → 5. Follow Up
6. BR Sold / Deposit Collected → 7. Not Sold / Dead → 8. Install Booked → 9. Installed → 10. Monitoring

### New Solar
Pipeline via `solar_status` field using `NS_STATUSES` object:
`ns_eval_booked` → `ns_eval_canceled` → `ns_welcome_scheduled` → `ns_welcome_rescheduled` →
`ns_welcome_dead` → `ns_welcome_closed` → `ns_button_up` → `ns_call_dead` →
`ns_permit_submitted` → `ns_install_scheduled` → `ns_pto`
★ Job phase starts at `ns_welcome_closed`
⚠️ `ns_eval_rescheduled` exists in `NS_STATUSES` (lead-phase) but was missing from this diagram until 2026-08-21 — code and doc were drifted; diagram now matches the real object.

### Axia/QCell pipeline rework (2026-08-21, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Firm decision on how this business line works going forward. Four changes, done together:
1. **Display rename "New Solar" → "Axia/QCell", display-only** (same pattern as the FixMy.Energy → Solar Review rebrand): every user-facing string across nav tabs, category pills, the Business Line dropdown, card badges, empty states, and Finance/Commissions labels (~30 spots in portal.html) now reads "Axia/QCell". **Nothing internal changed** — `lead_category:'new_solar'`, `NS_STATUSES` keys, `solar_status` values, `commission_new_solar`, and every other coded identifier are untouched. Code comments describing the internal `new_solar` category were deliberately left as-is (not user-facing).
2. **Trio dropped as an installer partner** — see the "3. New Solar" business-line note above. No data cleanup was needed since Trio was never a coded entity.
3. **Britney Seacombe's "non-clickable" Jobs card — real defensive fix, not just a stale-tab diagnosis.** Her record checked out fine (`sold_type:'new_solar'`, `solar_status:'ns_welcome_closed'` — correctly job-phase, confirmed `sold_type` auto-derives from `solar_status` via `NS_JOB_KEYS.indexOf(...)` at save time, so that coupling was never actually broken). The one real failure mode found: `openJob(id)` looked the id up in the in-memory `adminCustomers` array and **silently returned** if not found — indistinguishable from "the card doesn't do anything" if that array is stale (edited elsewhere since the tab loaded). `openJob` is now `async`: on a cache miss it calls a new `_refreshAdminCustomersQuiet()` (refetches just `adminCustomers`, not a full `loadAdminDashboard()` — the latter would also yank the admin back to the Dashboard view) and retries once before giving up with a visible alert instead of a silent no-op.
4. **Commission & Revenue Estimates — structure built now, formula deliberately deferred.** Dennis will teach the actual commission calculation separately; per his instruction this pass only builds the reporting scaffolding it'll plug into, with manual entry standing in for the computed value until then:
   - The lead editor's Axia/QCell accounting panel (`edLoadAccounting`, split out of the old shared Top-Tier/New-Solar `else` branch into its own `cat==='new_solar'` branch — Top Tier's original three-button shape is untouched in its own branch) now has **three** buttons: **+ Setter Commission**, **+ Tech Commission**, **+ Company Revenue** (relabeled from "+ Override → SRC" — same mechanism, still `kind:'override'`/`payee:'solar_review_corp'`, just named for what it actually represents on this line: Solar Review Corp's estimated income from the deal). **No Sub Sheet / job_costs section renders here** — confirmed structurally already true (the `isFixmy` gate that shows scope-of-work costs is `false` for `new_solar`), matching "there will not be any scope of work costs like FixMy's pipeline."
   - **New:** `edAddCommission` now resolves a real payee for `kind:'setter_commission'` — previously any non-override kind was paid to whichever `rep` (Tech/closer) was passed in, so a "setter" commission would have wrongly been entered against the Tech. It now reads the lead's own `setter_name` field (the same field the FixMy 20/20 split already reads) and resolves it to a rep_id via the existing `_repIdByName` helper when the setter is a known team member, falling back to the raw name string otherwise — so Setter and Tech commissions are tracked against different people, not collapsed onto one.
   - **New:** a pipeline-level rollup — `_nsCommSummaryHtml()` / `_loadNsCommSummary()` — renders three tiles (Setter Commission / Tech Commission / Company Revenue, each showing total + how much is already paid/received) at the top of the Jobs tab whenever viewing Axia/QCell, aggregating every `commissions` row with `line:'new_solar'` across the whole pipeline. This is the "report any Commission Estimates for Setters and Techs and company estimates on Revenue coming in" ask. Explicitly labeled **"Estimates from manual per-deal entries — not yet the final commission formula"** so nobody mistakes a placeholder total for a finalized number before Dennis's real calculation replaces the manual `prompt()` entry.
- ⚠️ **When Dennis provides the real commission formula**, the natural integration point is `edAddCommission`'s `new_solar` branch (currently a plain `prompt()` for the dollar amount, same UX as Top Tier) — replace the prompt with a computed value the same way FixMy's `edRecordFixmySplit` computes its 40%-of-net split automatically instead of asking. The rollup tiles need no changes either way, since they only aggregate whatever lands in `commissions`.

## Portal Views
- **Admin** — full CRM: leads, jobs, schedule, team, marketing tabs
- **Jobs** (Ops) — Cosmic Solar / Axia / Jon / John see only their assigned jobs
- **Tech/Sales** — setter lead capture + personal dashboard
- **Customer** — magic-link self-service portal

### A rep session could render the real admin dashboard (fixed 2026-08-19 — real bug, not cosmetic)
Reported: Ronda (Setter) saw the full admin nav (Team & Hiring / Agents / Finance / Personal / Business Model) above her own scoped Leads view. **Root cause:** `loadRepDashboard` auto-opens the New Lead form on login (`autoOpenForm:true`); if she completed it before `loadSalesDashboard`'s async setup reached the line assigning `window.salesRefresh`, the confirmation panel's "Done" button fell back to calling `renderAdminView(adminView)` directly — a plain global function with **no role check**, callable from any session. It looked scoped to just her 1 lead only by coincidence: `loadSalesDashboard` repurposes the same `adminCustomers` global that `renderAdminView` reads from, filled with only her own records. Team & Hiring / Agents / Finance / Personal run their **own unscoped queries** and would have shown her the real company-wide data, not a filtered view — this was a data-exposure bug, not a stray button.
- **Fix:** `_adminOnlyGuard()` (near `CURRENT_ROLE`, top of the file) is called first in every admin-only render function — `renderAdminView`, `renderJobsView`, `renderAdminCanvassView`, `renderScheduleView`, `renderTeamView`, `renderAgentsView`, `renderFinanceView`, `renderPersonalView`. A non-admin session bounces to `window.salesRefresh()` (their own dashboard) instead of ever executing the admin view's body — closes this structurally so no future stray call anywhere in the file can leak admin data to a rep, setter, or Ops partner again. `_closeNewLeadRefresh()` also fixes the actual race (retries `salesRefresh` once instead of falling through to admin).
- ⚠️ **Any new admin-only `render*View()` function must call `_adminOnlyGuard()` as its first line.** This is not enforced automatically.

### Per-role portal access (as of 2026-08-19, post-fix)
| Role | Login routes to | What they can reach |
|---|---|---|
| **Admin** (`ADMIN_CODE`) | `loadAdminDashboard()` → `renderAdminView()` | Everything — Leads, Jobs, Black Box (admin pipeline/ops screen via `renderAdminCanvassView`), Team & Hiring, Schedule, Agents, Finance, Personal (Dennis-only, also gated server-side by `PERSONAL_ACCESS_KEY`), Business Model (external link) |
| **Ops partner** (`OPS_PARTNERS`, ops1-ops4) | `loadOpsDashboard()` | Own separate nav, scoped to `assigned_ops` — never touches any admin `render*View()` function |
| **Tech/Sales/Setter/Dialer** (`TECHS`/`SALES_REPS`/`SETTERS`, tech2/tech3/tech4/tech5/rep1/setter1) | `loadSalesDashboard()` via `loadTechDashboard`/`loadRepDashboard`/`loadSetterDashboard` | Own `renderSales()` view only, scoped to `rep_id` — Leads/Jobs/Black Box (their own canvass+dialer, not the admin pipeline screen)/Field (ops-assigned only)/Appts/Map (setter mode)/Training. Now structurally blocked from every admin-only tab by `_adminOnlyGuard()` |
| **Customer** | `loadCustomerDashboard()` | Self-service magic-link portal only, no shared nav at all |

### Pre-sale "Ask Ops for Advice" (2026-07-20)
Lets the admin share a **not-yet-sold** lead's proposal (Option 1 & 2 specs) with a specific Ops Partner for technical/sales input before the deal closes. **No migration** — the request rides inside the existing `customers.proposal` jsonb as `proposal.ops_advice = { ops_id, ops_name, requested_at, status }`.
- **Admin editor** (`_opsAdviceControl(c)` in portal.html, rendered under the Proposal Tools row): appears only when `c.proposal` has options. An `OPS_PARTNERS` `<select>` + **Request Advice** button → `edRequestOpsAdvice(id)` stamps `proposal.ops_advice` (status `pending`); **Withdraw** = `edClearOpsAdvice(id)`. Admin editor only.
- **Ops portal** (`loadOpsDashboard`): a second query (`proposal->ops_advice->>ops_id=eq.<ops.id>` + `sold_type is null`) feeds a "💡 Sales Advice Requested" section at the top of the Jobs tab (renders even with zero assigned jobs). Each card shows the customer name/address + `_opsProposalSpecs(proposal)` — option title, equipment (`line_items`), system specs (`prod_data`), retail price + Moderate savings. **Internal `redline`/`rep_commission`/`gross_commission` are deliberately omitted.** Targeting is per-lead: only the chosen partner sees the request.
- **Two-way reply:** `opsSendAdvice(id)` appends the partner's note to `customers.notes` via `_appendNote(notes,'💡 Ops Advice — <ops name>', text)` (surfaces in the admin lead-editor notes log) and flips `proposal.ops_advice.status` to `answered`.
- **Customer photos on the advice card (2026-07-21):** each advice lead's uploaded job photos are fetched (`job_photos` table `.or(job_id/customer_id)`, storage-bucket fallback) so the partner can eyeball the equipment/site while advising. These are the same customer-visible photos. Rendered by `_opsPhotoGridHtml` which **reuses the customer portal's exact grouped grid** (`renderPhotoGrid` structure: `.photo-summary-bar` + per-category `.photo-group`/`.photo-group-grid`/`.photo-card`), so multiple photos consolidate by category into one spot with the same look/feel; read-only (no delete), tap = `openPhoto` full-size. A scoped `.ops-photos` CSS block retints the shared (dark-themed) classes for the light Ops card.

### Lead-editor notes-logging fix (2026-07-21)
The admin lead editor auto-saves 1.5s after any input (`edPanel` 'input' listener → `saveLeadEditor({auto:true})`). `saveLeadEditor` previously also appended the `#edNoteInput` textarea to `customers.notes` on every save, so typing a note logged a **partial entry on every keystroke pause**. Fixed by removing the note-append from `saveLeadEditor`'s update object — notes are now logged **only** via the "+ Log Note" button (`addEditorNote` → `bbLogActivity`, which writes the DB + in-memory record). The tech editor (`savesSalesEdit`) has no auto-save, so it was unaffected.

- `GHL_API_KEY` — GoHighLevel private integration key
- `GHL_LOCATION_ID` — GHL sub-account location ID: `gXWwbOVymY0iRfj7c1It`
- `GHL_CALENDAR_ID` — GHL top-tier calendar ID (if used)
- `GHL_EVAL_CALENDAR_ID` — GHL "FixMy.Energy Evaluation" calendar ID: `UjlvHxE8AlyhG5frBkqr` — used by `/book` (`ghl-book.js`, `ghl-slots.js`) ✅
- `GHL_EVAL_USER_ID` — (optional) GHL user ID assigned to Evaluation appointments. `ghl-book.js` sends this as `assignedUserId` (the Evaluation calendar 422s "A team member needs to be selected" without it). If unset, `ghl-book.js` falls back to the calendar's first team member; set this explicitly if that calendar has no assigned member. Find it in GHL → Settings → Team, or the calendar's assigned-user list.

- `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` — Stripe publishable key (returned to client by sign-init.js) ✅
- `SUPA_SERVICE_KEY` — Supabase service role key (bypasses RLS, used in sign-init/complete) ✅
- `RESEND_API_KEY` — Resend transactional email key (rep-onboard welcome email + AI agent report digest)
- `AGENT_REPORT_EMAIL` — recipient for the daily AI agent report digest (defaults to `dennislarsen29@gmail.com` if unset)

Stored in portal.html as `GKEY` (client-side key, restrict to domain in Google Cloud Console → Credentials → HTTP referrers).

## GHL Integration
- Location ID: `gXWwbOVymY0iRfj7c1It`
- **Two distinct calendars/appointment types — do not conflate (fixed 2026-06-16, was previously bugged):**
  - **Evaluation** (`GHL_EVAL_CALENDAR_ID` = `UjlvHxE8AlyhG5frBkqr`, GHL calendar "FixMy.Energy Evaluation", 2hr slots) — the initial Tech visit booked by the customer via `/book`. Tech sets up the Diagnostic, signs the agreement, collects the Diagnostic Fee. Used by `ghl-book.js` + `ghl-slots.js` only.
  - **Diagnostic** (`GHL_DIAG_CALENDAR_ID` = `ZGOdyYdMUh07V1Ujav9R`, GHL calendar "Ops - Diagnostic Appointments", 10min slots) — the real technical-analysis visit by Cosmic/internal electrician, scheduled by admin in `confirmDiagSchedule()` AFTER agreement signed + invoice paid. Used by portal.html's diag-scheduling/reschedule calls to `ghl-calendar.js` only.
  - Bug history: `ghl-book.js`/`ghl-slots.js` originally defaulted to the Diagnostic calendar ID (copy-paste/stale default), so `/book` confirmations went out with "Diagnostic" wording/SMS instead of "Evaluation" even though the customer only booked an Evaluation. Fixed by repointing those two functions to `GHL_EVAL_CALENDAR_ID`.
  - Each calendar's Confirmation SMS/Email template is configured independently in GHL (Calendars → select calendar → Notifications tab, or via a Workflow keyed to that calendar's "Appointment Booked" trigger) — verify each says the correct terminology for its own appointment type.
- **Setter-entered leads book onto the SAME Evaluation calendar with automation suppressed, not a second calendar (2026-08-18, per Dennis — Ronda's role):** Ronda sets inbound leads sourced from the mailing service; Dennis wants them on the calendar for ops/tech visibility but with **no automated confirmation SMS/email**, since the setter — not the homeowner — is the one confirming the time. The Setter Lead Capture form (`openNewLead(...,true)` → `createNewLead()`) already showed an amber "No confirmation SMS/email will be sent" note for any `setter-*` lead type, but this was never actually wired to the real booking call — `_fixmyRealBooking` always hit `ghl-book.js` with the hardcoded `toNotify:true`, so setter leads silently got the same auto-SMS/email as a real self-service `/book` customer. Fixed by threading an optional `{notify:false}` through: `ghl-book.js` now accepts `notify` in its payload (`shouldNotify = notify !== false`, defaults true so every other caller — public `/book`, Black Box Dialer, Doors booking, admin re-book — is unaffected), sets `toNotify: shouldNotify` on the appointment create call, and tags the contact `no-automation` (alongside the existing `booking-confirmed` tag) when suppressed — same tag the manual-entry setter path already sends to `GHL_WEBHOOK`, so a Workflow keyed to that calendar's "Appointment Booked" trigger (as opposed to the calendar's own native Notifications-tab template, which `toNotify` gates directly) can also filter it out if Dennis has one configured. `createNewLead()` passes `notify: !_isSetterType` (`type.indexOf('setter-')===0`) for all three setter types (Ronda/Dennis/Other), not just Ronda — matching the UI promise already shown to whoever fills out the form.
  - **Real bug found in the first live test (2026-08-17):** the `no-automation` tag never reached the contact. Confirmed in GHL Contacts — the test contact showed only `booking-confirmed`. Root cause: `contacts/upsert`'s `tags` array only reliably lands on a **brand-new** contact; re-upserting an **existing** contact (the normal case — most test/repeat leads reuse a contact GHL already has) silently keeps the contact's original tags and drops anything new in the upsert body. Fixed by calling GHL's dedicated `POST /contacts/{contactId}/tags` endpoint explicitly whenever `!shouldNotify`, right after the upsert resolves `contactId` — works on both new and existing contacts. Non-blocking (try/catch, warns only) so a tag failure can never stop the booking itself.
  - **Confirmed live 2026-08-17: the Quoya "ACTION STEPS Before Solar Evaluation" email + the "Hi Ronda, Two steps before your tech arrives…" SMS are a GHL Workflow, not the calendar's native Notifications-tab template** — `toNotify:false` alone did not suppress either one, exactly the risk flagged above. **Next step is GHL-side, not code:** find the Workflow that fires on this calendar's "Appointment Booked" trigger and add an If/Else filter — condition *Contact → Tags → Does NOT contain → `no-automation`* — routing tagged contacts around the SMS/email actions. Re-verify the tag actually lands on the next test contact (GHL Contacts → search name → Tags column) before concluding the workflow filter didn't work; a missing tag and a workflow with no filter produce the identical symptom, so check the tag first.
  - **✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-18 — GHL-side fix, both workflows fixed in the GHL UI (no further code needed):**
    1. **"FixMy.Energy Solar Appointment Confirmation"** (the trigger workflow above): an If/Else was inserted between the Webhook step and Send Confirmation Email, using the built-in **"Has tag" scenario recipe** → `Tags Includes no-automation`. Tagged contacts (Ronda/Dennis-entered setter leads) route straight to the first **Internal Notification** step, skipping Send Confirmation Email + Send Confirmation SMS entirely; untagged contacts (real customers) still flow through Email → SMS → Internal Notifications unchanged. Ops/tech still gets notified on every booking either way — only the customer-facing send is suppressed.
    2. **A SECOND, separate workflow — "FixMy.Energy Stagnant \<Recapture Lead Follow-Up Workflow\>" — was independently sending its own "did something go wrong?" nudge to people who HAD successfully booked**, unrelated to the tag/notify work above. Its `Appointment Booking Status Check` If/Else required BOTH `Start date is not empty` AND `Start date Is In the Last 6 hours` to route to the "booked, do nothing" branch — but a real evaluation is scheduled *days* in the future, so "start date in the last 6 hours" was false for nearly every genuine booking, and almost everyone fell into the "None" branch → Wait 6 Hours → Send Follow-Up Email/SMS regardless of having actually booked. Fixed by deleting the second (`In the Last 6 hours`) condition segment, leaving just `Start date is not empty` — sufficient on its own to mean "this contact has a real appointment."
    - **Diagnostic playbook for next time a GHL automation misfires:** don't assume one workflow — search *all* workflows tied to that calendar's trigger. A symptom like "unwanted message after a real booking" can be two unrelated bugs stacked (a tag/filter gap on one workflow, a broken date-window condition on a completely different one) that only look like a single issue from the outside.
  - ⚠️ **The If/Else branch from the 2026-08-18 fix above was GONE by 2026-08-26 — reverted or never saved, cause unknown.** Reported as Peggy Guy (a Ronda setter-captured lead) receiving the confirmation email/SMS despite the "No Automations" toggle. **Diagnosed by elimination, code side first:** re-read `createNewLead()`'s notify-flag computation, `nlSetNoAutomation()`/the toggle's per-type default, and `ghl-book.js`'s `shouldNotify`/tag-write logic — all three matched the documented 2026-08-18 fix exactly, unmodified. Confirmed in GHL Contacts that Peggy's contact **did** carry the `no-automation` tag — proving the portal-side code worked correctly end to end (toggle → `notify:false` → tag written to the contact). That left only the GHL workflow itself as the remaining candidate, and opening "FixMy.Energy Solar Appointment Confirmation" in the builder confirmed it: the canvas went straight from **Webhook → Send Confirmation Email**, no If/Else in between at all — the entire tag-check branch was missing, not just misconfigured. Dennis re-added it in the GHL UI (same shape as the original fix: "Has tag" → `no-automation` → tagged contacts skip straight to Internal Notification, untagged contacts flow through Email → SMS → Internal Notifications unchanged) and confirmed it's published, not sitting in Draft.
  - ⚠️ **The standing lesson, worth repeating because it will recur:** a GHL workflow's internal structure is NOT something this codebase can guarantee stays intact — nothing here enforces it, and no code change caused this regression (all portal + Netlify function code was verified correct before concluding the break was GHL-side). Anyone with GHL access can delete or edit a step, and nothing here would know. **When "no automations" stops working again, check the tag on the contact FIRST** (proves or clears the portal side in one lookup) **before touching any code** — if the tag is present and the customer was still notified, go straight to the workflow canvas, don't re-audit `createNewLead()`/`ghl-book.js` from scratch.
  - **Explicit "No Automations" toggle (2026-08-18, per Dennis):** suppression was previously implicit — derived purely from which Lead Type button was clicked. Dennis takes these calls himself too, not just Ronda, and wanted a visible per-lead override rather than an invisible rule, since either of them occasionally wants the automation to actually fire (homeowner live on the line). `#nlNoAutomation` hidden field + `nlAutomationNoteHtml()`/`nlSetNoAutomation()` render a toggle whenever the form is in setter mode — covering **both** admin's "+ New Lead" flow AND Ronda/Dennis's own portal view, which never showed the old Lead Type buttons at all (that section is gated `!repId`, but the toggle isn't). Defaults ON (suppressed) for every `setter-*` lead type, OFF for Quoya/inbound, fully overridable either direction via a "Turn OFF"/"Turn ON" button on the note itself. `createNewLead()` reads the toggle's live state (`#nlNoAutomation`) rather than re-deriving it from lead type.
  - **Lead Type button "Setter - Other" stays as-is; the Lead Source dropdown's "— Other —" default is now "Customer Called In"** (2026-08-18) — these are two different fields (Lead Type = internal capture-method selector, gates the automations toggle; Lead Source = marketing-attribution category saved to `customers.lead_source`) and were briefly conflated mid-session — the Lead Type button was mistakenly renamed first, then reverted once the actual target (Lead Source dropdown) was confirmed by screenshot. Only the dropdown option's visible label changed; its value stays empty/null.
  - **"Enter date/time manually instead" silently never booked the calendar (fixed 2026-08-24 — Jim Leste report: landed in Portal leads, nothing on the info@fixmy.energy GHL calendar).** The FixMy Book Appointment section offers two ways to set the time: pick a real open slot from `nlLoadFixmySlots()`, or click "Enter date/time manually instead" to type a date/time by hand. Only the slot picker (`nlSelectSlot`) ever wrote to the hidden `#nlSlotStartISO`/`#nlSlotEndISO` fields — the only thing `createNewLead()` checked (`_fixmyRealBooking = fixmySlotStart && fixmySlotEnd`) before calling `ghl-book.js`. The manual fields fed `diagnostic_date` in the Supabase insert directly, so the lead looked fully booked in the portal while the real GHL Evaluation-calendar event was never created — and a failed/skipped `ghl-book` call only ever hit `console.warn`, invisible to the rep either way. Two fixes: (1) manual entry now resolves to the same start/end ISO pair the slot picker produces (built from `nlApptDateManual`/`nlApptTimeManual`/`nlApptEndManual`), so **both paths book for real**; (2) the confirmation panel now shows a red **"⚠ Not on the calendar"** banner with the actual failure reason whenever `ghl-book` doesn't come back with a real `appointmentId` — for any lead type, not just setter-type — instead of a silent success message. Also fixed in the same pass: `arrival_end` for non-Top-Tier leads was saved as the **raw unconverted `"HH:MM"` input string** (e.g. literally `"15:00"`), never combined with the date into a real ISO timestamp like the Top-Tier branch already did correctly — Jim Leste's own row had this exact malformed value, corrected by hand alongside the code fix. ⚠️ **Jim Leste's appointment still needs to be actually booked in GHL** — this fix only prevents the bug going forward; his lead was not auto-booked retroactively since that's a live customer-facing action.
  - **Full audit, 2026-08-25 — Dennis reported 3 more of the same symptom (Joe Bunn, Elias Shemirani, Steve Deambrogio), all older than the Aug 24 fix.** Pulled all 4 leads directly from Supabase. All four were created **before** the fix went live (Aug 18–24), all four are `lead_category:'fixmy'`, and all four carry the exact signature of the pre-fix manual-entry bug: Elias (`arrival_end:"12:00"`) and Joe (`arrival_end:"10:00"`) still had the **raw un-converted "HH:MM" string** sitting in the column (the smoking gun — only the old manual-entry code path could produce that shape, and both are 100% confirmed manual-entry bookings). Jim and Steve show `arrival_end:null`, consistent with manual entry where the end-time field was left blank. **Conclusion: all 4 are the same closed, historical bug — not a new or still-active booking gap.** No lead created after the Aug 24 fix shows this pattern.
  - **A SECOND, currently-live bug found during the audit, and the reason the first hand-fix on Jim silently "un-fixed" itself:** the admin lead editor's **Arrival Window End** field (`#edArrivalEnd`, an `<input type="time">`) rendered `value="`+c.arrival_end+`"` — the RAW ISO timestamp — directly into a field type that only accepts a plain `"HH:MM"`. The browser silently rejects the malformed value and renders the field **blank**, and the very next save (including the 1.5s auto-save that fires on any unrelated edit anywhere in the editor) reads that blank field back and writes `arrival_end = null` over whatever was really there. This is exactly why the direct SQL correction applied to Jim Leste's row on 2026-08-24 was gone again by the time this audit ran on 2026-08-25 — Dennis's own follow-up note on Jim's lead triggered an auto-save in between. **Fixed:** the field now parses `c.arrival_end` through the same `new Date(...)` → `HH:MM` formatting the (already-correct) Arrival Start Time field and the eval-booking confirmation callback both already used, and the save side now recombines the edited `HH:MM` with the appointment date into a real ISO timestamp before writing — the exact same fix pattern as the Aug 24 `createNewLead()` fix, just applied to `saveLeadEditor()` too, where it had been missed. ⚠️ **This bug could have been silently corrupting `arrival_end` on ANY lead's autosave, not just these 4** — it wasn't scoped to the manual-entry path at all, it fires on every edit of every lead that has a real arrival_end.
  - **Per-lead resolution status (read the notes log before re-booking anything — two of the four are already closed):**
    - **Joe Bunn — still needs action.** Step 1 (Eval Booked), no follow-up note since the original 2026-08-19 capture, stored appointment data is unreliable (raw `"10:00"` end time, real value unrecoverable). The eval almost certainly never happened since it was never really on the calendar. **Call to confirm/reschedule — do not trust the stored time.**
    - **Jim Leste — likely resolved by phone, not by the visit.** Step 7 (Not Sold/Dead), Dennis's own 2026-08-25 note says "not interested in proactively upgrading his panels, no bill, already has a battery" — reads as a phone conversation reaching a conclusion independent of whether the original diagnostic visit ever happened. `arrival_end` was NOT re-corrected in this pass (the lead reads as closed either way); re-open if that's wrong.
    - **Elias Shemirani — closed.** Step 7, note says "Canceled AC." No action needed.
    - **Steve Deambrogio — closed for now, may want a follow-up later.** Note: "Stevie Called and canceled. He's going out of town for the next month." Worth a callback in ~4–5 weeks if nobody's already tracking that.
  - Neither Elias's nor Joe's garbage raw `arrival_end` string was rewritten by hand — deliberately. A misheard/mistyped time is worse to silently "correct" by guessing than to leave visibly blank; the editor now renders them blank (honestly) instead of showing wrong data, and either value self-heals to a clean `null` the next time that lead is saved.
  - **A FOURTH instance, same family, found + fixed 2026-08-26 — `createNewLead()`'s own `ghl-book.js` call never passed `customerId`.** Reported live: a test setter lead ("TestRondaSetter") showed no appointment on the real GHL calendar and sent no internal notification, even though the portal itself showed it as booked. Traced in Supabase: the new lead's own row had `diagnostic_date`/`arrival_end` (written client-side at insert) but **`arrival_window` stayed null** — that field is written only by `ghl-book.js`'s own post-booking confirmation PATCH, so that PATCH never reached this row. Found where it actually went: `ghl-book.js`'s fallback "does this email already exist" lookup (`?email=eq.<email>&limit=1`, no `order`) has no defense against multiple Supabase rows sharing one email/phone — and Dennis's own test contact (`dennislarsen29@gmail.com` / `8012328301`) had **6 rows** going back to March from repeated testing. The unordered lookup grabbed an arbitrary old one — a dead May 31st test row (`"TestRondaSetter Larsen"`, a bogus Georgia address, never a real lead) — and silently wrote today's real Sept 3 appointment onto it instead of onto the actual new lead. (That polluted row's phantom `diagnostic_date`/`arrival_window` was cleared back to null by hand once found — it was never a real customer.)
    - **Root cause: `createNewLead()` was the ONE `ghl-book.js` caller in the whole file that didn't pass `customerId`.** Every other caller — Doors booking, Dialer commit, admin re-book — already does, specifically to skip this ambiguous lookup entirely, per the comment already sitting on that code: *"callers that already know the row... pass customerId directly, skipping the email/access_code guess entirely so an ambiguous match can't create a duplicate or patch the wrong customer."* `createNewLead()` has the real id right there (`data.id`, from its own insert two lines earlier) but never threaded it through. Fixed by adding `customerId: data && data.id` to that one call.
    - **Defense in depth:** `ghl-book.js`'s own fallback lookups (email, then access_code) now carry `order=created_at.desc` — the same "never let an unordered `limit` pick an arbitrary row" fix applied repeatedly elsewhere in this codebase (dedupe scans, dialer pagers, enrichment sweeps). This is still the real path for the public `/book` page, where a first-time visitor genuinely has no `customerId` yet — newest-first means a repeat email/phone can never again cost the *new* booking its own confirmation.
    - ⚠️ **This bug is entirely inside this codebase's own booking call — it has nothing to do with the GHL workflow's notification branch** (the "FixMy.Energy Solar Appointment Confirmation" If/Else rebuilt the same day, see the § 632/no-automation entries above). Two unrelated problems surfaced back-to-back on the same test pass; don't assume a fix to one explains the other.
- Agreement flow: portal calls `ghl-diag-agreement.js` → upserts contact → adds `send-diag-agreement` tag → GHL workflow fires
- Status sync: GHL calls `ghl-status-update.js` with triggers: `invoice_paid`, `invoice_created`, `agreement_signed`, `agreement_sent`
- Scheduling: portal calls `ghl-calendar.js` to book real GHL calendar appointment (so reminders auto-recalculate on reschedule)
- Webhook tester: built into Team tab — tap trigger buttons to seed GHL mapping reference payloads

## Diagnostic Fee Flow (IMPORTANT — do not re-ask)
- Fee modal fires when admin/tech moves a lead to step 3
- Both Admin (`confirmDiagFee`) and Tech/Sales (`confirmSalesDiagFee`) can set the fee
- Both call `ghl-diag-agreement.js` after saving
- Webhook payload format (both admin + tech send identical structure):
  ```js
  { firstName, lastName, full_name: firstName+' '+lastName,  // full_name kept for GHL backward compat
    email, phone, address1: rec.address, diagnostic_fee: fee,
    rep_name, source: 'Diagnostic Fee Set',
    tags: ['diagnostic-fee-set','agreement-pending'],
    customField: { diagnostic_fee: fee, invoice_amount: fee, rep_name } }
  ```
  GHL field mapping: `{{trigger.full_name}}` → First Name, `{{trigger.diagnostic_fee}}` → custom field
- After confirming fee: generates a `sign_token` (UUID, 72hr expiry), saves to Supabase, shows "Sign & Pay Link" popup
- Sign & Pay link format: `https://fixmy.energy/sign?t=TOKEN`

## Sign & Pay Flow (sign.html + sign-init.js + sign-complete.js)
This is the combined customer agreement + payment page. **DO NOT rebuild — it is already built.**

### How it works:
1. Admin/Tech confirms diagnostic fee in portal → `sign_token` generated + stored in `customers.sign_token`
2. Portal shows shareable link popup with Copy + "Text It" buttons
3. Customer opens `fixmy.energy/sign?t=TOKEN` on their phone
4. `sign-init.js` validates token, creates Stripe PaymentIntent server-side (amount locked — client cannot tamper)
5. `sign.html` shows: agreement text → typed signature field → Stripe Card Element → "Sign & Pay $X" button
6. Stripe processes card — card data never touches our servers (PCI compliant, handled by Stripe Elements)
7. On success: `sign-complete.js` verifies PaymentIntent, updates Supabase (`invoice_status=paid`, `agreement_status=signed`, clears token), fires GHL `diag-signed-and-paid` tag
8. Auto-conversion: portal detects `invoice_status=paid` + `agreement_status=signed` → converts lead to `sold_type=diagnostic` job → opens scheduling modal

### Supabase columns added for Sign & Pay:
- `sign_token TEXT` — UUID token (nulled out after use)
- `sign_token_expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ` — 72hr from generation
- `stripe_payment_intent_id TEXT` — reused on page reload to avoid duplicate charges

### Netlify env vars required (not yet added to Netlify UI):
- `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, `STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, `SUPA_SERVICE_KEY`

## Payments & Invoicing (IMPORTANT — do not re-ask)
- Stripe is used through GHL Payments integration for existing flows
- GHL's combined sign+pay document does NOT charge the card — it only captures CC info. Do NOT use this.
- The Sign & Pay page (sign.html) IS the correct all-in-one solution — already built
- `invoice_url` is currently set manually; automated GHL invoice creation is a future item

## Auto-Conversion to Diagnostic Job
Triggered in `saveLeadEditor` when: `!cRec.sold_type && invoice_status === 'paid' && agreement_status === 'signed'`
- Sets `sold_type = 'diagnostic'`
- Opens `openDiagSchedulingModal` to assign ops partner + set date/arrival window
- Scheduling fires `ghl-calendar.js` to book GHL appointment

## Diagnostic Scheduling Modal
`openDiagSchedulingModal(id, rec)` + `confirmDiagSchedule(id, rec)`:
- Ops partner picker, date field, arrival start/end time
- Saves `diagnostic_date`, `arrival_window`, `assigned_ops` to Supabase
- Books GHL calendar appointment via `ghl-calendar.js`
- Formats arrival window as "10 AM – 1:30 PM"

## Reschedule modal — "Text Them Options" (2026-07-29)
`quickReschedule(id, dateField, name, currentISO, currentEnd)` (the Schedule tab's day-view reschedule popup) has a collapsible **"📱 Text Them Options"** section below the normal Save/Cancel buttons — for the common case where the rep doesn't yet know which new slot the customer will take (e.g. a no-show/still-at-work reschedule) and picking one date to save isn't the right first move. Rep types each open window as its own line in a plain textarea ("Tomorrow 9-11am" / "Saturday 9-11am"), taps **Generate**, and `qrBuildTextOptions(id, name)` builds a numbered, reply-with-a-number SMS ("1) Tomorrow 9-11am\n2) Saturday 9-11am\n\nJust reply with the number that works…") — no scheduling link, no page for the customer to load, just a text they reply to. **Copy** puts it on the clipboard; **Text It** opens `sms:<phone>?body=<message>` pre-filled with the lead's phone (falls back to a "No phone on file" note if missing). Once the customer replies, the rep still uses the existing date/time picker in the same modal to actually save the chosen slot — this only solves the "which time works for you" round-trip, not the save step.

## Pending Action Banners (Lead Editor)
Red/amber banners at top of editor when:
- `agreement_status === 'sent'` or `'pending'` → red banner with "View Agreement" + "Resend" button
- `invoice_status === 'sent'` or `'pending'` or `'overdue'` → amber banner with "View Invoice" + "Copy Link" button
- `edResendAgreement(id)` re-fires `ghl-diag-agreement.js`
- `edResendInvoice(id)` copies `invoice_url` to clipboard

## Netlify MCP Setup
- Official Netlify MCP: `@netlify/mcp` (npx)
- Configured in `.claude/settings.json` (mcpServers.netlify, no token in file)
- `NETLIFY_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` stored in `.claude/settings.local.json` under `env` key (gitignored)
- User needs to replace `PASTE_YOUR_PAT_HERE` with actual PAT from Netlify → User settings → Applications → Personal access tokens
- Once PAT is set and session restarted, Claude can set Netlify env vars directly without user going to UI

## customers Table — Key Columns
Standard: `id, first_name, last_name, email, phone, address, notes, created_at`
Pipeline: `step, solar_status, lead_category, sold_type`
Scheduling: `diagnostic_date, install_date, arrival_end, arrival_window, install_type`
Financial: `invoice_status, invoice_number, invoice_amount, invoice_url, invoice_items`
Deposit: `deposit_status, deposit_amount, ops_milestone1_status, ops_milestone1_amount`
Assignment: `assigned_ops, ops_payout_status, rep_id, setter_name`
Lead info: `lead_source, referred_by, referral_incentive_paid, lead_temp, dnc`
Title: `title_owner, apn, title_confirmed`
Solar: `system_size, utility, monthly_bill, nem_status`
Agreement: `agreement_status, agreement_url, agreement_signed_at, agreement_signature`
Sign & Pay: `sign_token, sign_token_expires_at, stripe_payment_intent_id`
Dialer: `dial_status, dialed_at, dial_attempts, callback_at` (added by 20260702_black_box_dialer.sql migration)

### Assessor-roll owner names — trusts on the call card (2026-08-08)
Reported after the dedupe pass: the dialer queue was full of `KOKJOHN FAMILY TRUST 05-04-15`, `EDGAR THOMAS R REVOCABLE TR`, `STRICKER MICHAEL&AMIE F`.
- **The merge did not create these.** `_bbMergeFields` only ever **fills blanks**, so it surfaced `title_owner` values that already existed on one copy of a duplicate onto the survivor. More leads now show an owner name, and a large share of San Diego parcels are genuinely held in family/living trusts. To quantify: `select count(*) filter (where title_owner ~* '(TRUST|TR$|LLC|REVOCABLE)') as trusts, count(*) filter (where title_owner is not null) as with_owner from customers where lead_source='orphaned_list';`
- **No parcel API fixes a trust.** Regrid, SANDAG and the county all return the owner *of record*, and for these parcels the owner of record IS the trust. The only source of a human name is **skip-trace** (Tracerfy → `first_name`/`last_name`) — which is why `_bbLeadDisplayName` always prefers a skip-traced human over the roll string, and why the Tracerfy backfill matters more than any parcel source here.
- **`_bbOwnerParse(raw)` → `{display, surname, isEntity}`** is now the one place a roll string becomes sayable: strips the trailing recording date (`05-04-15`) and a dangling `TR`/`TRS`, title-cases while keeping real acronyms upper (`LLC`, `INC`, `JR`), and derives the **surname from the first token** — the assessor convention is surname-first for people (`STRICKER MICHAEL&AMIE`) and family-name-first for trusts (`KOKJOHN FAMILY TRUST`), so one rule covers both.
- `_bbLeadDisplayName(lead)` feeds every card (dialer current call, Up Next, Recent Calls, Black Box quarantined list, admin canvass door, rep Doors door). ⚠️ The Black Box list already had a one-off cleaner the dialer never got — **same "built in one place, not the other" pattern as the AI notes and shift control.** There is now one function; don't add a second.
- **A `TRUST` badge + "👤 Ask for: the Kokjohn household"** render on the call and door cards, and the script's Opener stage shows a red warning **not to read the entity name out loud** when the name came off the roll rather than from skip-trace.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-owner-names.js`): the raw string with its recording date never reaches the card, the cleaned name + TRUST badge + ask-for line do, and a lead with real first/last beats the roll string. Gated both ways.
- **Getting an actual TRUSTEE name (2026-08-08).** Ranked by what is real:
  1. **Skip-trace — already own it, already paid for it.** Tracerfy resolves the *address* to the people living there, and for an owner-occupied trust property that person is the trustee in the overwhelming majority of cases. The exports already carry `first_name`/`last_name` (and `owner_1_first_name`/`owner_1_last_name`). **`tracerfy-backfill.js` may already hold trustee names for these exact leads** — it walks queues whose results were downloaded and discarded by the old `lead_id`-only matcher.
  2. **`_bbTrusteeNote(lead)` — free cross-check, built here.** When `title_owner` is an entity and a skip-traced surname exists, compare it to the trust's family name: match ⇒ green "✓ almost certainly the trustee"; mismatch ⇒ amber "could be a renter, or the property changed hands — confirm before using the name". The mismatch case is the valuable one; it is the difference between opening with the right name and the wrong one. Gated both ways (forcing `match = true` reproduces the wrong-name behaviour).
  3. **The recorded grant deed is the only authoritative source.** The deed that moved the property into the trust names the trustee ("JOHN A KOKJOHN AND MARY B KOKJOHN, TRUSTEES OF…"). **AB 1785 restricts what the ASSESSOR publishes, not the Recorder's index** — recorded documents remain public, searchable by APN. This is a per-parcel lookup, not something to bulk-scrape, and the county portal's URL/ToS were **not verifiable from the dev sandbox** (network blocked) — verify before wiring anything.
  4. **The rep at the door.** Still the fastest single answer; `canvassOwnerManual` already captures it.
- **Trust-owned leads are counted separately in Stage 3** (`window._bbTrustNoHuman`) rather than sitting inside "missing owner" looking fixable — no parcel source will ever resolve them, and mixing them in makes owner coverage look worse than it is.
- ⚠️ **Harness note:** `renderBBDialerView()` writes into `#dashBody`, which stays hidden until a dashboard boots — call `loadSalesDashboard(...)` then `salesSetView('dialer')` first. The dialer also loads its queue over **raw PostgREST**, not supabase-js, so a harness must stub `window.fetch` for `/rest/v1/customers` as well as the supabase client.

### Owner info at the door (2026-08-08) — and a correction to the "Regrid has no CA owners" note
Reported from the field on **8838 Greenview Pl, Spring Valley 91977**: a commercial parcel app showed owner `CASEY OWEN J&TRISTANA N`, APN `4992711700`, "updated 2026-Q3", while the rep's Doors card showed no owner at all. Three separate defects, all fixed:
1. **The rep Doors card never rendered `title_owner`.** It printed only `first_name + last_name`, which Black Box leads almost never have — so even leads we HAD enriched showed nothing at the door. The admin canvass card had always rendered `title_owner` + a Verify Owner button; the rep card (the one actually used on a route) had neither. Now shows owner + APN, with `_cvOwnerControlsHtml` when missing.
2. **A redacted parcel threw away its APN.** `regrid-lookup.js` only returned a result when `owner` was truthy, so a parcel positively identified via SANDAG/SD-City with the name withheld returned `{owner:null, apn:null}` — discarding the one field we *did* get. **APN is the primary join key for the SanGIS bulk parcel file** (`_bbBuildParcelIndex` matches APN first, then street+zip), so every discarded APN is a row the $30 county drive can't land later. Now returns `{owner:null, apn, owner_redacted:true, redacted_via, regrid_key_set}`, and both `adminCanvassEnrichAll` (new `apnSaved` counter, shown live in the progress bar) and the door-side lookup persist it.
3. **No way for a rep to record what they can see.** `canvassOwnerManual` — a rep on the porch reading the name off the mailbox is a better source than any API California lets us query. Trimmed and saved to `title_owner` like any other source.
- **Honest messaging:** a redacted parcel now says *"Found the parcel (APN …) but California law blocks the county from publishing the owner name online — read it off the mailbox and tap Type it in."* Reporting that as "not found" was false and stopped reps doing the thing that works.
- ⚠️ **`regrid-lookup.js` now emits `regrid:no_key` into the debug chain when `REGRID_KEY` is unset.** Without it the chain read as though the free SD services failed, when the paid source had never been called at all. **CONFIRMED 2026-08-08 — this WAS the explanation for the earlier "0 of 5,682 owners came from Regrid" finding** — that was measured via `assessed_value`, which only the Regrid parser populates, and every Regrid step is wrapped in `if (key)`. **Correction to the 2026-08-05 note above:** the evidence for "Regrid cannot supply CA owner names" was consistent with an unset/expired key, and the screenshot from the field shows a licensed parcel product *does* carry San Diego County owner names. The law restricts **publication by the agency**, not licensed redistribution. Check whether `REGRID_KEY` is set and valid before concluding anything else about owner coverage — the Doors lookup surfaces this inline for admins.
- **Revisiting a knocked door (2026-08-08):** the Session Log on the right of the Doors view was **decoration** — plain divs, not controls — so once a door was dispositioned there was no way back to it. That is exactly the workflow in use: knock, cross-reference the owner in **LandGlide**, then come back and type it in. Log entries are now buttons calling `canvassReopen(id)`.
  - ⚠️ It cannot work by re-sorting: the Next Door card renders `unvisited[0]`, and a knocked door has a `knock_status` so it is excluded **by definition**. Clearing that status to force it back would destroy the disposition. `_cvReopenId` is an explicit override instead — the card renders that lead regardless of status, under a blue "↻ Revisiting · <disposition>" banner with **← Back to route**.
  - It also renders **after the route is finished** (`!unvisited.length` no longer short-circuits when a revisit is open) — that is precisely when a rep circles back to fill in names they looked up afterwards.
  - Re-dispositioning clears the revisit and returns to the live route. `canvassSkip` while revisiting skips **the revisited door**, not the next one in the route — skipping a door the rep isn't looking at is silent data loss.
  - If the route re-fetched around a new position since the knock, the lead may no longer be in `canvassLeads`; `canvassReopen` falls back to a `customers` fetch by id and pushes it back in.
- **"Add Note" had never worked — two stacked bugs (2026-08-08):**
  1. **`window.getElementById` is not a function.** Four toggles across the portal used `onclick="(function(w){var el=w.getElementById('…');…})(window)"` — passing `window` and then calling a **document** method on it. Every click threw. Affected the Doors note box, the admin canvass note, the Black Box quarantined-lead note, and the login QR text toggle. All four are now `w.document.getElementById`. ⚠️ **Grep for `w.getElementById` before adding another inline toggle** — this pattern got copy-pasted four times.
  2. **A GPS tick wiped it.** Even with the toggle fixed, `renderSales()` rebuilds `dashBody` wholesale and the canvass `watchPosition` handler calls it every few seconds — so the box slammed shut and the typed note was destroyed within seconds of opening it. Fixed by holding open-state and draft text **outside the DOM** (`_cvNoteOpen` / `_cvNoteDraft`, rendered back into `display:` and the textarea body), plus `_cvUiBusy()` which suppresses the GPS-tick re-render entirely while a note is open or the AI transcript is recording.
  - **The state-restore is the load-bearing fix; the `_cvUiBusy` guard is belt-and-braces** — verified by reverting each independently. Removing the guard alone still passes the harness (content survives via state); removing the state binding fails immediately. The guard's real value is not losing focus/caret mid-typing, which a headless test can't observe — don't delete it on the strength of a green test.
  - Same family as the dialer rule already documented above: *"Stage switching and collapse are DOM-local updates only — a full renderBBDialerView() would wipe the rep's typed note and any live AI transcript."* **Any canvass/dialer UI a rep can type into must survive a full re-render, because the GPS watch forces them.**
- **Owner controls render on every door card, not just blank ones** — correcting a wrong name matters as much as filling an empty one, and the LandGlide flow arrives *after* the door is already dispositioned. `✎ Edit owner` when present, `🔍 Find Owner` + `✎ Type it in` when not, plus **📋 Copy address** (retyping an address on a phone at a door is the friction that stops cross-referencing happening).
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-owner-door.js`): an owner already on file renders at the door, a redacted lookup saves the APN and explains itself without claiming "not found", a manually typed name is trimmed + persisted + rendered, a successful lookup still writes owner + APN, a session-log entry pulls its door back up, the owner can be recorded on an already-knocked door, and a revisit still renders once the route is finished. Gated both ways — dropping `title_owner` from the card reproduces the reported blank, and removing the `_cvReopenId` override makes the revisit silently not render.

## Guided Solar Evaluation (2026-08-08, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Built to make the closing role scalable: *"dummy proof and not a Solar Expert's analysis space… someone who doesn't have solar experience will know innately how to sell."* The eval used to be a free-form photo dump plus expert judgement, which is exactly what caps hiring. Now it is a stepped flow with the expert part done by Quoya at the end.
- **Scope decisions (Dennis):** full-screen **⚡ Start Evaluation** (not an editor section); Quoya returns **question cards** when unsure rather than guessing; output is **scope + talking points + proposal prefill**; **FixMy only** for now (a New Solar variant is deliberately deferred — there's no existing system to diagnose).
- **The flow** — `EVAL_STEPS` in portal.html, opened by `openEvalWizard(id)` from `_proposalToolsRow` (both admin and tech editors, gated `!isNS`):
  1. **Hardware** (required): inverter photo, serial-number photo, meter photo, front of house + an `INVERTER_BRANDS` select and model/serial text fields. MSP and battery-placement are optional. Every slot has a **Not present** toggle so a skip reads as answered rather than missing.
  2. **Production** (optional, never blocks): a monitoring-platform picker that renders **written click-paths** (`MONITORING_APPS`) for mySolarEdge and the Enphase/Enlighten app — which tab, which range, what to screenshot. A rep who has never opened these apps cannot find the production chart on their own.
  3. **Consumption** (required): utility picker + per-utility **bill-retrieval instructions** (`UTILITY_HELP`, SDG&E/SCE/LADWP/other), including the NEM true-up. The bill slot accepts **PDF as well as images**.
  4. **Quoya's Read** — auto-runs on arrival; no button to discover.
- **`netlify/functions/eval-analysis.js`** — the analyst. Vision over the whole evaluation (ranked by `VISION_PRIORITY`, capped at 8 images), `web_search` for this exact model's failure modes / service bulletins / recalls / warranty terms, serial-date decoding, and a `report_evaluation` tool call. Carries the `anthropic-beta: web-search-2025-03-05` header (**omitting it fails every call** — the finance-agent bug). Returns either `confident:true` with diagnosis + likely causes + recommendation + talking points + objections + `proposal_prefill`, or `confident:false` with **questions**. A `confident:false` with no questions is backfilled with a free-text prompt so the rep is never stranded.
- **Prefill is by exact `option_key`, never a name match** — the real `proposal_catalog` is sent in the prompt precisely so this can't be fuzzy. `_propApplyPrefill()` adds the matched items to Option A, sets the PW3 stepper and `restoreArray`/`arrayOutputPct`, and renders `_propPrefillNote` **inside** `_propRenderBuilds` (a sibling banner would be wiped by the re-render every edit triggers). ⚠️ **An unmatched key is reported in that banner, not silently dropped** — swallowing it means the rep quotes a job missing the part Quoya called for.
- **Prices in the catalog are DEALER COST.** The system prompt forbids stating a price to the homeowner or putting one in talking points.
- **`lead_evaluations` table** (migration `20260808_lead_evaluations.sql`, ⚠️ **not yet applied**) — one row per lead (unique on `customer_id`, so reopening resumes rather than forking). Degrades silently like `rep_shifts`: without it the wizard still uploads and still analyzes, it just can't resume tomorrow.
- **Photo-category alignment (real bug fixed here):** the customer portal's `categoryMap` emitted `'Inverter'`, `'Main Panel'` and mapped serial→`'Inverter'` — **none of which are `PHOTO_CATEGORIES` values**. So photos the homeowner had already sent never filled the matching slot and the rep was asked to re-shoot them. Now canonical (`Inverter Photo`, `Serial Number`, `MSP Step Back Photo`), with `labelToType` accepting both spellings so legacy rows still map back. Three new categories were added to **both** `PHOTO_CATEGORIES` lists (portal.html **and** `netlify/functions/lib/quoya.js` — they are duplicated): `Serial Number`, `Meter Photo`, `Production Screenshot`.
- **Wizard uploads stamp their own category.** `uploadPhotos()` honours `window._evForceLabel`, writing `label` + `expected_label` up front — the wizard always knows what it asked for, so unlike a free-form drop the categorizer never has to guess. Quoya's quality pass still runs.
- ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-08-19 — hit in production, fixed as planned.** Reported from the field as "Analysis didn't run — Quoya took too long on this one" (Netlify's 26s synchronous cap cutting off a slow vision+web-search run mid-flight, exactly the risk flagged below when this was built). `eval-analysis.js` is retired; the prompt/tool/photo-ranking logic moved to `netlify/functions/lib/eval-analysis-core.js` (shared, single implementation) and `netlify/functions/eval-analysis-background.js` (background function, 15-min budget) now does the actual run and writes `analysis`/`questions`/`status`/`error`/`analyzed_at` straight into the `lead_evaluations` row instead of returning them in the HTTP response. `evRunAnalysis()` in portal.html fires the background call, then polls that row every 3s for up to 2 minutes (`_evPollAnalysis`) — polling stops client-side on timeout but the job keeps running server-side either way, so reopening the wizard later still picks up the finished result via the existing `_evLoadRow` restore. Added migration `20260819_lead_evaluations_error.sql` (`lead_evaluations.error text`, ✅ applied) so a genuine failure (rate limit, credit balance, upstream error) can be told apart from "still working" while polling, and so `CURRENT_ROLE==='admin'` still sees the real cause — same rep-safe/admin-detailed pattern as `_aiNoteFailText`. `inverter-analysis.js` (the older, manual, admin-only version this superseded for field use) is unaffected — different function, left as-is.
  - **Same day, second failure mode:** one evaluation (Karen Lipscomb) came back `status:'failed'` with a real Anthropic error — `request_too_large`. A separate evaluation from earlier the same day (Arturo Ma) was stuck at `status:'analyzing'` for hours with no error ever written, found via direct SQL. **Root cause: full-resolution phone photos sent to Anthropic uncompressed** — this project has no image-resize library (`sharp` or otherwise), so `toImageBlock()` base64-encoded whatever bytes the fetch returned. 8 photos at several MB each both (a) exceeds Anthropic's request size limit outright and (b) risks OOM-killing the whole Netlify function while buffering them in memory — a process-level crash bypasses every try/catch, which is almost certainly what happened to Arturo's stuck row (the size-limit case at least gets a catchable 4xx from Anthropic; a crash gets nothing).
  - **Fix, `lib/eval-analysis-core.js`:** `MAX_SINGLE_IMAGE_BYTES` (8MB raw) skips any one outlier photo outright; `MAX_TOTAL_B64_BYTES` (24MB, headroom under Anthropic's ~32MB cap) stops adding further images once the running base64 total would cross it — checked against the *next* image's size, not the current total, so one huge image right at the boundary can't still slip through. Photos already come pre-sorted by `VISION_PRIORITY`, so a budget cutoff only ever trims the least-diagnostic tail, never the most useful photos. No accuracy cost either way — Anthropic downscales anything over ~1568px on the long edge server-side regardless, so sending full camera resolution was already wasted bandwidth.
  - No stuck-row cleanup needed — `evRunAnalysis()` always sets `status:'analyzing'` again on retry regardless of a row's prior state, so a plain "Try again" tap self-heals a stuck row under the new code.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-eval-wizard.js`): required slots gate Next while optional ones don't, customer-uploaded photos pre-fill their slot, wizard uploads carry the right label, `confident:false` renders answerable cards and **no diagnosis**, the rep's answers are sent on the re-run, a confident read prefills the builder with the real key while the invented key is reported, and **no vendor/billing text reaches a rep** on failure. Gated both ways — breaking the slot/category alignment blocks the rep at step 1.
- ⚠️ **Harness trap:** `text-transform:uppercase` changes what `innerText` returns in Chromium (it returns *rendered* text), so `/Solar Evaluation/` never matches the header. Same family as the `\bDay\b` innerText trap in the 1:1s harness — match case-insensitively.

### Quoya's Bill Analysis — the Consumption step reads itself now (2026-08-27, per Dennis)
Per Dennis: once a tech uploads the utility bill, it's helpful to know total annual consumption, average rate per kWh, whether the account is on CARE/FERA/Medical Baseline, and the annual + monthly amount paid — as a form Quoya fills in right after the bill is uploaded, not something the rep has to compute by hand off a PDF.
- **`netlify/functions/eval-bill-analysis.js`** (new) — fires automatically the instant a rep uploads the Utility Bill in the Consumption step (`evUpload` → `evRunBillAnalysis()` when `label==='Utility Bill'`). Deliberately a separate, focused call from the full `eval-analysis-background.js` diagnosis: one document, no `web_search`, forced `tool_choice` (same pattern as `finance-extract.js`) so it always comes back machine-readable, and fast enough to fill a form while the rep is still standing there rather than sit behind a multi-minute vision+search pass. `report_bill_analysis` tool returns `annual_kwh`, `avg_rate_per_kwh`, `monthly_amount_paid`, `annual_amount_paid`, `care`/`fera`/`medical_baseline` booleans, `source` (`true_up` vs `monthly_bill` — a True-Up statement gives real annual figures, a single month is an estimate and says so), `confidence`, and `notes` for anything the rep should double-check. `netlify.toml` carries `timeout=26` for it (single-doc Claude call fired live, same reasoning as `seo-insights`/`permitstack-pull`).
- ⚠️ **Real bug found and fixed here, scoped to this new function only:** `eval-analysis-core.js`'s `toImageBlock()` (the FULL hardware/production/consumption diagnosis pipeline) has a comment claiming a PDF bill "is passed as a document block" but the code actually just returns `null` for any non-image content-type — **a PDF utility bill is silently dropped from Quoya's Read entirely**, despite the wizard telling reps "PDF is best" for this exact upload. `eval-bill-analysis.js` does NOT have this gap — it branches on content-type and sends `application/pdf` as a real `{type:'document', source:{type:'base64', media_type:'application/pdf', ...}}` block (the same shape `finance-extract.js` already uses for statement PDFs). **The gap in the main eval pipeline was deliberately left alone** — that's a separate, already-shipped/tested pipeline, and today's ask was specifically the bill-analysis form, not a rewrite of Quoya's Read. Worth fixing there too in a future pass, using this function's content-type branch as the template.
- **Storage:** `lead_evaluations.bill_analysis jsonb` (migration `20260827_lead_evaluations_bill_analysis.sql`, ✅ applied) — one column on the existing per-lead row, alongside the hardware diagnosis fields.
- **UI (`_evBillAnalysisHtml()` in portal.html):** renders under the bill upload slot in the Consumption step as five editable fields — Annual Consumption (kWh), Average Rate ($/kWh), three Discount Program pills (CARE / FERA / Medical Baseline), Monthly Amount Paid, Annual Amount Paid — plus the `source`/`confidence` caption and any `notes`. Every un-edited auto-filled field carries the same purple border + "Filled by Quoya Assist — double-check this" tooltip convention as the New Lead Capture live-fill build, so a rep always knows what to verify. **Never blocks Next** — this is enrichment, not a required-slot gate. A **"↻ Re-read bill"** link lets a rep re-run it after replacing the file.
  - ⚠️ **A re-read must not silently overwrite a field the rep already corrected by hand.** `evBillField()` stamps `bill_analysis.edited[field]=true` on any manual change; `evRunBillAnalysis()` merges a fresh read in field-by-field, skipping anything already in `edited` — same "never let Quoya's own write clobber a human correction" rule the New Lead Capture build established for `_qaLock`.
  - ⚠️ **Picking "the" uploaded bill required an explicit sort, not array order.** `loadJobPhotos()` selects `job_photos` with no `.order()`, so array position is not upload order — the same unordered-lookup family documented repeatedly elsewhere in this file. If a rep replaces the bill, taking the array's last element could analyze the WRONG (older) file. Fixed by sorting the matching rows by `id` (bigint, autoincrement) descending before picking one, with `Uploaded_at` as a tiebreaker.
- **Not wired into the proposal builder's usage math in this pass, deliberately.** `_propSplitByUsage`'s `annualUsageKwh` is currently derived everywhere as `bill × 12 ÷ blendedRate` (a flat-rate estimate, not a real bill read) across four separate call sites in portal.html — real extracted consumption/rate data from this feature would be a natural, more-honest replacement, but that's financially load-bearing, heavily-documented math (see "NEM true-up honesty" above) touched in four places, not a one-line swap. Flagged as a follow-up rather than bundled in unrequested; revisit if Dennis wants Quoya's real bill numbers to feed the savings math directly instead of the estimate.

### Yasmen Bellemsieh — Internal Proposal Tool "isn't reading the Utility Bill" (2026-08-31, per Dennis)
Reported directly off the Internal Proposal Tool showing "No utility bill on file" + "Could not read amount. Enter manually" for a lead whose customer HAD already uploaded a real bill PDF through the customer portal on 2026-08-26. Two independent, real bugs, both PDF-shaped — the recurring family documented throughout this file.
- **Bug 1 — `lib/quoya.js`'s general categorization pipeline (the "🔄 Sync with Quoya" button + nightly sweep) never handled PDFs.** `categorizeOne()` unconditionally built `{type:'image', source:{...}}` regardless of the fetched file's real content-type — so a PDF bill got sent to Anthropic as an `image` block with `media_type:'application/pdf'`, which Anthropic rejects outright. Confirmed on Yasmen's own row: `quoya_status:'skipped'`, `quoya_attempts:3` — every attempt failed the identical way. **Same PDF-content-type gap already documented for `eval-analysis-core.js`'s `toImageBlock()` above (2026-08-27) — that entry deliberately left `eval-analysis-core.js` alone as a separate pipeline, but never checked whether `lib/quoya.js` had the same bug. It did.** Fixed the same way `eval-bill-analysis.js` already does it: branch on `mediaType === 'application/pdf'` and send a real `{type:'document', source:{type:'base64', media_type:'application/pdf', ...}}` block. Yasmen's stuck row was reset to `quoya_status:'pending', quoya_attempts:0` so the fixed code picks it up on the next sync.
- **Bug 2 — the Internal Proposal Tool never looked for a bill that was already on file.** `openProposalBuilder` only ever checked `customers.monthly_bill`; it had no idea `job_photos` might already hold a real Utility Bill upload (customer-portal uploads, or a rep's Eval Wizard capture). So a lead with a real bill sitting in Storage still showed the "no bill on file, please upload" banner and made the rep upload a duplicate copy from scratch — the actual "isn't reading the Utility Bill" symptom, distinct from Bug 1 (which is about the general photo-categorization sweep, not this tool).
  - **Fix:** `openProposalBuilder` now checks `job_photos` (both `job_id` and `customer_id`, merged and deduped like every other job_photos lookup in this file) for a `label`/`expected_label` of `'Utility Bill'` whenever `customers.monthly_bill` is unset, sorts by `id` descending (⚠️ **never array order — the same "unordered lookup" family bug documented repeatedly in this file**; a replaced bill must read as the newest, not whichever the query happened to return first), and auto-reads the newest one via a new `window._propReadBillAmountFromUrl(url)` — a `{type:'document'|'image', source:{type:'url', url}}` call straight to `claude-vision.js`, so Anthropic fetches the Storage URL itself server-side rather than this browser or Netlify function doing it (the same `source:{type:'url',...}` pattern `generate-proposal-insight.js`'s photo blocks already use successfully — sidesteps any Storage-bucket CORS question entirely). A successful read saves `monthly_bill` immediately, same as a manual upload would.
  - **If a bill exists but still can't be auto-read** (a genuinely illegible scan, say), the banner now says so explicitly — *"A utility bill is on file, but Quoya could not read the amount automatically"* — instead of the generic "No utility bill on file" that reads as though nothing was ever uploaded. Only when NO bill photo exists at all does the original "please upload" prompt still show. Same "never let a real negative and an untaken code path render identically" rule as the rest of this file.

### Sold-by-accident had no way back — "↩ Move back to Lead" (2026-08-31, per Dennis)
Same Yasmen Bellemsieh lead, follow-up report: *"I was trying to get the pricing tool to populate and it didn't when I marked it as sold... How do I move back to Leads?"* Root cause of how she got there: `saveLeadEditor`'s `stepImpliesSold = stepVal === 6 || stepVal >= 8` auto-derives `sold_type='diagnostic'` the moment the Progress Step dropdown is moved to step 6 or ≥8 — **regardless of whether an invoice was ever actually paid**. Dennis moving her step up (trying to unlock the accounting/pricing panel, which only renders for sold jobs) silently sold her with `invoice_status` still `'none'`. Confirmed no `payments`/`job_costs`/`commissions` rows existed for her — this was purely a step-dropdown side effect, not a real sale.
- **The deeper gap: `sold_type` only ever gets SET in this codebase, never cleared.** Every code path that touches it (`saveLeadEditor`'s `diagConverting`, the tech editor's `savesSalesEdit`, the booking commits) either sets it or preserves whatever it already is — there was no admin control anywhere to undo an accidental sale. Fixed her directly via SQL (`sold_type=null`, verified zero payments/job_costs/commissions first) — she's back in Leads.
- **New: `revertJobToLead(id)`** — a "↩ Move back to Lead" button on every FixMy admin Jobs card (next to Mark Installed). Confirms, then **refuses server-side** (not just the confirm dialog) if the job has any real `payments.category='revenue'` row, any `job_costs` row, any `commissions` row, or (for battery retrofit) a nonzero `br_revenue`/`br_redline` — same "check before you undo" caution as every other real-money action in this file.
