A job with real money attached can't be silently hidden from Jobs this way; the button tells Dennis to ask Claude instead. New Solar/Top Tier job cards are unaffected — this is FixMy only, matching where the accidental-sold-via-step-dropdown bug can actually happen.
- ⚠️ **Not fixed in this pass, flagged as the real underlying trap:** `stepImpliesSold` firing purely off the Progress Step value — with no requirement that a fee was ever actually collected — is what let this happen in the first place, and will happen again to any admin/tech who moves a lead's step to 6 or 8+ without realizing it silently marks the job Sold. Revisit if this keeps recurring; the safe fix would likely be requiring an explicit confirmation on that specific transition, mirroring the diagnostic-fee popup pattern used elsewhere, rather than a silent side effect of a dropdown change.

### ⚠️ SUPERSEDED same day — "There's no need for another button. This should be automated based in disposition."
The manual `revertJobToLead(id)` button above is **deleted, not hidden** — Dennis explicitly rejected the button approach. The actual fix landed at the root cause flagged as "not fixed" just above: `saveLeadEditor`'s sold_type logic is now **symmetric**, not one-way. Moving the Progress Step dropdown INTO the sold range (6 or ≥8) still auto-converts to `sold_type='diagnostic'` exactly as before; moving it back OUT of that range — with no invoice ever paid — now **automatically clears `sold_type` back to `null` on save**, no button, no confirm dialog, purely a consequence of the disposition (step) the admin chose.
- **Scoped tightly so it can never touch a real sale:** the auto-revert only fires when `cRec.sold_type === 'diagnostic'` (the ONE value this branch ever auto-sets — `battery_retrofit`/`monitoring` are set through separate flows and are never touched here) AND the invoice was never paid AND the step is now below the sold range. Even then, it still checks `payments`/`job_costs`/`commissions` for this customer before clearing — a real dollar recorded against the job (even if `invoice_status` never got flipped to `'paid'`) blocks the revert and leaves `sold_type` alone, same caution the deleted button had, just automatic instead of asking first.
- **Net effect for Dennis:** drag the Progress Step back down below "BR Sold" / "Install Booked" on a lead that was never actually paid, hit save, and it's back in Leads — no separate action, no button to find.
*"Sometimes that number is off based on what the H/O says over the phone or during setting. We want to go off actual data."* All four `bill × 12 ÷ 0.453` estimate sites now prefer Quoya's Bill Analysis when it exists for the lead, over the hardwired guess.
- **`_propUsageFromBill(bill, fallbackRate)`** (portal.html, next to `_propSplitByUsage`) is the one place this decision is made: returns real `annual_kwh` from `_propBillAnalysis` when present (Quoya-read OR rep-corrected via `evBillField` — a human correction counts exactly the same as Quoya's own read, since the whole point of making those fields editable was to let "actual data" win over a first guess), and real `avg_rate_per_kwh` as the rate, falling back to the `bill×12÷0.453` estimate only when there's no evaluation on file. Every downstream dollar figure in each function (production value, escalation math, 20-year total) inherits the real rate automatically, since `blendedRate` itself is now sourced from this helper rather than hardcoded.
- **`_propBillAnalysis`** (new module var, same caching pattern as `_propSolarData`) is fetched **once per builder session** in `openProposalBuilder` — a single `lead_evaluations` read by `customer_id`, awaited before the modal renders — so `propShowScenario`, `generateProposalInsight`, `sendProposal`, and `previewProposal` can all read it synchronously without four separate round trips. ⚠️ **Explicitly reset to `null` at the top of `openProposalBuilder`, before the fetch** — opening the builder for a second customer without this would leak the first customer's bill data into the second one's savings math.
- **The four sites, matched to what already existed:** `generateProposalInsight` and `propShowScenario` (both live-preview functions) and `sendProposal` (the actual persisted save) all now call `_propUsageFromBill` instead of computing the estimate inline. `previewProposal` gets the same treatment, plus a genuine second fix found while touching it: it was calling `_propCalcBaseProduction` with **no 4th argument at all**, so the battery-arbitrage usage cap (`_propCalcBaseProduction`'s 60%-of-daily-usage clamp) that the other three sites already respect silently never applied there — Preview could show a higher battery savings figure than what `sendProposal` actually persists. Passing the real usage value through fixes both things at once.
- **Methodology transparency, `propShowScenario`'s "How we calculated this" breakdown:** two lines previously read the literal hardcoded string `"$0.453"` regardless of what rate the math above them actually used — misleading the instant a real bill rate differs from the estimate. Both now interpolate the real `blendedRate` in play, and a new first line states outright whether the usage figure came from *"the uploaded utility bill by Quoya's Bill Analysis"* or is *"estimated: bill × 12 ÷ rate — no bill analysis on file yet."* Same "never let an estimate read identically to real data" rule as the rest of this file.
- Degrades exactly like every other `lead_evaluations`-dependent feature: no evaluation on file, or the fetch fails for any reason, and every site falls straight back through to the original `bill × 12 ÷ 0.453` estimate — nothing new to break for a lead that was never run through the Eval Wizard.

## Doors & Dialing — the two prospecting motions (2026-08-07, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Black Box is one product with two motions, and they are now named explicitly: **Doors** = prospecting the service door to door; **Dialing** = prospecting over the phone. `_bbModeToggleHtml(mode)` (portal.html) renders the 🚪 Doors / 📞 Dialing pill pair. **Rep portal only** — the admin Black Box tab stays the pipeline/ops screen (`renderAdminCanvassView()` gates the whole GPS Routes block behind `CURRENT_ROLE !== 'admin'`), so the admin dialer keeps a plain "← Back to Black Box" link instead of a mode switch.
- ⚠️ **STANDING RULE — ask before building.** The Dialer accumulated a year of features (AI notes, auto-clock, focus ZIP, outage badges, script panel) that were never brought to Doors, which is what made Black Box feel like two uneven halves. **Any new prospecting feature must be explicitly clarified: "Doors, Dialing, or both?"** Do not build one side and leave the other behind.
- **Doors parity pass (2026-08-07)** — what was actually missing vs. what only looked missing:
  - **AI notes were already there, just invisible.** `_cvAiControls()` was wired into all three door cards but rendered *inside* the collapsed `#tgpNi_`/`#gpNi_`/`#bbNi_` "Add Note" box, so a rep at the door never saw the feature existed. Moved onto the card's visible action row. `_cvAiStop()` already un-hides the note wrapper when the summary lands, so the prefilled text still surfaces.
  - **The auto-clock already covered Doors** (`canvassKnock` → `bbLogActivity` → `_bbAutoShiftOnActivity`); only the *visible* control was dialer-only. `bbShiftControlHtml()` now renders on both. ⚠️ `bbShiftStart`/`bbShiftEnd` used to hard-call `renderBBDialerView()`, which would blank the Doors route out from under a rep — they now call `_bbShiftRerender()`, which prefers `window._bbShiftRerenderFn` (set to `renderSales` by Doors, `renderBBDialerView` by Dialing).
  - **Satellite map** — `window._cvRenderDoorsMap(elId, leads, lat, lng)`, collapsible (`localStorage.cvMapOpen`), `mapTypeId:'hybrid'`, blue dot for the rep and lead pins coloured by `knock_status`; tap a pin → `canvassPrioritize(id)` makes it the next door. Deliberately classic `google.maps.Marker`, **not** `AdvancedMarkerElement` — the latter needs `libraries=marker` + a `mapId` on the one global Maps `<script>` tag that Coverage/Places/geocoding all depend on. Only the nearest 40 leads get pinned (80 markers over a city is confetti). Mounted via `setTimeout` after `renderSales()` writes `dashBody`, and rebuilt only when the container element actually changes so pan/zoom survives the GPS ticks.
  - **SDCP is a sort key, never a filter** (`_cvSdcpFocus`, sessionStorage `cvSdcpFocus`) — same prioritise-don't-hide behaviour as the dialer's Focus ZIP, so a rep can't walk past a good non-SDCP door because it was hidden.
  - **"+ Add a Door"** (`canvassAddDoor`/`canvassSaveDoor`) — covers the two cases a permit-sourced list can't: a house we never sourced, and one whose installer is still in business (Sunrun, Tesla). Address is geocoded **before** insert (a lead with no lat/lng can never enter a GPS route), Google's `", USA"` suffix is stripped so `extractZip` keeps working, and it dedupes on normalized **street+zip** (`_cvStreetKey`, same suffix-folding key as the merge/skip-trace matchers) against both the live route and a targeted query. Two destinations, chosen at save: **Save to Black Box** (`black_box:true, lead_source:'orphaned_list'`) or **Save to My Leads** (`lead_category:'fixmy', step:1, rep_id`, `lead_source:'self_generated'`, opens the editor).

### Doors 8/7 batch — route order, dispositions, doorstep booking, hours gauge
- **Route order is street-at-a-time, both sides together** (`_cvSequenceRoute`). Pure nearest-first *bounces*: two doors 80ft apart across a road beat the one next door, so the route left a street and came back to it. Now doors are grouped by street (`_cvStreetName` strips the house number), streets are visited nearest-first, and within a street the doors run in **house-number order with odds and evens interleaved**, starting from whichever end the rep is closer to. `_cvRouteCmp` sorts on the resulting `_seq`; heading only ranks *streets*, never individual doors.
  - ⚠️ **The cold-load cap must still be by true distance.** `_canvassLoadLeads` sorts by `_distMi` to keep the nearest 80, *then* `_cvSortRoute()` sequences the kept set. Sequencing before the cap would keep whole far-away streets and drop near ones.
  - ⚠️ **Test-fixture trap:** with two cleanly separated streets, nearest-first and street-order produce the *same* answer, so the harness passes either way. `test-doors-v2.js` deliberately places KENWOOD parallel and staggered so the two streets **interleave by straight-line distance** — reverting the sequencer then reproduces the report exactly (`8838 → 8839 → 3950 KENWOOD → 8840`).
- **Door dispositions** (`CV_DOOR_OUTCOMES`): Not Home · Come Back · Warm · Renter · Not Interested · Customer, plus a full-width **✅ BOOKED!** primary. `come_back` deliberately keeps the door **in** the route (`_cvIsOpen` treats it as open, ranked behind untouched doors) — the rep was asked to return. `warm` activates the lead into the pipeline like the old `interested` did but **leaves the rep on the route** instead of throwing them into the editor; they're standing on a sidewalk with more doors to knock.
  - **Come Back carries a TIME (2026-08-08).** "Try me after six" is worthless unless the door actually resurfaces then, so the button opens a picker (2 hours / this evening 6pm / tomorrow 11am / Saturday 10am, plus a `datetime-local`) and **writes nothing until a time is chosen**. Stored in **`customers.callback_at` — the same column the dialer's callbacks use**, so a door come-back and a phone callback are one concept rather than two parallel systems. `_cvCbDue()` (with a 20-minute lead, since the rep has to walk there) flips the door to `_hot`, which floats it to the front of the route with a green "Due now" badge; until then it sits behind untouched doors with its scheduled time shown. ⚠️ `callback_at` had to be added to the canvass `SEL` list — without it the time can be written but never read back. Degrades quietly if `20260702_black_box_dialer.sql` was never run: the knock still records, the timer just doesn't.
  - ⚠️ **No DNC on the door card, deliberately (per Dennis):** a door rep having a bad approach must not be able to permanently kill a lead's phone eligibility for every other rep. A genuine "never contact me" goes through Not Interested + a note; an admin sets the real `dnc` flag from the lead editor.
- **Booking at the door** (`canvassStartBooking` → `_cvBkRender`, 3 steps): pick day + 2-hour window → **read every detail back out loud** (`CV_CONFIRM_ITEMS`: name, phone, email, address, appointment, everyone-on-title — each with a scripted line and a **✎ Fix it** that edits the lead in place) → **capture photos** (`CV_BOOK_PHOTOS`: inverter, serial, MSP, production screenshot, utility bill — the same categories the homeowner could upload, reusing `uploadPhotos` + `window._evForceLabel`). Commits through the same `ghl-book` path as the dialer, and requires `appointmentId` with no `apptError` — `ok:true` alone is not proof. Then activates the lead (`step:1`, `black_box:false`) and stamps `diagnostic_date`.
- **Doors / Dialing pills** restyled to match the FixMy / New Solar / Top Tier category tabs — same size, weight and colour-when-active — with live counts.
- **Shift controls removed entirely.** `bbShiftControlHtml` / `bbShiftStart` / `bbShiftEnd` / `_bbShiftRerender` are **deleted**, not hidden. Hours come only from `_bbAutoShiftOnActivity` + the `shift-autoclose` sweep, so there is nothing to press and nothing to forget.
- **Hours gauge on Team → Dialers** (`_dialHoursGaugeHtml`): this week's hours vs a **30h target per rep**, team bar + per-rep bars + a day-by-day accumulation across Mon–Sun. Ranges are now calendar-aligned **Day / Week / Month / Quarter / Year** (`DIAL_RANGES`, `_dialRangeStart`) rather than rolling windows — a 30h target that resets Monday needs "week" to mean the actual working week. ⚠️ `_weekStart` must treat **Sunday as day 7**, not day 0, or every Sunday reports an empty week. The gauge always shows *this* week regardless of the range pill, since the target resets weekly.

### The route didn't follow the rep — "next door is 5 miles away, opposite direction" (fixed 2026-08-07)
Reported while driving to an appointment. **Root cause:** `_canvassLoadLeads()` fetched the lead set **once**, around wherever the rep tapped "Get My Location", and the `watchPosition` handler only **re-sorted that same array** — it never re-queried. Drive 15 miles and the nearest door in the stale set is still back at the starting point. A `localStorage` `bbLeadsCache` restore could seed an even older set and mark it `ready`. This is *not* another unordered-`LIMIT` bug (the progressive box from 2026-08-04 was working correctly) — it's a set that was correct for a position the rep had left.
- **Fix:** the box query is factored into `_canvassFetchBox()` so the same code can re-run; `_canvassRefetchAround(lat,lng)` re-queries around the rep's current position when they pass `CV_REFETCH_MI` (1.5 mi) from the last fetch point and **merges by id** — never replaces, so knock_status and in-session card state survive and doors just outside the new box don't vanish mid-shift.
- **Heading:** `pos.coords.heading` is used **only** when `pos.coords.speed > CV_MOVING_MPS` (2.2 m/s ≈ 5 mph) — a stationary phone reports whatever the magnetometer feels like, and sorting a route by that noise is worse than not sorting at all. Doors more than `CV_BEHIND_DEG` (100°) off the heading are pushed down, except anything within 0.15 mi (a door 200 ft away is on the way regardless of which side it's on). With no reliable heading the sort is unchanged — pure nearest-first — so walking a street is unaffected.
- **One comparator, `_cvRouteCmp`**, now drives all three re-sorts (initial load, GPS tick, post-knock) so they can't drift: hot → unknocked-before-knocked → ahead-before-behind → SDCP-when-focused → distance.
- The GPS pill states "re-centered 2m ago · heading SW" and there's a manual **📍 Re-center here**, so a genuinely-far next door reads as sparse coverage rather than a bug.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-doors.js`) by replaying a 14-mile drive between two lead clusters. **Gated both ways** — reverting the re-fetch reproduces the exact report: nearest door **14.3 mi away and still from the starting cluster**, vs **0.2 mi in the destination cluster** with the fix. ⚠️ The fixture puts **70** doors at the origin on purpose: with fewer than 60 the progressive box widens to 0.25° (~17 mi) on the cold load, reaches the destination cluster by itself, and the "route followed the rep" assertions pass whether or not the re-fetch works at all. Also asserts SDCP prioritise changes the order but not the count, heading disengages at 0.2 m/s, AI Notes is visible without expanding "Add Note", and Add-a-Door refuses a suffix/case-variant duplicate.
- ⚠️ `navigator.geolocation` is a **read-only accessor** in Chromium — a plain `navigator.geolocation = {...}` in a harness is silently dropped, sends `canvassInit` down the no-GPS path, and voids the whole test. Use `Object.defineProperty`.

## Black Box Dialer (two-way dialer ↔ door knocker note share)
- **Dialer view:** `renderBBDialerView()` in portal.html — opened via "📞 Black Box Dialer" button on the Black Box tab. Power-dialer queue over Black Box leads (`black_box=true` or `lead_source='orphaned_list'`, phone present, not DNC, `sold_type` null).
- **Two-way share architecture:** every dialer disposition, door-knock outcome, and quick note writes to BOTH:
  1. `customers.notes` JSON feed (`_appendNote` format, `by` prefixed `📞 Dialer — <Rep>` / `🚪 Knock — <Rep>`) — instantly visible in canvass view, lead editor notes log, dialer card
  2. `lead_activity` table (structured: channel, outcome, callback_at, call_duration, recording_url) — powers callback queue + future stats
- **Single write path:** `bbLogActivity(id, channel, outcome, note, opts)` — used by dialer dispositions, `adminCanvassKnock`, `adminBBKnock`, `adminCanvassAddNote`, `addEditorNote`
- **Dispositions:** no_answer, left_vm, callback (with datetime), warm, booked, not_interested, wrong_number, dnc. `booked` → activates lead (black_box=false, step 1) + opens editor. `dnc` → sets customers.dnc=true, removed from queue.
- **Queue buckets:** Fresh / Callbacks / Worked, driven by `dial_status` + `callback_at` on customers.
- **Selective ZIP-code dialing (2026-07-20):** a "🎯 Focus ZIP" row under the Fresh/Callbacks/Worked chips (`bbDialFocusZip`, sessionStorage-persisted). When set, leads whose `extractZip(address)` matches sort to the **top** of the fresh/callback buckets (prioritize, not filter — added as a sort key in `bbDialBuckets()`'s `pri()`, below explicit `bbDialPriorityIds` thumbs-up but above dialed_at order), so a rep can cluster calls around a booked appointment's area. UI offers a 5-digit input + one-tap suggestion chips built from upcoming-appointment ZIPs (`adminCustomers` rows with a future `diagnostic_date`) + a live match count + "All ZIPs" clear (`bbDialSetFocusZip`/`bbDialClearFocusZip`). Exact-ZIP match only; radius/adjacent-ZIP would need geocoding (future).
- **Canvass "Next Door" showing a lead miles away (fixed 2026-08-04):** reported as "this doesn't look like the closest lead" — GPS active, 80 doors in the route, and the first one 6.3 miles out. **Third instance of the same root cause** as the Focus-ZIP and Phase-2 bugs below: a `limit` with **no `ORDER BY`**. Both canvass loaders (`_canvassLoadLeads` for reps, `adminCanvassInit` for admin) queried a single **~7-mile bounding box** and took `limit(80)` / `limit=100` — PostgREST returns an arbitrary slice of however many thousand leads sit in that box, and the client-side haversine sort can then only order *the arbitrary rows it received*. The genuinely-nearest doors were never fetched. Postgres can't `ORDER BY` a computed great-circle distance without PostGIS, so the fix is a **progressive box** (`CANVASS_BOX_STEPS = [0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25]` ≈ 1.4/3.5/7/17 mi) that starts tight and widens only when the near ring is genuinely thin (<60 leads), with `limit=1000` so each query returns the **whole box** rather than a sample of it; the distance sort then runs over complete data and the 80/100-row cap is applied **after** sorting. Also fixed in the same pass: the box used the same delta for latitude and longitude, but a degree of longitude shrinks with latitude (~0.84× at San Diego's 33°N), making the box ~19% too wide east-west and biasing it toward farther east/west leads — `_canvassGeoBox()` now divides the longitude delta by `cos(lat)`. ⚠️ **The widening must ACCUMULATE, not replace** (caught the same day, before the first version had been relied on): a wider box is geographically a superset, but it carries its own `limit=1000`, and a 17-mile box over San Diego holds far more than 1000 leads — so in a *low-density* area (fewer than 60 leads in the tight box) the widened query truncates arbitrarily and would discard the near doors the tight box already found, reintroducing the original bug exactly where it hurts most. Both loaders now merge each step's rows into an accumulator deduped by `id`. The settled radius is also surfaced under the Remaining/Knocked/Interested tiles ("Searched within ~7 mi — widened because few leads are nearby") so a genuinely-distant next door reads as sparse coverage rather than a bug. Verified in Chromium/Playwright with two harnesses: `scratchpad/test-canvass-nearest.js` (3,000 leads, nearest sitting last in natural order → nearest door **6.3 mi → 0.072 mi**, full 100-door route inside 0.15 mi) and `scratchpad/test-canvass-sparse.js` (only 5 leads near, 5,000 far — the 7-mile query returns a truncated 1,000 rows, and the test asserts the 5 near leads still survive: nearest **0.09 mi**).
- **Focus ZIP showing "0 in \<zip\>" for zips that really do have leads (fixed 2026-08-03):** reported as "92078 isn't showing any leads — why is the permit stack missing this zip?". It was **not** a permit-pull gap: San Marcos is in Phase 1c's `SD_PS_CITIES` and the leads existed. Root cause was in the **dialer's own queue load** (`bbDialLoadQueue()` in portal.html): it fetched `&limit=2000` with **no `order`**, and PostgREST without an explicit order returns an arbitrary (in practice stable, physical-order) slice — so out of ~13k dialable leads the dialer always loaded the same 2,000, and the other ~85% were permanently invisible to it. Focus ZIP compounded this because it is only a **sort key over the already-loaded array** (`bbDialBuckets()`'s `pri()`, plus the `bbDialQueue.filter(...)` that renders the "N in \<zip\>" count) — never a query — so any zip whose leads fell outside that window read as a hard `0`. Two-part fix: (1) the main query now carries `&order=lead_score.desc.nullslast`, making the 2,000-row window the *best* 2,000 rather than a random 2,000; (2) when `bbDialFocusZip` is set, `bbDialLoadQueue()` runs an **additional targeted query** (`address=ilike.*<zip>*`, same dialable predicate, `limit=1000`) and merges the results into `bbDialQueue` deduped by `id`, so focusing a zip always surfaces everything actually callable in it regardless of the window. `bbDialSetFocusZip()` now also sets `bbDialLoaded=false` so changing the zip actually re-runs the load instead of just re-sorting stale data. Client-side `extractZip()` still decides exact zip membership, so the broader server-side `ilike` match can't introduce false positives (a lead whose *street number* is 92078 still resolves to its real trailing zip).
- **Phase 2 owner enrichment stuck re-grinding the same leads (fixed 2026-08-03):** the nightly run kept reporting a trivial number of new owners ("P2: 6 owners") while ~22k leads sat unenriched. Exact same root cause as the Phase 1c rotation bug below — `bb-auto-pipeline-background.js`'s Phase 2 query fetched `title_owner=is.null ... &limit=2000` with **no `order` and no offset**, so every night it pulled the *same* first 2,000 leads, which by definition are the ones that had already failed lookup on every prior night. Fixed with a persisted rotating offset (`pipeline_state` key `phase2_offset`, same pattern as `phase1c_cursor`): the query is now `&order=id.asc&offset=<cursor>&limit=2000`, the real pool size comes from a `Prefer: count=exact` probe (`Content-Range`), and the offset advances + wraps after each run so the whole backlog gets worked a page at a time. `supaFetch()` was also fixed to **merge** caller-supplied headers instead of spreading them over `supaHeaders` (a bare spread would have dropped apikey/Authorization and 401'd the count probe) and to return `contentRange`.
- **Live SDG&E outage targeting (2026-07-31):** `netlify/functions/outage-sync.js` (scheduled every 20 min via netlify.toml — outages are transient, needs to stay fresh) pulls current outage data from California's public statewide outage feed, **not outage.sdge.com directly** (that's SDG&E's own bot-protected consumer map — scraping it was ruled out for both access and ToS-cleanliness reasons in favor of the state's open ArcGIS FeatureServer covering PG&E/SCE/SDG&E/SMUD). Filters to SDG&E, writes a current-snapshot-only `pipeline_state` row (`key: 'sdge_outages'`, no history table — outages resolve, there's nothing worth keeping). ⚠️ The exact field schema (utility name field, zip field) could not be verified from the dev sandbox (`services.arcgis.com` is blocked by that environment's network policy) — the sync function self-discovers the Incidents layer + utility/zip fields heuristically at runtime instead of hardcoding names, and logs everything it finds (layer list, field guesses, sample attributes) so the first live run's Netlify function logs should be checked to confirm the SDG&E filter is actually matching real data before trusting this further.
  - Portal side: `bbLoadOutageZips()` lazy-loads the snapshot once per session (from any of the three surfaces below) into `bbOutageZips`. Feeds two things reps already use — no new screen: (1) a **"⚡ Active outages"** Focus ZIP suggestion-chip row in the dialer header, same style as the existing upcoming-appointment chips; (2) an **"⚡ OUTAGE"** badge next to the SDCP badge on the dialer's current-call card and both the admin + tech/sales canvass "Next Door" cards — gives reps a live, real talking point ("your power's out right now — that's exactly why a battery matters").
  - ⚠️ **"I don't ever see that there is an outage" — real bug, fixed 2026-09-01.** The sync WAS running correctly every 20 min (confirmed via `pipeline_state.sdge_outages`: a fresh row, 4 real active SDG&E incidents found) — but the live "Power Outage Incidents" ArcGIS layer's actual schema (confirmed from a real run's logged sample attributes) carries `UtilityCompany`/`StartDate`/`Cause`/`ImpactedCustomers`/`County`/etc. and **no zip or city field at all**, only County. `zips` — the only thing the portal's badge/chip logic ever reads — stayed permanently empty on every run regardless of how many real outages existed, indistinguishable from "no outages tonight." Same "never let an untaken code path render identically to a real negative" rule as the Regrid `no_key`/Tracerfy 429/permit-tally entries elsewhere in this file — this makes a sixth-plus instance of it, just discovered a month later once the feed was actually verifiable live.
    - **Fix, no schema dependency needed:** `outage-sync.js` now requests `outSR=4326` on the incident query so each point feature's `geometry.x`/`geometry.y` come back as plain lat/lng, and — only when the schema has no zip field — point-in-polygon matches each SDG&E incident's coordinates against the **already-cached ZCTA zip boundaries** this project fetches for the Black Box Coverage map (`zip-boundaries.js` → `pipeline_state.zip_boundaries_geojson`, itself already covering the SDG&E service area). Dependency-free ray-casting (`pointInRing`/`pointInPolygonGeom`, no turf.js in this project's functions) — trivial at this scale, a handful of incidents against a few hundred cached polygons. Reuses data that already existed for a different feature rather than adding a second geocoding dependency.
    - Degrades honestly if the zip-boundary cache has never been populated (`pipeline_state.zip_boundaries_geojson` missing): logs it plainly ("run 'Refresh Boundaries' on the Coverage map first") rather than silently staying at zero zips with no explanation. Confirmed live at fix time that the cache *is* populated (519KB, fetched same session via the Coverage map).
    - ⚠️ **Not verifiable end-to-end from the dev sandbox** (`services.arcgis.com` and `fixmy.energy` are both blocked by that environment's network policy) — same standing caveat as the original build. The fix will self-correct on the next scheduled run (≤20 min) or a manual hit of `/.netlify/functions/outage-sync` from a real browser; check `pipeline_state.sdge_outages.zips` is non-empty (when real incidents exist) to confirm.
    - ✅ **Confirmed live the same day, directly from `pipeline_state`:** the first post-fix scheduled run resolved **21/21** real SDG&E incidents to real zips (92065×8, 92028×3, 91911×2, 92025×2, 92067×2, 92081×2, 92131×2) — the point-in-polygon match works against real data, not just in theory. ⚠️ **`bbLoadOutageZips()` only fetches once per browser session** (see the Portal side bullet above) — a rep's tab open from before this fix shipped needs a page reload to pick up fresh outage data; it won't refresh itself mid-session.
- **Satellite roof photo had no pin on the actual house (fixed 2026-09-01, per Dennis — "hard to tell which one is owned by Chris here"):** every zoom-19 satellite thumbnail (dialer current-call card, Doors card, the lead editor's + Ops job detail's "Rooftop Solar Intelligence" panel, New Lead Capture's satellite preview) was a bare Google Static Maps image centered on the address with no marker — a strip of a dozen near-identical roofs with nothing indicating which one is the subject property, exactly the reported symptom on Christopher Day's card. All five now add `&markers=color:red|<location>` to the Static Maps URL; the two spots that already had a real geocode on hand (`loadSolarData`, `loadOpsSolarPanel`) pin the precise `lat,lng` rather than re-passing the address string for a marker. The proposal builder's Google-Solar-API panel-layout view (zoom 20, already draws individual panel outlines directly on the roof) was left alone — a pin there would be redundant clutter, the panels themselves already mark the house unambiguously.
- **Coverage map (2026-08-02, replaces the earlier chip-grid same-day):** the Black Box Dashboard's "Coverage" panel (`adminCanvassLoadPhase0Panel()`, expandable) renders a **real Google Map** (`adminCanvassRenderCoverageMap()`) instead of a flat zip-chip grid, per Dennis: "I want to see what we're missing." Google Maps JS API was already loaded globally in portal.html (`<script src=".../maps/api/js?key=...&libraries=places">`) for other features, so this needed zero new script tag / Cloud Console setup — first real `google.maps.Map`/`google.maps.Data` usage in the portal (everything else is Static Maps images). `netlify/functions/zip-boundaries.js` fetches real ZCTA (Zip Code Tabulation Area) polygon boundaries from the Census Bureau's public TIGERweb ArcGIS service for a bounding box covering San Diego/Orange/Riverside/San Bernardino, and caches the GeoJSON into `pipeline_state` (key `zip_boundaries_geojson`, same snapshot pattern as `sdge_outages`) — on-demand only via a "🔄 Refresh Boundaries" button (no netlify.toml schedule; county boundaries don't change). Color logic runs client-side against the already-existing `BB_EXPANSION_QUEUE`/`expansion_index` data: **blue** = San Diego home territory (same `/^919\d{2}$/`/`/^92[012]\d{2}$/` regex documented above), **green** = active expansion zip, **gray** = queued-but-locked expansion zip, **amber outline** = a real zip inside our fetched region that isn't in `BB_EXPANSION_QUEUE` at all — the literal "what are we missing" gap, made visible for free by real geography (a hole next to an active cluster reads as obviously relevant; a remote desert zip reads as obviously not, no distance-threshold logic needed). Clicking a green/gray zip reuses the existing `bbDialSetFocusZip(zip)` unchanged; clicking a blue/amber zip shows an info window only (no action — flagging gaps, not auto-adding them, is the deliberate scope of this pass). Also fixed in the same change: `adminCanvassLoadPhase0Panel()` had a hardcoded `totalExpZips = 84` left over from before the San Bernardino batch extended the real queue to 102 — now derived from `BB_EXPANSION_QUEUE.length` so it can't drift again. ⚠️ **Same verification gap as `outage-sync.js`, and for the same reason** (`tigerweb.geo.census.gov` is blocked from the dev sandbox this was built in): `zip-boundaries.js` self-discovers the ZCTA layer from a short candidate-service list and self-discovers the zip-code field name rather than hardcoding either, logs everything, and degrades to a clear on-map error state instead of crashing — but the first live "Refresh Boundaries" click's Netlify function logs should be checked to confirm it actually found real ZCTA polygons before trusting the map further.
- **"Run Owner Lookup does nothing" — the real cause (fixed 2026-08-03):** the earlier try/catch/finally fix (below) cleared the *stuck-flag* failure mode but not the reported symptom, because the actual problem was that **every bit of the run's feedback rendered into `#bbEnrichBar`, which lives inside pipeline Stage 3** — far below the fold. The Lead Funnel's "Run Owner Lookup →" CTA sits near the top of the page, so clicking it started a real run whose progress bar, errors, and completion message all painted off-screen: indistinguishable from a dead button. Fixed with a `position:fixed` progress overlay (`_bbEnrichOverlay()`, z-index 99999, bottom-center) that `adminCanvassEnrichAll()` paints **before its first `await`**, so the click always produces instant visible feedback regardless of scroll position. The overlay carries live progress, a **Stop** control (`bbStopEnrich()` — the loop already checked `_bbEnrichRunning` between leads), and terminal complete/stopped/failed states with a Dismiss link; `#bbEnrichBar` is still updated in parallel so Stage 3 stays correct. Re-clicking while a run is in progress now scrolls the existing overlay into view instead of silently no-oping. Also added: a **screen wake lock** (`navigator.wakeLock`, `_bbAcquireWakeLock`/`_bbReleaseWakeLock`) so a multi-hour run isn't killed by the device sleeping — it re-acquires on `visibilitychange` because iOS/Safari drop the lock whenever the tab is backgrounded, and degrades silently (run continues) where the API is unsupported or denied. The overlay shows "screen kept awake ✅" only when the lock was actually granted. **Verified in a real Chromium/Playwright run against portal.html itself** (harnesses in `scratchpad/test-enrich.js` + `test-enrich-edge.js`, Supabase + regrid-lookup stubbed): overlay appears immediately and is `position:fixed`, counts/DB-writes are correct, Stop halts partway, and a thrown error both surfaces its real message *and* leaves the button clickable again.
- **AI note-taker errors are no longer leaked to reps (2026-08-03):** when the Anthropic account ran out of credit, `dialer-notes.js` returned `{error: e.message}` and the dialer rendered it verbatim, so a setter mid-call saw *"summarize failed (Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API. Please go to Plans & Billing to upgrade or purchase credits.)"*. Vendor/billing text on a rep's screen is confusing, looks broken, and isn't actionable by them. Fixed in two layers: (1) **server** — `dialer-notes.js` now classifies the upstream failure into a `code` (`credit` | `rate_limit` | `auth` | `upstream`) and returns a rep-safe generic `error: 'AI summary unavailable'` plus the raw text in a separate `detail` field (also `console.error`'d for Netlify logs); (2) **client** — new `_aiNoteFailText(data, err)` in portal.html, used by BOTH `_bbAiStop()` (dialer) and `_cvAiStop()` (canvass door-knock cards), shows reps only *"AI summary unavailable right now — your transcript is saved above, just type the note below."* while `CURRENT_ROLE === 'admin'` additionally gets the real cause and the fix (e.g. "Anthropic credit balance is empty — top up at console.anthropic.com → Plans & Billing"). The transcript is always preserved either way, so the rep can still type the note manually. Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-ainote-error.js`) asserting no billing/vendor markers reach the setter view and that the admin view does name the cause.
- **~8k duplicate leads: cause fixed + merge pass built (2026-08-07):** a duplicate-address audit found **7,981 addresses carrying more than one lead across 16,204 rows** (~8,200 excess). Root cause was `normAddr()` in `bb-auto-pipeline-background.js`, the Phase 1 dedup key: it compared the **entire lowercased address string**, so one formatting difference between two permit sources minted a second lead for the same house — `"…, CA 92028"` / `"…, CA, 92028"` / `"SHADOWCREST LANE"` / `"…, CA 92028-1421"` are four distinct keys for one property. **Fixed:** `normAddr` now returns `normStreet(addr) + '|' + normZip(addr)` with a `STREET_SUFFIX` folding table (LANE→LN, AVENUE→AVE, NORTH→N …), falling back to the old whole-string key only when no street can be parsed. The dedup-set loader also gained `&order=id.asc` — it paged with `offset` and no `ORDER BY`, which can repeat or skip rows and leave holes in the set (same unordered-pager family as the enrichment bugs above).
  - **`bbScanDuplicates()` / `bbMergeDuplicates()` (portal.html, "🔀 Merge Duplicates" next to County File):** groups leads by the same street+zip key (`_bbDupKey`, incl. suffix folding; rows with **no zip are skipped entirely** as too risky to group). **Merges, never blind-deletes.** Keeper priority: (1) has call history, (2) has `original_installer` (permit-sourced, richer), (3) oldest. `_bbMergeFields()` only ever **fills blanks** on the keeper — a value already there is never overwritten — except **DNC, which is sticky** (if any copy is do-not-call the survivor is too, otherwise a merge could put a protected number back in the dialer); `lead_score` takes the max. Any loser with `lead_activity` has that activity **repointed to the keeper before its row is removed**, and if the repoint fails the row is left untouched rather than orphaning history. Scan renders a preview (groups, rows affected, and how many phones/emails/owners/installers the survivors gain) with an explicit Merge button — nothing changes until it's clicked. Reuses the enrich overlay + wake lock + Stop.
  - ⚠️ **`first_name`/`last_name` were MISSING from `_bbMergeFields`'s fill-blanks list until 2026-08-08** — a real data-loss window. Keeper priority is call history → `original_installer` → oldest, so a **skip-traced row (has the human name, usually no call history and no installer) loses to a permit row**, and its name was deleted with it rather than copied across. Phone and email were always carried; names were not. Fixed, and gated in `test-dedupe-merge.js` (removing the two fields reproduces the loss). **Recovery: `tracerfy-backfill.js` re-applies names from the original Tracerfy queue CSVs and only ever fills blanks, so it is the undo for this.** Measure the exposure with: `select count(*) from customers where lead_source='orphaned_list' and last_name is null and (phone is not null or email is not null);`
  - **The merge genuinely combines, it does not just pick a winner (2026-08-07):** three fields would lose real information under "keeper wins". (1) **Installers are concatenated** (`_bbMergeInstallers`, ` · `-joined, case/punctuation-deduped) — two leads exist for one house *precisely because* the same job was permitted under two contractor names (SunPower / Complete Solar Inc / BRS Field Ops), and the dialer's opener script reads that name out loud. (2) **Note feeds are merged into one timeline** (`_bbMergeNotes`) — parses every copy's `customers.notes` through `_parseNotes` (so legacy plain-text rows survive as `by:'Legacy'`), de-dupes on ts+by+text, sorts oldest-first, undated entries first. (3) **Progress takes the furthest value across the group**: `step` max, `dial_attempts` summed, `black_box:false` wins (an activated copy must never be merged back into the quarantine queue), `callback_at`/`knock_status` fill blanks, and **`dial_status` + `dialed_at` follow the MOST RECENT call across all copies, not the keeper's** — a keeper dialed in July as `no_answer` merged with a copy that went `warm` in August must read `warm`, or the merge walks the lead backwards into the wrong dialer bucket. The scan preview names what each group will gain (combined installer string, note count, step change).
  - Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-dedupe-merge.js`): four formatting variants of one house collapse to a single group, a different house number on the same street and the same street in another zip both survive, the row with call history wins keeper over the permit row, a loser's history is repointed before deletion, absorbed fields land on the survivor, DNC is sticky, fields already present are not overwritten, all three installer names survive, a legacy plain-text note and both dialer notes merge into one chronological feed, step/attempts/activation carry over, and dial_status follows the latest call rather than the keeper's.
- **Two skip-trace matchers existed; the Import tab's was non-functional (fixed 2026-08-06):** the Import tab's "📱 Update Contacts from Tracefy CSV" box (`runTracefyUpdate`) carried its own matcher, separate from the Black Box one, and reported **"Matched 0 of 72"** on a real export. Four independent reasons it could never work: (1) it normalized and compared the **whole** address string, but the CSV's `address` column is street-only (`"2635 N TWIN OAKS VALLEY RD"`) while `customers.address` is the full `"…, San Marcos, CA 92069"` — so a match was arithmetically impossible; (2) `lines[i].split(',')` broke on any quoted field containing a comma; (3) it read `phone` / `email` / `dnc` columns that a Tracerfy export does not emit (they are `primary_phone`, `Email-1`, `Mobile-1`); (4) it never wrote `title_owner`, discarding the owner names that are the main reason to skip-trace. It also used an unordered `limit(5000)` over a ~26k pool. **Fixed by deletion, not repair:** the working implementation was extracted as `window.bbApplyTracerfyCsv(raw, statusEl)` and BOTH boxes now call it, so there is one tested path (street+zip matching, quoted-field parser, link resolution, owner+phone+email+dnc writes). ⚠️ Do not reintroduce a second matcher — if a new entry point needs skip-trace apply, call `bbApplyTracerfyCsv`. Verified against the real 86-row export in `scratchpad/test-tracefy-box.js`.
- **Import tab dedupe could never catch a Black Box duplicate (fixed 2026-08-06):** reported after a handful of Tracerfy CSVs were pasted into **Import → Parse CSV** instead of the Black Box skip-trace box. `importSelectedLeads()` built its `existingAddrs` set from **`adminCustomers`**, which is loaded with `.or('black_box.is.null,black_box.eq.false')` and therefore contains almost no Black Box leads — while every record it inserts carries `black_box: true`. The check was structurally incapable of catching the case it existed for, so a re-imported list silently doubled the queue. It also compared **raw address strings**, which never match anyway: Import re-joins as `[address, city, state, zip].join(', ')` → `"…, CA, 92028.0"` (comma before the zip, Tracerfy's float zip) versus the stored `"…, CA 92028"`. Now queries the real Black Box pool via `_bbBuildStreetMap()` and matches with `_bbMatchLead()` on **normalized street+zip**, plus an intra-batch guard on the same key. **The `", CA, "` comma-before-zip shape is the fingerprint of a lead created by this path** — useful for finding them after the fact. Also added: parsing a skip-trace CSV in the Import tab now renders an amber warning that importing there creates *new* leads, with a button through to the Black Box paste box that updates in place. Gated both ways in `scratchpad/test-import-dedupe.js` — reverting the street+zip branch inserts all 4 rows instead of 1.
- **Skip-trace CSV input — three ways in (2026-08-06):** the Tracerfy results box (`bbShowTracerfyPaste`) already had a working file picker (`bbLoadTracerfyFile`, FileReader → textarea) and raw-CSV paste. The missing path was the one Tracerfy actually hands you: a **download link**. `_bbResolveCsvInput()` now detects a bare URL in the textarea and resolves it through **`netlify/functions/fetch-csv.js`** — needed because their CDN sends no CORS headers, so the browser cannot fetch it directly. ⚠️ A URL fetcher is an SSRF hole if left open, so the host is **allowlisted** (`*.tracerfy.com`, `*.digitaloceanspaces.com`), https-only, 25MB cap — not merely validated. Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-csv-input.js`) that file / link / paste all produce identical writes from the real 86-row export, and that cloud-metadata IPs, arbitrary hosts, same-origin function URLs and `file://` are all rejected.
- **Skip-trace matching is street+zip, not street alone (2026-08-06):** `_bbBuildStreetMap` used to key on normalized street only, first-wins, "duplicate streets are rare". That breaks on a multi-county batch — the Aug 6 export spans Anaheim and San Diego, where the same street number and name genuinely exists twice, and first-wins would write one homeowner's phone/email/name onto a different homeowner's lead. It now returns `{byStreetZip, streetOnly}` with colliding street-only keys **dropped rather than guessed**, and `_bbMatchLead(map, street, zip)` resolves street+zip first. Tracerfy exports zips as floats (`92028.0` — 52 of 86 rows), so the zip is normalised to its 5 digits; `tracerfy-results.js` carries `zip` through on the polled path too. Gated both ways in `scratchpad/test-tracerfy-match.js` — disabling the zip branch drops the ambiguous match to zero.
- ### ❌ RETRACTED 2026-08-08 — "Regrid cannot supply CA owner names" was WRONG
  **Verified false in Regrid's own app:** `8838 Greenview Pl, Spring Valley, CA 91977` (APN `4992711700`) returns **Owner Name (Assessor): CASEY OWEN J & TRISTANA N** — a real San Diego County owner name, matching what the rep read in the field. Regrid carries CA owner names.
  **How the wrong conclusion was reached, so it isn't repeated:** the claim rested on `count(*) where title_owner is not null and assessed_value is not null` returning `0 of 5,682` — `assessed_value` is written *only* by the Regrid parser, so zero was read as "Regrid never resolves anyone." But **every Regrid call is wrapped in `if (key)` and `REGRID_KEY` was unset**, so Regrid was never called at all. The metric could not distinguish *"the vendor refused"* from *"we never asked"* — and defaulted to the more pessimistic reading, which then hardened into an AUTHORITATIVE note and a standing instruction not to try. ⚠️ **A zero produced by a code path that never executed is not evidence about the vendor.** `regrid-lookup.js` now emits `regrid:no_key` into the debug chain precisely so this is visible.
  **What remains true:** AB 1785 / Gov. Code § 7928.205 does bar *agencies* from publishing owner names online — which is why the free SANDAG / SD-City services return `ok_no_data`/`ok_no_owner`, and why SanGIS sells the roll on physical media instead. The restriction is on **publication by the agency**, not on licensed redistribution, which is how Regrid can carry it.
  **Practical standing — SETTLED 2026-08-08, do not re-open:** owner coverage is **17,745 of 19,008 (93%)** off the county parcel import + dedupe. The remaining 1,263 break down as **123 San Diego County / 1,140 expansion-or-other** (`select case when address ~ 'CA 9(19|2[012])[0-9][0-9]' then 'San Diego County' else 'Expansion / other' end, count(*) from customers where lead_source='orphaned_list' and title_owner is null group by 1;`). So:
  - **Regrid's API is a PAID product.** A free account gives the web app only — with `REGRID_KEY` set from a free account, every call returns **`regrid_latlon:403`** (well-formed request: `regrid-lookup.js` already sends both the Bearer header *and* the `?token=` query param, so 403 = authenticated but not entitled, not an auth bug).
  - **Not worth subscribing.** It would enrich 1,140 leads in counties nobody is knocking or dialing. Revisit the day an expansion territory actually opens — Regrid is then the ONLY option there, since SANDAG/SD-City are San Diego-County-only.
  - **The SanGIS $30 drive is also no longer the lever it was** — the parcel import already took San Diego to 93%, leaving 123 SD leads.
  - **Owner enrichment is no longer the bottleneck.** The funnel is Owner 17,745 → **Phone 9,091** → **Activated 14**. Chase phones (skip-trace) and activation, not owner names.
  - ⚠️ Diagnose's failure pattern is the tell: `census_geocode:ok` on 7 of 10 while every SD parcel service returns `ok_no_data` means the geocode worked and the parcel simply isn't in San Diego's coverage — i.e. an out-of-county lead, not a broken lookup.

- **Owner enrichment ceiling — the legal background (researched 2026-08-05; the Regrid claim in this bullet is RETRACTED above, the statute is not):** California Gov. Code § 6254.21 (recodified § 7928.205) bars agencies from publishing property-owner information on the internet, and in practice **nearly all 58 CA counties redact owner names from public online portals and GIS layers**. SanGIS states outright that "parcel owner name and address information is no longer published in any publicly accessible online location" — which is why SANDAG returns `ok_no_data`/`ok_no_owner` even for clean San Diego addresses. It propagates to paid vendors: **Regrid's own docs say "California state law effectively prohibits counties from sharing owner names, so Regrid is not able to provide that information in many places."** Confirmed empirically against the live DB — `count(*) where title_owner is not null and assessed_value is not null` (assessed_value is populated ONLY by the Regrid parser; the SANDAG/SD-City paths hardcode it null) returned **0 of 5,682 owners from Regrid**, i.e. Regrid has never contributed a single owner name. Anaheim (Orange County) sits at 0 of 1,331 because SANDAG/SD-City are San Diego-County-only and no *free* CA source carries owner names — a licensed source such as Regrid does. ~~**Do not chase Regrid trials for owner data in CA.** `REGRID_KEY` can simply be cleared~~ — **RETRACTED, see above; this instruction was wrong and clearing the key is what produced the false evidence.** Every Regrid call is guarded by `if (key)`, so an unset key silently skips the paid source entirely. **The real path to 85–95% is bulk licensing**, which is legal because the restriction is on *internet publication*, not on the record: county assessors sell the secured roll (LA County has a formal Assessor Data Sales product; SanGIS directs owner-data requests to `webmaster@sangis.org`). Getting to high coverage means four licenses — San Diego (~90% of lead volume), Orange, Riverside, San Bernardino. 100% is unreachable regardless: trusts/LLCs own many parcels, the roll lags recent sales, and mobile homes aren't parcels.
- **SanGIS bulk parcel file — the real fix for owner data (2026-08-05, AUTHORITATIVE):** SanGIS (Louise Wedley, Data Librarian) confirmed in writing that **California AB 1785** is why they cannot publish owner name/address "in any publicly accessible online location" — which is exactly why the free spatial services return `ok_no_data` for the owner field and why Regrid has never produced a single CA owner name. **The one legal route is a direct handoff: a physical data drive with the full county parcel layer in Esri FGDB format, $25 + $5 to mail, refreshed weekly.** San Diego County is ~90% of lead volume, so this ends the owner problem for the whole book at a cost of $30. ⚠️ **Licence condition: "ownership information must not be publicly displayed."** Verified at build time that `title_owner` appears in **zero** public files (index/sign/check-preview/book/thank-you/careers/meet/onboarding/loan-*/business-model — the only `business-model.html` hit is the literal field name in docs prose) and never in the customer portal; it renders only in staff-authenticated admin/tech views. **Never surface owner data on a public page, and re-check this whenever a new public surface is added.** Open item: confirm no RLS policy grants anon `SELECT` on `customers` — the anon key ships in portal.html, so an anon-readable owner column would arguably count as publicly accessible (`select polname, polcmd, pg_get_expr(polqual, polrelid) from pg_policy where polrelid='customers'::regclass;`).
  - **Facts extracted from the `PARCELS_ALL` metadata** (the PDF has no ToUnicode map — decoded via pdfminer + a substitution pass; raw text in `scratchpad/meta.txt`/`dec.txt`): dataset holds "site address information along with owner names and addresses"; **>1,000,000 parcels**; documented fields include `NUCLEUS_USE_CD`, `ASR_LANDUSE`, `NUCLEUS_ZONE_CD`, `ASR_ZONE` (land-use/zoning are **not regularly maintained** — approximate only, updated solely on new construction or ownership change); layer names containing `-NORTH-`/`-SOUTH-`/`-EAST-` are **subsets** (use `PARCELS_ALL`); **possessory-interest APNs start with `76`** and **mobile-home APNs start with `77`** — neither is a real parcel and both share the parent parcel's polygon, so matching on them attaches the wrong owner.
  - **`bbShowParcelImport()` / `bbRunParcelImport()` (portal.html, "📦 County File" button next to Free: Owners + Diagnose):** the county export is far too big to paste or upload, so it is read **locally** — `File.slice` + `FileReader` in 4MB chunks with a remainder carry across slice boundaries; the county file is never transmitted anywhere. `_bbDetectParcelCols()` auto-detects owner/APN/situs/zip columns (seeded with the real SanGIS names — `OWN_NAME1`/`APN`/`SITUS_*` are already proven against their GeocoderMerged service in `regrid-lookup.js`) so a schema revision or a differently-configured QGIS export doesn't silently import nothing, and **throws a visible error rather than importing zero** when no owner column exists. `_bbBuildParcelIndex()` indexes owner-less leads by **APN first, then street+zip**; street-only keys that collide across zips are **dropped, not guessed** ("500 Main St" exists in a dozen SD cities). 76x/77x rows are skipped. Reuses the enrich overlay + wake lock + Stop.
  - Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-parcel-import.js`): column detection across both export shapes, APN match, street+zip match, `OWN_NAME2` join, quoted fields containing commas, 76x/77x exclusion (asserting a mobile-home row can't overwrite the real owner), ambiguous street-only match refused, missing-owner-column error, and **chunk-boundary integrity on a 12MB/3-slice file — 150,002 rows in, 150,002 parsed exactly**. Gated both ways: breaking the remainder carry yields 150,004 (one phantom row per boundary).
  - Not built yet: importing the owner **mailing address** (absentee-owner detection — mail addr ≠ situs is a different pitch) would need one new column; deliberately deferred to avoid adding to the unapplied-migration backlog.
- **Address repair — the biggest free lift (2026-08-05):** leads whose stored address has **no trailing zip** resolve owners at **~2%** versus **~20%** for complete addresses (7,318 of 26,311 leads, ~28%). They are not missing coordinates — all 26,311 are geocoded — but those coordinates were derived from an incomplete address, so they're imprecise and land **outside the parcel polygon**. Since every parcel lookup is point-in-polygon, an imprecise point is an automatic miss regardless of which service is queried. `adminBatchGeocode` couldn't help: it only targets `lat is null`. New **`adminRepairAddresses()`** (🔧 Repair Incomplete Addresses, pipeline Stage 2) pages through owner-less leads, filters client-side for a missing trailing zip (`_addrHasZip`), re-geocodes via Google, and writes back **both** the canonical address and the sharper coordinates — but **only for `ROOFTOP`/`RANGE_INTERPOLATED` results**; `APPROXIMATE` means Google fell back to a city/zip centroid, which would be *worse* than the stored point, so those are skipped rather than written. Reuses the enrich overlay + wake lock + Stop control. ⚠️ Gotcha found in testing: Google's `formatted_address` ends with `", USA"`, so a trailing-zip regex never matched and the completed address was silently never written back — `_geocodeFull()` now strips the country suffix, which also keeps stored addresses in the existing `STREET, CITY, CA ZIP` shape that `extractZip()` expects. Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-repair.js`): only zip-less leads are geocoded, APPROXIMATE results are skipped, ROOFTOP writes both address + coords, and a leading 5-digit *street number* is never mistaken for a zip.
- **Owner Lookup diagnostics — finding out WHY the hit rate is low (2026-08-03):** owner enrichment sits at ~16% overall (4,095 / 26,298), and per-city analysis showed it is **0% in Orange County** (Anaheim: 0 of 1,331) and only 9–31% across San Diego cities — so Black Box leads reach the dialer without an owner name. `regrid-lookup.js` already returns a precise `debug` chain per failed lookup (`regrid_latlon:403 | census_geocode:no_match | sandag:ok_no_data | …`), but the client threw all of it away except the *first* miss, so the actual failing step was invisible. Added: (1) **`bbDiagnoseOwnerLookup(n)`** — a "🩺 Diagnose" button next to Run Owner Lookup that runs the real lookup against 10 sample leads (~20s instead of a multi-hour full run) and reports the resolve rate, which source succeeded (`sandag`/`regrid_search`/`sd_city`), an aggregated **step:reason × count** tally of why the rest failed, and a per-lead detail expander; (2) the same aggregated tally on the **full run's** completion state (`_bbFailKeys()`/`_bbTallyHtml()`), plus a "Worked via:" source breakdown. HTTP-status/error keys render red so an auth/credit failure (`regrid_*:403`, `:429`) is instantly distinguishable from a genuine no-match (`sandag:ok_no_data`). **This is a diagnostic, not a hit-rate fix** — the sandbox this was built in blocks all outbound network (`app.regrid.com`, `geo.sandag.org`, `geocoding.geo.census.gov` are all unreachable), so which upstream is failing could not be determined from here; running Diagnose once in the live portal pinpoints it. Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-diagnose.js`) against two simulated failure shapes — dead Regrid key (403s, SANDAG carrying the successes) vs. Census rate-limiting (429s) — confirming the two render as clearly different diagnoses.
- **Diagnose control group + random sampling (2026-08-05):** the first live Diagnose run came back **0 of 10 resolved**, every failure `ok_no_data` (zero features returned) from all three SD parcel services on both stored and Census coordinates — a shape that does NOT look like redaction (`ok_no_owner`, features found but the name blanked). Two problems made that result uninterpretable. (1) **Fourth instance of the unordered-`LIMIT` bug** — the sampler used `limit(10)` with no `ORDER BY`, so it re-tested the *same* arbitrary rows every run, which for unenriched leads are by definition the ones that have failed every prior run. Now probes pool size with `count:'exact'` and reads a random page via `.order('id').range(off, off+N-1)`. (2) **No control** — a 0% result can't distinguish "the upstream service changed/broke" from "these particular leads are genuinely hard". `bbDiagnoseOwnerLookup` now also re-runs **5 leads that already have a `title_owner`** (i.e. the services resolved them before) through the identical lookup and renders the verdict as a banner above the tally: green *"Parcel services still work — the misses above are genuinely hard leads"* vs. red *"⚠ Leads that resolved before now return nothing. The upstream service has changed or is blocking us — not a data problem."* **Read the control line first** — it decides whether the next move is chasing coordinates/address quality or chasing a broken SANDAG/SD-City endpoint. Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-diagnose.js`) against both verdict shapes plus an assertion that the sample offset actually varies across runs.
- **Enrichment pager was skipping most of the backlog (fixed 2026-08-05) — the actual reason the hit rate looked terrible:** with the random sampler in place, Diagnose came back **8 of 10 resolved (80%), all via `sd_city`, control 5 of 5** — i.e. the parcel services work fine and the earlier `0 of 10` was purely the sampler re-testing leads that had already failed every prior run. That made the full run's "123 owners added" impossible to explain by data quality, and the real bug turned out to be in the pager. **Both** `adminCanvassEnrichAll()` (portal, `PAGE_SIZE` 500) and `bb-auto-pipeline-background.js` Phase 2 (`P2_PAGE` 2000) page through a query filtered on `title_owner is null` and then advanced the cursor by a **fixed page size** — but every lead that resolves leaves that filter *immediately*, so the result set shrinks underneath the cursor and a fixed step lands N rows into an already-shorter list, skipping every lead the removals shifted forward. At an 80% hit rate that discards roughly 4 of every 5 leads per page. Both now advance by the number of leads that **stayed** unenriched (`pageStayed` / `p2Stayed`), which is the only value that keeps the cursor aligned to a mutating set. Measured against the real portal with a shrinking-set stub (`scratchpad/test-enrich-paging.js`, 1,200 leads at an 80% hit rate): the old pager visited **800 of 1,200** and wrote **640 of the 960** available owners; fixed, it visits all 1,200, writes all 960, and looks up no lead twice. The harness is gated both ways — reverting the line to `+= PAGE_SIZE` reproduces the 400-lead skip exactly. **Same bug family as the four unordered-`LIMIT` instances above: a cursor that assumes a stable result set while mutating it.** ⚠️ When adding any new paged sweep over a filter the sweep itself changes, advance by the rows that remain, never by the page size.
- **Coverage map fixes (2026-08-02):** two bugs found immediately after the map above shipped. (1) **"Run Owner Lookup" going permanently unresponsive**: `adminCanvassEnrichAll()` set a module-level `_bbEnrichRunning` guard flag but had no try/catch around the function body — any thrown error (a transient Supabase hiccup on the very first count query, etc.) stranded the flag at `true` forever, silently no-oping every future click with no error shown. Fixed by wrapping the whole body in `try/catch/finally` (`finally` always resets the flag; `catch` renders a real "Owner lookup failed: ..." message into `#bbEnrichBar` instead of leaving it frozen). ⚠️ **Known coverage limitation, not fixed (no free alternative found):** the free owner lookup's non-Regrid fallback sources in `netlify/functions/regrid-lookup.js` — SANDAG (`geo.sandag.org`) and City of San Diego GeocoderMerged (`webmaps.sandiego.gov`) — are San Diego County-only spatial services. For Orange/Riverside/San Bernardino expansion-zip leads, owner lookup can **only** succeed through the paid Regrid API (steps 1-3 in that file); if `REGRID_KEY` is unset or out of credits, every expansion-county lead legitimately returns "no owner" with zero fallback. (2) **The map showed zips like 92008 (Carlsbad) as solidly "active/covered" despite zero real leads in the Dialer** — the color logic colored purely by theoretical queue/regex membership, disconnected from actual data. Fixed by adding a real-data layer: `_bbGetZipLeadCounts()` tallies actual `customers` rows per zip (reuses the Lead Insights cache when already populated, else its own lightweight address-only fetch), and `map.data.setStyle()` now scales fill opacity by that real count — a zip with 0 leads renders hollow/near-empty regardless of its home/expansion category, one with real leads renders solid. The 4-category legend (blue/green/gray/amber) is unchanged; this adds a "how populated is it really" dimension on top, which is what makes 92008 now read correctly as hollow.
- **Manual/override GHL booking (2026-07-20):** the dialer + editor "Confirm Booking" go through `ghl-book.js`, which now sends `assignedUserId` (from `GHL_EVAL_USER_ID` env or the Evaluation calendar's first team member) — the Evaluation calendar 422s without it — plus `ignoreDateRange`/`ignoreFreeSlotValidation`/`toNotify` so arbitrary times land and confirmation automations fire. `bbDialCommitBooking`/`edCommitEvalBooking` now require a real `appointmentId` (not just `ok:true`, which `ghl-book` returns even on calendar failure) so a rejected booking surfaces the real GHL reason instead of a silent false "Booked".
- **Migration:** `supabase/migrations/20260702_black_box_dialer.sql` — creates lead_activity + dialer columns. ✅ Applied (verified 2026-08-08). The portal degrades gracefully without it (notes feed works, but worked/callback buckets don't persist across reloads).
- **`ghl-bulk-sync.js`** — "Sync Queue to GHL" button (admin): pushes all dialable BB leads into GHL as contacts, tag `bb-dialer-lead` ONLY (deliberately never uses workflow-trigger tags like `send-diag-agreement`). Enables calling via GHL's built-in Power Dialer with no CSV import/export.
- **`ghl-dialer-sync.js`** — inbound webhook for GHL call dispositions: matches customer by last-10-digit phone, writes lead_activity + notes feed + dial state. Wire in GHL: workflow on call disposition → Webhook POST to `https://fixmy.energy/.netlify/functions/ghl-dialer-sync` with `{ phone, outcome, note, rep_name }`.
- **Spam protection decision (2026-07-02):** main LC Phone number = transactional only. Buy 2–3 dedicated local dialing numbers (~$1.15/mo each), cap ~100–125 calls/day each, register at freecallerregistry.com, treat as semi-disposable.

### Dialer Script + Call Flow panel (2026-07-23, rewritten same day per Dennis's exact contact-sequence + script)
Embedded in the current-call card of the Black Box Dialer (`bbScriptPanelHtml(lead)` in portal.html, rendered right under the Call/Text buttons — both admin and tech/sales see it since they share `renderBBDialerView`). Collapsible "📜 Call Script" panel (open state in localStorage `bbScriptOpen`), stage chips: **🗺 Structure** then **1·Opener / 2·Qualify / 3·Bill Branch / 4·Set Expectations / 5·Book It / 🛡 Objections / 📧 Voicemail / 🔁 Callback**.
- **Structure tab = the 10-step contact sequence** (`bbScriptBodyHtml('flow', lead)`), verbatim per Dennis: (1) text first — not a sales text, purpose is to get the iPhone to auto-label the number "Maybe [Rep]"; (2) Share Contact Card in that same thread (iOS); (3–4) call immediately, then a second time; (5) a third call — straight-to-voicemail every time signals Do Not Disturb, not avoidance; (6) still no answer → send a short non-salesy video of yourself; branches to (7) they answer → tap AI Notes immediately; (8) Book! — confirm BOTH date and arrival window before hanging up; (9) the magic-link text+email fires automatically (portal upload of inverter/utility-bill photos — helpful, not required); (10) book fast, next-day when possible, never more than 48 hours out.
- **Script stages** carry the actual words: Opener blends the intro + reason-for-call in one line naming the homeowner's street (`_bbStreet()` — first comma-segment of `address`), the permit-derived `install_year`, and the defunct installer (the dialer's own `customers` select — `BB_DIAL_SEL_FULL`/`BASE` — didn't include `install_year` even though the column exists and is used elsewhere in the app; added it so the opener can say "installed in 2019 by SunPower"); Qualify asks the three SDG&E-bill questions; Bill Branch splits on the answer — **under $50/mo** pivots to battery-backup / the coming-soon cleaning+monitoring service, **over $50/mo** drives the pain point (the emotional hook that carries them from booking to actually showing up at the eval) while two hard warnings render as red `_bbWarn()` callouts: never talk down the defunct installer (makes the tech's job harder on-site), and never try to sell over the phone — keep it informational and nonchalant; Set Expectations covers the free-eval/no-show-fee, the optional photo upload, what the tech does on-site, and that **the homeowner must be present**; Book It is the closing line with the 48-hour/2-hour-window rule. Objections/Voicemail/Callback carry over, retoned to match.
- Auto-personalizes per lead: homeowner first name, street, defunct `original_installer`, SDCP-territory tip, and the rep's own name via `bbRepName()`. Stage switching (`bbScriptSetStage`) and collapse are **DOM-local updates only** — a full `renderBBDialerView()` would wipe the rep's typed note and any live AI transcript. DNC stays scripted + mandatory on "take me off your list."
- **On/off toggle (2026-07-23):** a small switch next to Focus ZIP (`_bbScriptToggleHtml()`) — on by default (new reps need it), lets a veteran rep who knows the pitch cold turn the whole panel off to reclaim screen space. Persists per-device in localStorage `bbScriptEnabled`. `bbScriptSetEnabled()` is DOM-local (swaps only `#bbScriptWrap`'s contents), same rationale as stage switching.

### Script one-sheet + script corrections (2026-08-08, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
A setter asked to read the script **top to bottom** for training rather than clicking stage chips. `bbOpenOneSheet(which, leadId)` renders every stage of either script continuously in a full-screen overlay, **from the same `bbScriptBodyHtml` / `cvScriptBodyHtml` used by the chips** — so the training sheet and the live panel can never drift. A 📄 One sheet button sits next to the script on/off toggle on both surfaces. Print/Save-PDF is `window.print()` plus a `body.bb-onesheet-open` print block that hides everything else and re-colours the dark theme for paper — the same "browser print → Save as PDF" pattern as `finPrint()`, no PDF library. Opened without a lead it shows a "Training view" banner, since the name/street/installer placeholders read as instructions rather than one customer's data.

**Four script decisions, all from Dennis:**
1. ⚠️ **NO manufacturer affiliation.** A rep's draft said *"we work 'in conjunction' with SolarEdge & Enphase."* **There is no agreement with either company** — the script now carries an explicit red warning never to say it, and replaces it with the true version that does the same job: the hardware warranty is usually still good, what the homeowner lost is anyone to file the claim or do the labour. Never reinstate an affiliation claim without a real, documented agreement.
2. **There is NO no-show fee.** The dialer script had been promising *"we do have to charge for the next one"* while the door script promised free rescheduling — reps contradicting each other on money. Both now say free, no obligation, free to move.
3. **Opener still names the installer + install year.** The orphaned-account hook is the differentiator; a street-only opener was considered and rejected.
4. **Assumptive close, confirm date AND 2-hour window out loud, book inside 48 hours** — a soft "can we add you to our list?" was considered and rejected as the thing that produces no-shows.

**Merged in from the rep's draft** (genuinely better than what was there): *"Have you had a technician come out at all since the install?"* now leads Qualify — least threatening question available, answer is almost always no, and it establishes the service gap before money comes up. True-up gained a tip explaining that a $30/mo homeowner can still owe four figures in March. The under-$50 branch gained the storage/NEM-credit angle, with a hard warning never to name a dollar amount or a specific incentive programme on the phone.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-onesheet.js`: both sheets render all 9 stages from the shared body function, print control present, closes cleanly, and all four corrections are in the rendered text. ⚠️ Harness trap: asserting the *absence* of "in conjunction with" fails on our own warning text — the check must confirm it appears only near "Do NOT say".

### Call/door transcription consent — § 632 gate (2026-08-10, AUTHORITATIVE)
Surfaced while writing the new `/privacy` page: both AI note-takers began transcribing **the instant the button was tapped**, with no disclosure anywhere. **California Penal Code § 632 is an ALL-PARTY consent statute** — being a participant is not enough — and § 637.2 attaches statutory damages per violation. ⚠️ **A line in the script is not a fix**: a script is advice a rep can skip, so the transcription itself is gated.
- **`_aiConsentGate(mode, onProceed)`** (portal.html, block 1) — one shared modal in front of **both** `bbAiToggle` (call) and `cvAiToggle` (door); the start logic moved into `_bbAiBegin` / `_cvAiBegin`, which are only reachable through it. Shows the exact words to read out loud (`AI_CONSENT_LINES`, per-mode wording — a phone call and a doorstep are not the same sentence), then **They said yes** / **They said no**. Escape = decline. There is no path around it.
- ⚠️ **Consent is per-conversation and deliberately NOT cached.** Remembering it across calls would throw away the entire point of the gate. Gated in the harness (step 4).
- **`_aiConsentTag()` stamps `[Transcription consent given <ISO>]` onto the saved note** at both `bbLogActivity` sites. A claim that you asked is worth far more with a timestamp behind it.
- **Script side:** the ask lives in the **call script's `opener`** and the **door script's `opener`** — deliberately *not* the door's `approach` stage, since that is body language before the door opens and you cannot ask consent of someone who has not answered. Both scripts also state what to do on a refusal (type the note; never transcribe anyway).
- **`/privacy` asserts this behaviour**, so the code and the published policy must not drift. Same rule as the FAQ ↔ FAQPage schema: if you change one, change the other.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-consent.js` — tapping does not record, declining does not record, consenting does, the next conversation asks again, the door variant uses in-person wording, both scripts carry the line, and both note-saves stamp the record. Gated both ways: bypassing `_aiConsentGate` reproduces the exposure exactly (recognition starts, no dialog).

### Dialer email field (2026-07-23)
Black Box leads often arrive from skip-trace/GIS enrichment without an email. The current-call card now shows an inline-editable email row (`bbEmailRowHtml`/`bbEditEmail`/`bbSaveEmail`, right under the address) — tap to confirm/correct/add it, saves straight to `customers.email`. Matters for Structure step 9 (the magic-link text+email needs a real address on file).

### Dialer AI Transcript / Note-Taker (2026-07-23, auto-save fixed 2026-07-29)
"🎙 AI Notes" button in the dialer's Call row (and the mirrored `_cvAiControls` version on Black Box canvass door-knock cards). Rep taps it, puts the call on speakerphone, and the browser's `SpeechRecognition` (webkit prefix; iOS Safari 14.5+/Chrome) transcribes both sides live into a purple transcript panel (`#bbAiPanel`/`#cvAiPanel_<id>`), auto-restarting on the silence-timeout `onend`. Tapping **⏹ Stop & Summarize** posts the transcript + lead context (name, installer, address) to **`netlify/functions/dialer-notes.js`** — hardened like claude-vision.js (origin allowlist, payload reconstructed, transcript capped 12k chars), model `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, ~300 tokens — which returns `{note, suggested_outcome}` (`note` is pre-tagged server-side as `'🎙 ' + note`). The note **prefills `#bbDialNote`** (appends to anything the rep typed) and the suggested disposition shows as a colored hint next to the status.
- **Auto-saved to the lead on Stop (2026-07-29 fix):** `_bbAiStop()`/`_cvAiStop()` now also call `bbLogActivity(id, 'dialer'|'door_knock', null, data.note)` immediately when the summary comes back — the note lands in `customers.notes` + `lead_activity` right away instead of only being saved if the rep *also* taps a disposition button afterward. Root cause of the original bug: the AI summary only ever touched the textarea's DOM value: if a rep got interrupted or moved to the next lead before dispositioning, the transcript-derived note was silently never persisted anywhere. The **disposition itself is still always manual** (`outcome` is passed as `null` on the auto-save — `bbLogActivity` already tolerates a null outcome) — this only fixes note durability, it does not auto-pick booked/no_answer/etc. **Known tradeoff:** if the rep still taps a real disposition afterward without clearing the textarea, that logs a second, separately-tagged entry with the same note text + the real outcome — an accepted minor duplication, not a bug, in exchange for never silently losing a note again.
- **Feeds the 1:1s coaching pipeline more completely:** `coaching-agent.js` scans `lead_activity` for the 🎙 prefix grouped by `rep_name`. Previously any un-dispositioned call was invisible to it; auto-saving on Stop means every summarized call reaches `lead_activity`, dispositioned or not.
- Unsupported browser or denied mic degrades to a friendly message; short transcripts (<25 chars) are skipped rather than summarized into noise.

### AI Notes added to the Setter Lead Capture form (2026-08-23, per Dennis)
Reported missing for both Dennis and Ronda — correctly, it had never been built there. The Dialer/Doors version (`_cvAiControls`) calls `bbLogActivity(leadId, ...)`, which needs a real `customer_id` — but the New Lead form runs *before* the lead exists in `customers`, so that mechanism doesn't apply as-is.
- **`_nlAiControlsHtml()` / `nlAiToggle()` / `nlAiStop()`** (portal.html) — a standalone third implementation (not a reuse of `_cvAiControls`, which is leadId-shaped) that writes straight into the `#nlNotes` textarea already on the form, consent-gated through the same `_aiConsentGate('call', …)` § 632 flow as the Dialer and Doors versions, summarized through the same shared `_aiSummarize()` helper (so no second Anthropic-call implementation). Whatever lands in `#nlNotes` is exactly what `createNewLead()` already persists as the lead's opening note — no new save path needed.
- **One shared form, one control — covers both Dennis and Ronda automatically.** `#nlNotes` and the New Lead form itself are not duplicated per-role (same form the "No Automations" toggle sits on), so there was no second surface to add this to separately.
- Passes whatever's typed into `#nlFirst`/`#nlAddr` so far as lead context to the summarizer, even though the record doesn't exist in the DB yet.

### Quoya Assist — unified rebrand + live field auto-fill on New Lead Capture (2026-08-25, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Three previously separate AI-branded touchpoints — the live call/door transcription note-taker ("AI Notes"), the rep chat helper ("Solar Rep Assistant"/"Rep Assistant"), and the photo-based proposal savings/equipment analysis ("Quoya Insight") — are now all displayed as **"Quoya Assist"** everywhere a rep sees them (Dialer, Doors, New Lead Capture, Team dashboards, training tour, both call/door scripts, the rep-chat modal, the proposal builder's insight panel, the Ops job-detail insight card).
