Per Dennis: *"I want all to merge into one AI doing all features."* **Internal identifiers are untouched** — `_cvAiControls`, `_bbAiBegin`, `dialer-notes.js`, `openRepChat`, `generateProposalInsight`, `job.proposal.insight` etc. all keep their existing names; this is a display-layer rename plus one real functional upgrade (below), not a backend merger of three engines into one.
- **New Lead Capture is the flagship build — Quoya Assist now types straight into the form as the call happens**, not just a note-taker anymore. Per Dennis's exact ask: *"I want it designed to be an ai administration assistant transcribing and typing out the information as they call takes place... guide the caller if they're off track, or highlighting a spot if they're missing crucial information."*
  - **Button moved to the top of the form**, right under the No-Automations toggle (`#nlAutoNote`) — previously it sat buried down in the "Note to Tech" section near the bottom. Full-width, prominent gradient button: *"🤖 Quoya Assist — tap to start listening."*
  - **`netlify/functions/quoya-assist-extract.js`** (new) — hardened like `dialer-notes.js` (origin allowlist, payload reconstructed server-side, transcript capped). Takes the running transcript + whatever the form already knows, returns `{fields, missing, guidance}` via Claude Haiku. This is a **separate, cheaper, more-frequent call than the end-of-call summarizer** — `dialer-notes.js` still owns the final note; this one only extracts structured fields and a coaching line, called repeatedly while the call is live.
  - **`_qaStartLoop`/`_qaStopLoop`/`_qaKick`** (portal.html, shared by all three surfaces) — event-driven, not clock-driven (see the 2026-08-25 "types live in real time" fix below for why this changed). A failed/slow extraction pass is silently swallowed; it must never interrupt or stall the actual call.
  - **Auto-fillable fields:** First/Last Name, Cell Phone, Email, Service Address, Avg Monthly Bill, Existing Solar Yes/No, and all 3 Qualification Checklist items (Postcard Available / Utility Bill Available / Two Hour Window Prompted) — the checklist items are inferred from the conversation (e.g. the setter confirming the homeowner has the postcard in hand), not typed by the AI as free text.
  - ⚠️ **Never overwrites a field the setter has already edited (per Dennis's explicit answer on this).** `window._qaLock(fieldId)` is wired onto every auto-fillable field's real `oninput`/`onchange`/`onclick` handler — a genuine keystroke or click permanently protects that field for the rest of this form. Quoya's own writes go through `_qaApplyNlUpdates`, which sets `.value`/`.checked` directly **without dispatching a real input/click event**, so its own writes can never trip the lock. `_qaLock` also carries an `window._qaWriting` guard as a second line of defense. Locks reset only when a **new** New Lead form opens (`_qaResetNlState()`, called from `openNewLead`) — not on every start/stop of a single recording, so a setter who corrects a field, stops to think, and resumes recording doesn't have that correction silently unprotected again.
  - **Every auto-filled field gets a visible purple outline + a "Filled by Quoya Assist — double-check this" tooltip** (`_qaTagFilled`) so the setter always knows what to verify before submitting — this satisfies Dennis's "giving the opportunity for the phone setter to simply ensure the typed out information is accurate" requirement directly.
  - **Live "Still need: …" + coaching line** renders under the transcript (`#nlAiMissing`), driven by the same extraction pass's `missing`/`guidance` fields. `missing` lists required fields not yet known; `guidance` is one short coaching sentence, only when something is genuinely off (a required field seems to have been skipped past, or the conversation has drifted) — the prompt explicitly instructs `null` as the common case, never invented urgency.
  - Only ever sets a field when the transcript actually supports it — the extraction prompt explicitly forbids guessing ("leave null rather than fabricate").
- **Dialer and Doors get the rename + the SAME live coaching line, not literal field-typing.** Per Dennis's answer that this should span "all three surfaces," but an existing Black Box lead already has its name/phone/address on file — there's nothing blank to type into. What DOES carry over: `_qaStartLoop` runs on both, feeding the extraction endpoint the lead's **real known values** (never an empty object — an earlier draft of this sent `{}` for Dialer/Doors, which would have made the guidance pass wrongly nag the rep to ask for information that was already sitting right there; fixed before shipping) so `guidance` only fires for genuinely missing context. Renders in a new `#bbAiMissing` / `#cvAiMissing_<id>` line under each transcript panel.
- ⚠️ **Not built in this pass, flagged rather than silently dropped:** a single fused reasoning engine where the live-call panel can also answer ad-hoc rep questions (the old "Solar Rep Assistant" capability) or surface photo-based equipment analysis (the old "Quoya Insight" capability) mid-call. All three are unified in **name** and share the same visual identity, but remain three separate code paths today. Revisit if Dennis wants the actual backend capabilities merged, not just the branding — flagged as its own roadmap card, `quoya-assist-unified-engine`.
- Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit — clean throughout.

### "Not one answer was typed out" — the extraction loop was clock-driven, not live (fixed 2026-08-25, same day)
Real first test call: one field (First Name) got auto-filled early on with the correct purple tag, proving the pipeline worked end to end — but nothing else filled during the rest of the call, despite the setter asking every qualifying question. Per Dennis: *"I want Quoya Assist to actively type live in real time while the setter asks the questions."* The original `_qaStartLoop` only checked in on a flat `setInterval(…, 4000)` — so an answer could sit unread for up to 4 real seconds, plus the network + Claude round trip on top, before it had any chance of reaching a field. That is not what "typing it out as the call happens" means.
- **Fix: event-driven, not clock-driven.** `_qaStartLoop` now exposes `state.run` as a callable function and a new `_qaKick(key)` that runs it on demand. Every SpeechRecognition `onresult` handler (Dialer's `_bbAiRec`, Doors' `_cvAiRec`, New Lead Capture's `_nlAiRec`) now calls `_qaKick(...)` the instant it sees a new `isFinal` result — the browser's own signal that a spoken phrase just completed, a natural pause rather than an arbitrary clock tick. The old 4s `setInterval` is kept as a backstop only, shortened to 2.5s, for the rare case of one long unbroken run of speech with no `isFinal` boundary in between.
- ⚠️ **There is still an inherent floor.** Even with the trigger firing immediately, each check-in is a real Claude Haiku API round trip — realistically ~1-3 seconds, not instant. `_qaKick` removes the *avoidable* delay (waiting on a clock that had no relationship to when something was actually said); it does not make the network disappear. Set that expectation if "real time" reads as literally zero-latency.
- Not built in this pass, flagged as a possible follow-up: a zero-network instant path for indisputably-patterned fields (phone number, email) via client-side regex on the transcript, so those two fields could appear with no API latency at all. Skipped for now because browser speech-to-text doesn't reliably format spoken digit sequences as literal numerals across all browsers, so a regex pass risks being unreliable rather than genuinely faster — worth revisiting with real device testing before building it.

### "It's very slow" — a second dead-air gap found the same day (2026-08-25)
The event-driven kick above fixed the *trigger* (checking the instant a phrase completes instead of on a clock), but `state.run()`'s guard against overlapping calls (`if (state.extracting) return;`) silently DROPPED every kick that landed while a call was already in flight — during continuous back-and-forth conversation, the loop could go quiet for however long the in-flight call took, then wait for the NEXT isolated kick or the backstop timer, rather than immediately re-checking the moment it was free. Fixed with `state.pending`: a kick that arrives mid-flight is coalesced into exactly one guaranteed follow-up, fired the instant the current call's `finally` block clears — so the loop runs essentially back-to-back through active conversation instead of leaving a gap after every response. Backstop interval also tightened 2.5s → 1.5s (now genuinely just a backstop, not doing the real work), and `quoya-assist-extract.js`'s `max_tokens` trimmed 400 → 300 (the real payload is ~200-250 tokens; minor, since the model wasn't hitting the old cap anyway — not the primary fix here).
- ⚠️ **There is still a real floor that no client-side change can remove: a live Claude Haiku API round trip, typically well under 2s but not instant.** If it still feels slow after this fix, the next lever is a genuinely zero-network path for the two most patternable fields (phone, email) via client-side regex — deliberately not built yet, see the entry directly above for why.

## Setter Database + Onboarding Automation (2026-08-03)
Built after Dennis hired 3 setters and needed real per-person accountability plus a repeatable onboarding path. Extends the existing Team → 1:1s view rather than adding a new tab (deliberate — 1:1s is already where he runs the meeting).
- **Migration `20260803_setter_database.sql`** (✅ applied — verified 2026-08-08): `rep_shifts` (shift clock), `one_on_one_notes` (durable 1:1 record), plus `team_members.onboarded_at / gusto_status / gusto_sent_at / start_date`. Anon-key RLS, same trust model as `lead_activity`/`coaching_reports`. Everything degrades silently if unapplied — dialing is never blocked.
- **Shift clock:** Start/End Shift control in the Black Box Dialer header (`bbShiftStart`/`bbShiftEnd`/`bbShiftControlHtml`). One `rep_shifts` row per shift. An un-ended shift counts only up to now, and anything over 16h is excluded entirely rather than distorting the average (forgotten clock-out).
- **Per-call timing — no migration needed:** `lead_activity.call_duration` has existed since `20260702_black_box_dialer.sql` but **nothing ever wrote to it**. The Call button now stamps `bbCallStarted(leadId)` and `bbDialCommit` reads `_bbCallDurationFor(leadId)` at disposition. Stamps are per-lead (switching leads mid-call can't misattribute time) and anything >2h is discarded as a stale stamp.
- **`rep_id` bug fixed:** `bbLogActivity` was writing `rep_id: CURRENT_ROLE` — a role string like `'setter'`, not a person — so per-setter history could only ever be matched on the `rep_name` string. New `bbRepId()` uses `window._bbDialerPersonId` (already set by the sales portal at login) and falls back to the role. **Historical rows still carry the role**, so per-person queries should keep joining on `rep_name` until the backlog ages out.
- **1:1s view (`_team1on1sHtml`)** now renders per-rep: a 30-day metrics strip (dials, contacts, booked, contact rate, hours, dials/hr, avg call length, doors) above the existing AI coaching block, plus a **1:1 Record** section (`teamSave1on1`/`teamToggle1on1Form`) for what was discussed + what they committed to. Metrics come from a **second, wider `lead_activity` fetch** — the pre-existing query is note-only (`.not('note','is',null)`) because that's what renders, which would have undercounted dispositions logged without a note.
- **Onboarding (`🚀 Onboard New Hire` in Team & Hiring):** wraps `rep-onboard.js`, which already created the login + access code, upserted to GHL, and sent the Resend welcome email — but had **only ever been reachable from the public `onboarding.html`**, never from admin. Now `adminShowOnboard`/`adminRunOnboard` post to it with `role` (setter → `team_members.role='setter'` so they land in the dialer-first dashboard), `start_date`, and `gusto_url`.
- **Gusto is a link, not an API:** Gusto's provisioning API is partner-gated, so the welcome email embeds a contractor self-onboarding invite link and the hire completes their own W-9/1099-NEC details. The link is remembered per-device in `localStorage.gustoOnboardUrl` and falls back to the `GUSTO_ONBOARD_URL` Netlify env var; blank = the Gusto section is omitted from the email entirely.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-setterdb.js`): shift row shape, call-duration measurement + cross-lead isolation, `rep_id`/`call_duration` persistence, metric tiles computing off real activity, and the onboarding payload (role, lowercased email, gusto link, localStorage persistence).

### 1:1s ⟵ Team Dashboard consolidation (2026-08-06)
Per-rep performance lived in **two** places: the Black Box Dialer's collapsible "📊 Team Dashboard" (`bbTeamDashboardHtml()` — Day/Week/Month/Year range, full roster, calls/booked/AI-notes/latest-note/insight table) and **Team → 1:1s** (`_team1on1sHtml()` — elaborate per-rep cards with metrics strip, AI notes, coaching report, 1:1 record). Merged into 1:1s; the dialer now shows a two-button pointer strip to Team → 1:1s / Team → Dialers instead.
- **Roster bug (the reported symptom — "Dwayne and David aren't showing up"):** 1:1s built `repNames` purely from `lead_activity` rows that had a note, so any rep who hadn't logged one **vanished entirely** — precisely the person a 1:1 is for. Roster is now `team_members` (active, role rep/tech/setter/dialer) **∪** anyone with notes, canonicalized through `_repCanonical` so the admin alias folds in. Reps with activity sort first; the rest alphabetical. Same union pattern as the Dialers tab.
- **Range control absorbed:** `window._oooRange` + `_oooRangeMeta()` give Day / Week / Month / Year (default Month). It scopes **all three** queries (note fetch, wider metrics fetch, shift fetch) and retitles the per-rep metrics strip — previously the strip was hardcoded "Last 30 days".
- **At-a-glance table** from the old dashboard renders above the cards; each rep name anchors to its own `#ooo_<rep>` detail card below.
- ⚠️ **Test-harness gotcha worth remembering:** `portal.html` runs `var adminTeamMembers = []` at load, which **clobbers anything `page.addInitScript` set**. Stubbing the roster via `window.adminTeamMembers` therefore silently does nothing and the code falls back to the hardcoded `TECHS` list — which made the Dialers-tab roster assertion pass for the wrong reason (Dwayne only appeared there because he had a `rep_shifts` row). Assign it **inside `page.evaluate`** instead. Both harnesses now do, and `test-dialer-dash.js` gained a "Ghost" rep who is on shift but not on the roster to keep the union path honest.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-1on1-roster.js`) against the real 8-person roster: zero-activity reps present, inactive member and ops partner excluded, admin alias merged, summary rows and detail cards in agreement, and a note ~27h old correctly dropping out of the Day window while the rep stays on the roster.

### Tracerfy auto-apply was silently discarding every result (fixed 2026-08-07)
Reported as "why is this not automated — why am I downloading the email file and uploading it?" It **was** automated end to end: Phase 3 of `bb-auto-pipeline-background.js` submits all no-contact leads to Tracerfy, saves `queue_id` to `pipeline_state` (`tracerfy_pending_queue`), and the next nightly run downloads the finished CSV and applies it. The apply step was the problem — it did `const leadId = row['lead_id'] || null; if (!leadId) continue;`, but **Tracerfy's advanced trace does not echo back custom columns**, so `lead_id` is never present and *every row was skipped*. The nightly run therefore spent real skip-trace credits, downloaded the results, and wrote nothing — which is why the manual paste path existed at all. `tracerfy-results.js` already knew this (its comment says so) and matched on address; the pipeline never got the same treatment. Phase 3 now falls back to **street+zip** using the same `normStreet`/`normZip` helpers as the dedup key, builds the lead index only when some row actually lacks an id, **drops street-only keys that collide across zips** rather than guessing, and writes only rows that resolved. ⚠️ Do not reintroduce a `lead_id`-only match on Tracerfy output.

- **Recovering the credits already spent — `netlify/functions/tracerfy-backfill.js` (2026-08-07):** every skip trace submitted before the fix above was paid for and discarded. This walks Tracerfy's own queue history (`GET /v1/api/queues/?page=N`), downloads each completed queue's CSV, and applies it with the same street+zip matching. **Dry run by default** (`?apply=1` to write, `?maxQueues=N` to bound the walk). It indexes leads and **only ever fills blanks** — a lead that already has a phone keeps it — so the run is idempotent and a re-run cannot undo a rep's correction. Ambiguous street-only keys are dropped as everywhere else. Verified in `scratchpad/test-backfill.js`: suffix-folded + float-zip match lands, a lead with an existing phone/owner gains only the missing email, the wrong-city lead is never written, a dry run writes nothing, and a second run after the data has landed produces zero writes.
  - ⚠️ **Tracerfy rate-limits, and a 429 used to be indistinguishable from success (fixed 2026-08-08).** The queue walk broke out of the loop on any non-200 and reported `Found 0 queues` with every gain at zero — which reads as *"there is nothing left to recover"*, the exact opposite of the truth. `getQueuePage()` now retries a 429 four times with exponential backoff (honouring `Retry-After` when sent), and a run that still can't read the history returns `rateLimited:true` / `incomplete:true` plus an explicit log line. **A zero-gain run is only meaningful when `rateLimited` is false.** Gated in `scratchpad/test-backfill-429.js` — reverting the retry reproduces the reported output exactly (one attempt, `written:0`, no flag).

### ⚠️ Tracerfy returns NO do-not-call data (confirmed 2026-08-08 — AUTHORITATIVE)
The backfill logged the real CSV header from a live queue:
`address,city,state,zip,first_name,last_name,mail_address,mail_city,mail_state,mailing_zip,primary_phone,primary_phone_type,email_1..email_5,mobile_1..mobile_5,landline_1..landline_3`
- **There is no DNC / litigator / TCPA column of any kind.** `trace_type: "advanced"` at `credits_per_lead: 2` buys names, phones and emails only. The `dnc: 0` in the backfill is accurate, not a parsing miss.
- **Therefore no DNC data has ever entered the system from skip-trace.** Every `customers.dnc = true` came from a rep manually flagging it in the dialer. The dialer correctly excludes `dnc=true` from its queue — but nothing authoritative has ever set it. ~8.6k skip-traced phone numbers are unscrubbed.
- **This is a TCPA exposure, not a data-quality one.** Cold-calling homeowners off a permit list is squarely the telemarketing case the National DNC Registry covers. Scrubbing options, in order of directness: (a) subscribe to the FTC's National DNC Registry as a seller and scrub by area code — the authoritative source, tiered pricing with a limited free allowance, **verify current terms at telemarketing.donotcall.gov before relying on any figure**; (b) a commercial scrub vendor; (c) ask Tracerfy whether a DNC/litigator scrub is sold as a separate trace type. Do **not** treat the absence of a flag as permission to call.
- ✅ **`mail_address` / `mail_city` / `mail_state` / `mailing_zip` are now captured** — see "Absentee owners / rentals" immediately below.

### Absentee owners / rentals — mailing address captured (2026-08-08)
Every Tracerfy trace has always returned the owner's **mailing** address next to the property, and all four columns were discarded for a year. When the mail goes somewhere other than the house, the owner doesn't live there: that door is a **rental**, which is exactly the `renter` disposition a rep currently burns a knock discovering. It also changes the conversation — an absentee owner is a landlord thinking about the asset, not a homeowner thinking about their power bill.
- **Migration `20260808_owner_mailing.sql`** (⚠️ **not yet applied**) adds `mail_address`, `mail_city`, `mail_state`, `mail_zip`, and `absentee boolean` to `customers`, plus a partial index on `absentee is true`.
- **Written by both skip-trace apply paths** — `tracerfy-backfill.js` and `bb-auto-pipeline-background.js` Phase 3. `absentee` is derived at write time by comparing **normalized street+zip** (`normStreet`/`normZip`, the same suffix-folding key as the dedup matcher) of the situs against the mailing address, so `77 Oak Ave` vs `77 OAK AVENUE` in zip `92064.0` correctly reads as owner-occupied. ⚠️ **It stays `null` unless BOTH sides parse to a street *and* a zip** — a missing zip must not flag every owner-occupied house as a rental, and a false rental flag is worse than no flag.
- **Same wrong-column bug found in Phase 3 while building this:** like the backfill before it, `bb-auto-pipeline-background.js` wrote the skip-traced person into `title_owner` instead of `first_name`/`last_name`, so the nightly run skipped the human name on every trust-owned lead. Fixed in the same pass, along with widening DNC detection over any `/dnc|do_not_call|litigator|tcpa/` column (which finds nothing today — see above — but will if Tracerfy ever adds one).
- **Portal side:** `_bbAbsenteeNote(lead)` renders "🏠 Owner lives elsewhere — mail goes to Los Angeles, CA. Likely a rental…" on the **dialer's current-call card** and the **rep Doors card**, right under `_bbTrusteeNote`. The Doors stats strip also shows "N of M on this route are owner-not-resident" (client-side over the loaded route — free, no extra query).
- ⚠️ **`bbMailSel(base)` is a one-shot capability probe, and it is load-bearing.** PostgREST **400s the entire query** on one unknown column. The canvass `SEL` has *no* fallback tier — an unknown column there sets `canvassGpsStatus='error'` and takes the whole Doors route down for every rep until the migration is run. `bbMailSel` probes `select=absentee&limit=1` once per session, caches the result in `_bbMailColsOk`, and appends `BB_MAIL_COLS` only if it succeeded. **Any future column added to a canvass or dialer select needs the same treatment** — the dialer's FULL→BASE fallback would silently drop callback/dial state instead of erroring, which is arguably worse.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-backfill.js` (mail columns captured, another city flags absentee, suffix/float-zip variant of the *same* address reads owner-occupied, absentee stays unset with no mailing data) and `scratchpad/test-absentee-card.js` (note renders for a rental and stays silent for an owner-occupied or unknown door; **a portal running against a pre-migration database still loads its dialer queue and asks for no missing column**). Gated both ways — reverting the normalization flags the owner-occupied door, and removing the probe reintroduces the 400.
- **Measured rate (2026-08-08, first full population): 1,524 rentals / 7,517 owner-occupied / 9,967 unknown** — i.e. **~17% of skip-traced doors are landlord-owned**, about 1 in 6. The unknowns are leads never skip-traced, the same population as the ~9.6k with no contact info. Worth knowing before designing anything downstream: a rental filter would hide roughly a sixth of the route, which is why the flag is a *note*, not a filter.
- ⚠️ **`absentee` must be derivable independently of `mail_address` (fixed 2026-08-08).** The derive originally sat *inside* the `if (mAddr && !lead.mail_address)` branch, so once any path stored the mailing address — the nightly pipeline's Phase 3 does — that branch never ran again and the flag could never be backfilled. It is now a separate `lead.absentee == null` pass, which fires once per lead and then converges. Gated in `test-backfill.js` step [3]/[4]: a lead with an address but a null flag gains exactly `{absentee}` (not a rewritten address), and the following run writes nothing.
- **A zero-gain backfill run has two causes and they must not look alike.** `mailRowsWithValue` / `mailBlockedByExisting` are reported in the log and JSON precisely so "the vendor sent blanks" can be told apart from "every matched lead already has it" — the second is what actually happened on 2026-08-08 (10,762 of 10,762 blocked by existing data). Same lesson as the 429 and the Regrid `no_key` retraction: **never let an untaken code path and a real negative produce the same output.**
- Not built: an absentee **filter/sort key** on the route, and a landlord-specific script stage. Both are cheap once the column has data; neither is worth designing before Dennis has seen how many of his doors are rentals.

### "Last pipeline run: 3d ago" while the pipeline was running nightly (fixed 2026-08-08)
Reported as a stalled nightly pipeline. It was not stalled — `pipeline_state` showed **`phase2_offset` updated 2026-08-07 09:45 while `last_run_at` still read 2026-08-05**, i.e. the run was doing real work and dying before the finish line. `bb-auto-pipeline-background` wrote `last_run_at` **once, after Phase 4**, so any run killed mid-flight (Netlify caps a background function at 15 min, and a 09:00 start still writing at 09:45 means more than one invocation is in play) left the portal reporting the last *completed* run.
- **Fix: `checkpoint(phase)` after every phase** — writes `last_run_at`, the new **`last_run_phase`**, and a partial `last_run_summary`. The final call passes `'complete'`. `checkpoint` swallows its own errors on purpose: a failed stamp must never kill a run.
- **Portal** (`adminCanvassLoadPhase0Panel`) reads `last_run_phase` and renders the timestamp amber with "⚠ stopped after Phase 2 — did not finish (earlier phases still saved)" for anything but `complete`.
- ⚠️ **This does NOT fix the underlying timeout** — it makes it visible and names the phase. If runs consistently stop at the same phase, that phase is over its time budget.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-pipeline-checkpoint.js`: all nine checkpoints fire in order, none carries the stale timestamp, a run killed after Phase 2 reports `phase2` rather than claiming completion, and the portal both reads the field and warns on it. Gated both ways — stripping the per-phase calls leaves a single `complete` write and reproduces the stale-timestamp report exactly.
- **Same family as the Regrid `no_key`, the Tracerfy 429, and the mailing-capture zero, all found the same day: a partial or never-taken code path rendering identically to "nothing happened."** When adding any long multi-stage job, checkpoint progress as it goes — do not report only on success.

### "P1: 0 permits" meant four different things (fixed 2026-08-08)
Asked whether a nightly run could be simulated to see if the permit pull was working. It could not be answered, because **every non-200 from PermitStack hit `break` with no `stamp()` and no counter** — in Phase 1c *and* Phase 1b. A dead `PERMITSTACK_KEY` (401), a rate limit (429), a malformed body, and a genuinely quiet night all produced the identical output: `P1: 0 permits`.
- **Fix:** a `tally(phase, reason)` counter plus `tallyReport()`. Reasons are `ok` / `empty` / `end_of_pages` / `http_<status>` / `bad_json` / `unexpected_shape`. The first 3 of any HTTP status are also stamped by city+installer (a dead key would otherwise spam ~600 identical lines). The report lands in the log **and** in `last_run_summary.permit_pull_outcomes`, so it reaches the portal rather than only Netlify's console.
- **`max_seconds` body param** bounds `GLOBAL_DEADLINE` (never above the 13.5-min Netlify cap). Added so a simulated run finishes in seconds instead of burning the full budget; also usable for a bounded manual run.
- **How to actually simulate a run:** ⚡ **Run Full Pipeline** on the Black Box tab hits the real APIs — that is the only thing that proves the live integrations work. A local run against stubs (`scratchpad/test-permit-pull.js`) proves the code executes and *reports* correctly, nothing more. ⚠️ The dev sandbox cannot invoke it: `fixmy.energy` is 403 at the agent proxy and the Netlify MCP has no function-invocation operation.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-permit-pull.js`: the four failure modes produce four **distinct** outcome strings and the report reaches `last_run_summary`. Gated both ways — reverting the tally collapses all four to one identical report ("no permit queries ran"), reproducing the bug exactly.
- **Fifth instance of the same pattern in one day**, after the Regrid `no_key`, the Tracerfy 429, the mailing-capture zero and the pipeline's stale timestamp. ⚠️ **STANDING RULE: never let a failing or never-taken code path produce the same output as a real negative.** Count the reason.

#### …and the tally had the same hole in it (fixed 2026-08-09, from the first live run)
The 2026-08-09 nightly run reported **`P1: 0 permits · P2: 0 owners`** with no reason attached, and the log showed **~44 consecutive `fetch err: This operation was aborted`** lines against PermitStack. Two defects, both the same pattern one level up from the fix that was supposed to end it:
1. **`tally()` only covered HTTP statuses and JSON shapes — never a *thrown* fetch.** All four `catch(e)` sites (Socrata, 1c, 1d, 1b) stamped a log line and counted nothing, so a vendor timing out on every query produced `no permit queries ran` — a report claiming the phase never even ran. New **`netReason(e)`** classifies the throw as **`timeout`** (AbortController fired — vendor up but slow, the per-request budget is the lever) vs **`net_error`**, and all four sites now tally. The non-200 `break`s in 1d and `trySocrata` were untallied too; both now record `http_<status>`.
2. **The tally was folded into `progress` only after the FINAL phase.** The live run died at Phase 3, so `last_run_summary` carried no `permit_pull_outcomes` at all — the reasons were missing precisely in the case they were built for. `checkpoint()` now computes `tallyReport()` on **every** write. ⚠️ Same lesson as the per-phase checkpointing itself: **report progress as it happens, never only on success.**
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-permit-pull.js` — five failure modes (`dead_key`/`rate_limited`/`garbage`/`quiet`/`timeout`) produce five distinct strings, and all four `phase1*` checkpoints carry the tally. Gated both ways: reverting the catch tally reproduces `timeout → "no permit queries ran"` exactly; reverting the checkpoint change drops mid-run coverage to 0/4.
- **Benign, deliberately not "fixed":** Phase 2 logs `Regrid 403 — disabling for this run` ~20 times. `regridPausedUntil` is correctly run-scoped; a concurrent batch of lookups all read it before the first 403 returned. It costs ~20 wasted requests once per run and then genuinely stops. Not a scoping bug — don't rewrite it.

### Live pipeline status (2026-08-09 run — read before diagnosing "the pipeline is broken")
The instrumentation works; the three zeros have three different real causes, two of them account-level and neither fixable in code:
- **PermitStack: every query aborts at the 10s timeout.** Vendor-side slowness or throttling. This is why `P1: 0 permits`. Now visible as `1c:timeout ×N` in the summary.
- **Regrid: HTTP 403 on every call.** `REGRID_KEY` **is** set — 403 = authenticated but **not entitled** (free account, API is a paid product). Confirms the retraction note above from the other direction. Phase 2 added 0 owners but did save 292 coordinates. `phase2_offset` wrapped 1000→0 against a **pool of only 1,292** — the owner backlog is essentially exhausted, consistent with 93% coverage.
- **Tracerfy: out of credits** — *"You need 236 more credits."* Phase 3 submitted nothing and the run ended there (Phases 4+ never ran). ⚠️ **This is the funnel's actual bottleneck** (Phone 9,091 of 19,036 → Activated 15), so it is the one of the three worth spending money on.

### Search Console sync died silently for four weeks (2026-08-09)
Reported as "the performance online is failing miserably" off a Google Business Profile digest (July 2026: 76 profile views, 0 calls, 0 website clicks). Pulling `seo_metrics` showed the deeper problem: **the only rows that have ever existed are 2026-07-03 → 2026-07-13 — 11 days, 56 impressions, 0 clicks — and nothing has been written since.**
- **Not a code regression.** The `gsc_service_account` row in `app_config` is intact and untouched since 2026-07-04 (valid `client_email`, 1704-char `private_key`), `seo-insights.js` is unchanged, its `40 13 * * *` schedule is present, and Google's endpoints are reachable. The break is external — a revoked/rotated GCP key, a lost Search Console grant, a changed property, or genuinely zero traffic — **and nothing anywhere recorded which.**
- ⚠️ **The function had three silent exits**: no credential → `200 {skipped}`; any throw → `500` into Netlify logs only; and **an empty Google result → `supaUpsert` returns early on `!rows.length` and the run still reports `ok`**. A dead credential and a site with no search visibility produced the identical outcome — the same failure family as the Regrid `no_key`, the Tracerfy 429 and the permit tally.
- **Fix:** `classifyFailure(e)` maps the HTTP status to `auth` / `forbidden` / `property_not_found` / `rate_limited` / `upstream`, each with the actual remedy (401 ⇒ key disabled or rotated by org policy; 403 ⇒ service account lost property access or the API is off on the GCP project; 404 ⇒ `sc-domain:fixmy.energy` vs `https://fixmy.energy/` are *different objects*). Every terminal path now calls `writeStatus()`, and `ok_no_rows` is a distinct status from `ok`.
- ⚠️ **The health stamp goes in `pipeline_state`, NOT `app_config`.** `app_config` holds this very service-account private key plus the business and personal Plaid access tokens and is service-role-only; `pipeline_state` is already read by the portal with the anon key and carries no secrets. **A health stamp must never be the reason a secrets table gets opened to anon.** No migration either way.
- **Portal (`loadSEOPulse`):** the panel printed *"vs prior 28 days · updated nightly"* above whatever rows existed, so it presented month-old numbers as current for four weeks. It now reads the status row, renders `_seoSyncBanner` (amber, names the reason + the fix + a **Run sync now** button that hits the function and alerts the real error), and the caption states the **actual newest data date** instead of claiming nightly freshness. A working sync that legitimately found zero impressions renders a *different* message from a failing one.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-seo-health.js` across six states — failing / zero-rows / unconfigured / healthy / stale-with-no-status / fresh-with-no-status. Gated both ways: removing the banner call reproduces the reported bug (stale figures, no warning).
- **Context for whoever picks this up:** 56 impressions over 11 days at avg position 6.3 means the site ranks acceptably when it appears and almost never appears. GBP's 76 views/month is the same problem, not a separate one — **~0 organic demand reaching the site, not a conversion problem.** The 2026-07-23 SEO audit's unfixed items (no E-E-A-T/About/testimonial content, no blog, hero not WebP) are the content side of it; `GROWTH_ACTIONS.md` still has the GBP pass, review-ask workflow and Local Services Ads open.

### GA4 numbers were structurally dependent on Search Console (fixed 2026-08-10)
Found while wiring `GA4_PROPERTY_ID` — the GA4 half of `seo-insights.js` could **never** have produced data on this site. The GA4 response was merged **into** the Search Console rows:
```js
metricRows.forEach(m => { const g = byDate[m.date]; if (g) { …ga_sessions… } });
```
`metricRows` is built from the GSC daily query, so **zero GSC rows ⇒ zero GA4 rows written**, even on a perfectly good GA4 response. Search Console has returned nothing since 2026-07-13, so setting the property ID would have produced an empty panel and read as *"the Property ID is wrong."* Same failure family as the Regrid `no_key` and the Tracerfy 429: **a never-taken code path rendering identically to a real negative.**
- **Fix:** the two sources are **UNIONed** by date (`byRowDate`), so GA4 lands whether or not GSC has anything. ⚠️ `summary.emptyWindow` is now judged on `daily.length` (the GSC rows) rather than `metricRows.length` — after the union the latter can be non-empty purely from GA4, which would hide the fact that GSC reported nothing.
- ⚠️ **All rows are normalised to one key shape before upsert.** PostgREST rejects a bulk insert whose objects have differing keys (`PGRST102` "All object keys must match") — and after a union some rows carry GA4 columns while others don't. This was a latent bug even before the union, since GA4 rarely covers every GSC date.
- **GA4 health is reported separately from Search Console** (`ga4Reason` / `ga4Hint` in the `seo_sync_status` stamp; `_seoGa4Banner()` in portal.html renders alongside `_seoSyncBanner`). A working GSC sync must never imply the GA4 half is fine. **403 ⇒ the service account lacks Viewer on the property; 404 ⇒ wrong Property ID** (people paste the `G-` measurement ID instead of the numeric one) — deliberately different messages, and neither takes the Search Console status down with it.
- **Two setup facts confirmed 2026-08-10:** the live property is **`FIXMy.energy`, numeric ID `534612604`** (measurement ID `G-XFKY7F95JR`, which is on all 21 public pages). ⚠️ There is a **second, empty GA4 property** (`G-SG1FE12QH1`, property `538467012`) auto-created under an account literally named "Google Ads Account" — it has never received a hit and is **not** the one to use. Which properties a login can see depends on the Google account, which is why it looked like the real property had vanished.
- ⚠️ **GA4 "Key events: 0" is the Ads conversion problem, not a GA4 bug.** Ads imports key events from GA4; if `schedule_appointment` was never *marked* as a key event in GA4 → Admin → Events, GA4 records the event but reports zero key events and Ads shows "No recent conversions". Check the Events list before assuming the tag is broken — a genuine zero (nobody booked) looks identical.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-seo-ga4.js` (20 assertions, real RSA key so `googleToken` runs unmodified): GA4 rows land with an empty GSC window, the union keeps both sources with one key shape, 403/404/unset produce three distinct diagnoses, and the analytics scope is requested only when a property is configured. **Gated both ways** — restoring the inner join drops 6 assertions and reproduces the empty-panel bug exactly.

### The status row hid the one field it was diagnosing (fixed 2026-08-13)
After the timeout fix (below) resolved the actual blank-page bug, `pipeline_state.seo_sync_status` still showed a healthy `"status":"ok"` run with **no `campaigns` or `campaignsReason` field at all** — the run that was supposed to prove the new campaign-source feature was working couldn't confirm it, from the one surface built specifically to check.
- **Root cause:** `writeStatus()` takes an **explicit field allow-list**, not `...summary` — deliberate, so a stray field can't leak into the persisted status unreviewed. But that means every new `summary.*` field has to be added to that list by hand, or it's silently invisible to `pipeline_state` while still being present in the raw HTTP response body. `campaigns`/`campaignsReason` were computed correctly the whole time; they just never made the list when the feature was built.
- ⚠️ **Same family as the Regrid `no_key`, Tracerfy 429, permit tally, mailing-capture zero, and pipeline-checkpoint entries — a sixth instance in this file of a real result rendering as absence.** This one is narrower (one function's own diagnostic surface, not a whole pipeline) but the shape is identical: computed correctly, silently dropped before the one place anyone actually looks.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-seo-campaigns.js` step [8]: the persisted status row now carries the real campaign count on success and the real HTTP status on failure (`http_403`, etc.), not just the raw response. **Gated** — reverting the two added lines reproduces the exact empty-of-campaigns shape seen in the live `pipeline_state` row during troubleshooting.
- **Diagnostic sequence, for the record (2026-08-13):** reported as a blank page on manual test → ruled out browser tab/cache (fresh history + tabs, still blank) → confirmed `SUPA_SERVICE_KEY` present in Netlify env (ruled out) → Netlify's own Function log viewer was independently broken that day ("Function logs are currently unavailable") → used Netlify's **"Run now"** button + a direct `pipeline_state` SQL check as the actual ground truth, bypassing both the broken log viewer and the browser entirely → confirmed the function completes end-to-end (`status:"ok"`) → then found this narrower gap once real data was finally visible. **When the platform's own tooling is unreliable, a health-stamp table you control is worth more than the vendor's dashboard.**

### seo-insights.js blank response on manual test (fixed 2026-08-13)
Reported: hitting the function URL directly in Safari to test the campaign-report build showed a genuinely blank page — no error, no JSON, nothing. **Three** independent problems, and the first fix pass (parallelizing the GA4 calls + explicit Content-Type) did not resolve it — reported "same error" — because the real gap was structural, in `netlify.toml`, not in the function body.
- ⚠️ **A scheduled function's manual-GET budget is NOT its scheduled-run budget.** `netlify.toml`'s own comment says "Scheduled functions run as background functions (up to 15 min)" — true for the invocation Netlify's own cron scheduler makes, but a human hitting `/.netlify/functions/seo-insights` in a browser is a **plain HTTP call**, not a scheduled invocation, and does not inherit that 15-minute budget — only the default synchronous limit. The nightly run may have been completing fine the whole time; only manual testing was silently starved. `permitstack-pull` already carries `timeout = 26` for exactly this reason — `seo-insights` didn't, and nobody had needed to manually test it since the campaign report made it slow enough to matter. Added the same override.
- ⚠️ **Deliberately NOT converted to a `-background.js` function**, even though that's the documented pattern for slow calls elsewhere in this repo (`bb-auto-pipeline-background.js` etc.) and would raise the ceiling further. A background function always returns `202` immediately with no body — that would make manual testing **worse**, not better, since the whole point of hitting this URL by hand is to read the JSON summary back. `timeout = 26` (synchronous, readable) is the correct fix for this specific use case; background conversion would trade the debuggability this function was built for.
- Gated in `scratchpad/test-seo-campaigns.js` step [7]: string-checks the `netlify.toml` block for `timeout = 26` alongside the preserved `schedule` line. Reverting drops it and fails.

Two independent problems, both in `seo-insights.js` itself (kept as real fixes — they reduce load even with the timeout raised, and one of them may still matter to a slow cold start):
- **Up to 9 sequential external round trips.** `app_config` read → token exchange → GSC daily → (GA4 date report) → `seo_metrics` upsert → GSC queries+pages → `seo_queries` upsert → (GA4 campaign report, new) → `seo_campaigns` upsert. Adding the campaign report as a second back-to-back Google Analytics Data API call pushed a cold start further into Netlify's execution-time risk — the manual-test symptom of a function that never finishes is silence, not an error page.
  - **Fix:** the two GA4 Data API calls (per-date, per-campaign) are independent — same `token`, no shared dependency — so they're now fired with `Promise.all` instead of one after the other. Each fetch is wrapped in `.catch(e => ({ __fetchError: e }))` so a network failure on one doesn't short-circuit `Promise.all` before the other resolves; each branch then re-throws into its own existing try/catch, preserving all prior error handling and `summary.*Reason` fields unchanged.
  - Verified with two 60ms-sleeping mock fetches: `elapsed < 115ms` proves overlap (sequential would be ≥120ms), and a dispatch-order log confirms the campaign fetch starts before the date fetch resolves. **Gated** — reverting to sequential calls reproduces ~125ms and fails the timing assertion.
- **No response ever set `Content-Type`.** All 5 return paths (`SUPA_SERVICE_KEY` missing, credential load failure, no-credential skip, success, classified failure) returned a bare `{ statusCode, body }`. A browser has to guess how to render an untyped body — explicit `'Content-Type': 'application/json'` on every path removes the ambiguity. **Gated** — stripping the headers fails a source-level assertion checking all 5 return statements.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-seo-campaigns.js` (16 assertions total, up from 13).

### Two live GA4 bugs found the same day the account was actually opened (2026-08-13)
Walking Dennis through Search Console recovery + `GA4_PROPERTY_ID` setup surfaced two real problems in the live Google account, neither a portal bug — worth recording because the fix instructions are Google-console steps, not code.
- **All four marked GA4 key events were dead.** `ads_conversion_Book_appointment_1`, `close_convert_lead`, `purchase`, `qualify_lead` — none of these strings exist anywhere in the site's code (`grep` across every public HTML file + functions returns nothing), and GA4's own Events screen showed **"No stream data detected"** on all four. The real booking event, `schedule_appointment` (fires from `book.html`, `booked.html`, and `index.html`), was sitting unmarked in "Recent events" the whole time. `ads_conversion_Book_appointment_1`'s naming is the tell — that's GA4's auto-generated placeholder for an imported Google Ads conversion action, meaning **Ads has likely had zero real conversion signal since setup**, not just since GSC went dark. Marking `schedule_appointment` as a key event fixes GA4's own count; it does **not** automatically fix Ads — the Ads conversion action itself needs to be repointed at `schedule_appointment` (or recreated), a Google Ads console change outside this repo.
- ⚠️ **Two different GA4 properties exist under different Google accounts** — the CLAUDE.md note above (`534612604` real vs `538467012` empty) is why this took two screenshots to confirm; always check the numeric Property ID, not just that "GA4 looks connected."

### Campaign-source breakdown — "is Google Ads worth it" without the Ads API (2026-08-13)
Building toward that question surfaced there is **no Google Ads API integration anywhere in this repo** — `seo-insights.js` only ever talked to Search Console + GA4. Full Ads API access needs a developer token (manager-account approval, days-to-weeks) and OAuth (not the service account already in use for GSC/GA4) — a real project, not a quick add.
- **The cheap 90% of the answer needs no new Google credentials.** GA4 already carries `sessionSource` / `sessionMedium` / `sessionCampaignName` on every session — Ads traffic included, since `gclid`/`gbraid`/`wbraid` capture on `book.html`/`index.html`/`thank-you.html` already feeds GA4's own attribution (see the `/booked` conversion-tracking entry above). A third GA4 Data API report, added to `seo-insights.js` step 4, pulls sessions + key events grouped by source/medium/campaign over the same 30-day window and upserts into a **new** `seo_campaigns` table (migration `20260813_seo_campaigns.sql`, ⚠️ **not yet applied**). Same snapshot pattern as `seo_queries` — one row per (date, source, medium, campaign), overwritten daily.
- **What it can't answer:** cost, CPC, spend-by-campaign. Those live only in the Ads API. Monthly total spend is already in the P&L, so a rough ROI read (sessions/bookings per source vs. total spend) is possible without ever touching Ads API scope — full per-campaign cost would need the real integration.
- ⚠️ **Independently guarded, same as every optional add-on in this file.** The campaign report runs in its own try/catch; a missing `seo_campaigns` table (unapplied migration) or a Data API hiccup must not take down the GSC/GA4 pull it sits after — that pull is what everything else depends on. Gated: removing the guard makes an unmigrated table fail the *entire* sync with `500` and `ok:false`, the same failure class as five prior "unrelated add-on kills the whole run" bugs in this file (Regrid, Tracerfy, permit tally, mailing capture, pipeline checkpoint).
- **Portal side:** SEO Pulse panel gains a "Traffic by source · 30 days" table under Top Queries, sorted by key events. Paid rows (medium matching `cpc|ppc|paid`) get a green **`$ AD`** badge so Ads-driven bookings are visually distinct from organic/direct without needing the Ads API to tell them apart. The query is wrapped in its own try/catch — an unmigrated `seo_campaigns` table degrades to "just no campaign table yet," never a blank panel.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-seo-campaigns.js` (13 assertions, real RSA key so `googleToken` runs unmodified): the report writes real campaign/session/key-event data, a session with no campaign is recorded as `(not set)` rather than dropped, a missing table cannot break the core sync, and the paid/organic/direct classification matches GA4's actual medium values. **Gated** — stripping the try/catch around the campaign block turns a missing table into a full sync failure.

### Both spouses off the title, editable phone, no vendor names on a rep's card (2026-08-11, per Dennis)
Three field requests on the Black Box card. All three land on **both** Doors and Dialing, because the owner block and `bbEmailRowHtml` were already shared renderers — the standing rule is satisfied by construction rather than by remembering.
- ⚠️ **Rep-facing text must never describe how a lead was built.** The trust note read *"…owner lookup can't fix a trust. **Skip-trace resolves this one.**"* — that is proprietary pipeline detail and it is not actionable at a door. Rewritten to what the rep should *do*, with the internal detail behind `CURRENT_ROLE === 'admin'` (same pattern as `_aiNoteFailText`). **A vendor name in a `fetch()` URL or an admin-gated line is fine; anything a rep can read is not.**
- **`_bbOwnerContacts(raw)` — both people on title.** The assessor writes a couple as `SURNAME FIRST1 & FIRST2`, and a family trust keeps that shape: `BYRNE RAYMOND & SANDRA REVOCABLE FAMILY TRUST 09-27-` is Raymond *and* Sandra Byrne. We were collapsing that to one surname, so a rep asked for "the Byrne household" when two first names were sitting in the string. The Ask-for line now names them ("Ask for: **Raymond or Sandra Byrne**") and `_bbOwnerPeopleHtml` renders a tap-to-adopt button per person.
  - ⚠️ **A COMPANY must never yield a person.** Without the blocklist, `CAROLINE PROPERTIES LLC` produces the contact *"Properties Caroline"* — a fabricated human a rep would then ask for by name. `PROPERTIES|INVESTMENTS|HOLDINGS|MANAGEMENT|ENTERPRISES|REALTY|ASSOCIATES|GROUP|DEVELOPMENT|VENTURES|CAPITAL|LLC|INC|CORP|COMPANY|PARTNERS|LP|LTD` disqualifies extraction outright. **Family/living TRUSTS are deliberately NOT blocked** — those genuinely carry both spouses, which is the entire point.
  - Single-letter tokens are middle initials, not people (`STRICKER MICHAEL&AMIE F`).
  - **No migration:** adopting a name writes the existing `first_name`/`last_name`, which every card, script and export already prefers over the roll string. The row hides itself once a real name is on file.
  - Also fixed: `_bbOwnerParse` stripped a *full* trailing recording date but not the truncated form the roll sometimes carries (`… TRUST 09-27-`), which rendered as visible junk.
- **`bbPhoneRowHtml` / `bbEditPhone` / `bbSavePhone`** mirror the email row exactly and mount beside it, so both surfaces get phone editing from one renderer. ⚠️ **A malformed number is refused, not saved** — a half-typed phone that persists is worse than none, since the dialer would call it forever. Reuses `_bbEmailEditing` as the guard that suppresses the Doors GPS-tick re-render mid-type.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-owner-contacts.js` (26 assertions). **Gated both ways** — restoring the vendor line fails step 4, and removing the company blocklist reproduces "Properties Caroline" *and* "LLC Caroline" exactly.
- ⚠️ **Harness traps hit here:** a `'[^']*vendor[^']*'` regex spans newlines and matches explanatory **comments**, failing with the code perfectly correct — strip comments first. And counting `_bbOwnerPeopleHtml(lead)` matched the function *definition* as well as its two call sites; count the mount (`h += …`), not the name.

### An anonymous "Rep" row on every per-rep report (fixed 2026-08-11)
Spotted on Team → Dialers: a row labelled just **`Rep`** with 0.8h and real dials, next to the named roster. Same family as the "Admin" split documented above — a generic label reaching `lead_activity.rep_name` — but a different cause, and this one loses the person entirely rather than splitting them.
- **Root cause, two halves.** `window._bbDialerPersonName` / `_bbDialerPersonId` were assigned **inside the `salesView === 'canvass'` branch** of `renderSales`, and the dialer branch (`else if (salesView === 'dialer') { renderBBDialerView(); return; }`) **returns above it**. So a rep who went straight to **Dialing without ever opening Doors** never had an identity set. `bbRepName()` then fell through to `prompt(...) || 'Rep'` — and **cached the dismissed prompt in `localStorage.bbDialerRepName`**, so every call from that device was stamped `Rep` from then on.
- **Fix:** both assignments hoisted to the top of `renderSales`, before any view branch — identity is now set no matter which surface the rep lands on. And a blank/dismissed prompt is **never persisted**; it returns `'Unidentified rep'` for that one write instead of poisoning the device.
- **`David` → `David Samuel McGruder` added to `REP_ALIASES` (2026-08-11).** Same root cause: he reached Dialing before ever opening Doors, was prompted, and typed a short name that then cached. Folded at **read time** so the historical rows merge instantly with no data migration. ⚠️ **A first-name alias is only safe while exactly ONE person on the roster has that first name — remove it if a second David is hired.**
- ⚠️ **Do not add `Rep` to `REP_ALIASES`.** The Admin aliases map a known label to a known person; `Rep` is anonymous by construction and could be more than one human. Identify them from `lead_activity.rep_id` (which `bbRepId()` sets from `_bbDialerPersonId`, falling back to the role string) and rename the rows.
- **Repair SQL** — identify, then rename:
```sql
select rep_id, channel, count(*), min(created_at), max(created_at)
from lead_activity where rep_name = 'Rep' group by 1,2 order by 5 desc;
update lead_activity set rep_name = '<real name>' where rep_name = 'Rep' and rep_id = '<their id>';
```
  The affected device also needs `localStorage.bbDialerRepName` cleared, or it keeps stamping the old value until the rep next opens Doors.
- ⚠️ **Same lesson as the dialer `isPinned` bug: state assigned inside one view branch is not available to the others.** Anything the whole portal depends on belongs above the branch.

### Training module — modules, role tracks, comprehension checks (2026-08-11, per Dennis)
Extends the 2026-08-08 New Hire Walkthrough rather than replacing it. Decisions from Dennis: **hard gate** (no dialing or knocking until certified), **explain-and-retry** on a wrong answer (nobody fails out), **server-backed versioned certification**, and **role-based tracks that STACK** — "in the event that a setter is promoted to a Tech, they would only need to complete the latter portion."
- ⚠️ **THE PREREQUISITE, and it would have been a severe bug.** `rep_agreements` is a shared ledger — signed agreement, sub-sheet acks, and now training certs, told apart by `source`. The login gate picked "the agreement" with `find(x => x.source !== SUB_SHEET_ACK_SOURCE)`, so **the first training cert written would have been read as the rep agreement**, failed the `REP_AGREEMENT_VERSION` check, and forced *every rep* to re-sign. Replaced with `_isRepAgreementRow(r)`: NULL source ⇒ agreement (legacy rows predate the column), `sub_sheet_ack` and anything prefixed `training_cert:` ⇒ not. **Anything else stored in this table must be excluded there too.** Still client-side: a server-side `.neq()` evaluates NULL for legacy rows and would drop them.
- **`BB_TRAINING_MODULES`** = foundations / doors / dialing / closing. **`BB_TRAINING_TRACKS`** maps role → modules; setter+dialer get foundations+doors+dialing, tech+rep+admin add closing. ⚠️ **An unknown or blank role falls back to the SETTER track** — the safe default is more training, never less.
- **`bbTourStepsFor(role)`** filters the authored steps; `_bbTourSteps()` is what every navigation, the progress bar and the step counter run off. ⚠️ **Never index `BB_TOUR_STEPS` directly again** — a setter's progress bar would count closing steps they are never shown.
- **Certification is per module, not one flag.** That is what makes promotion free: a setter certified on foundations+doors+dialing who becomes a Tech owes only `closing`. `team_members.training_completed` stays as the roll-up.
- **A question is a property of a step (`check:{q,options,answer,why}`), not a step kind** — so a script edit and its question cannot drift apart. Wrong answer → renders `why` → retry the same question. `_bbTourFirstTry` records first-attempt correctness, which is the coaching number; `bbTourScore()` exposes it. Next renders disabled as **"Answer to continue"**, and `bbTourNext` refuses independently so a stale render can't skip it.
- **8 questions today**, all comprehension checks on already-approved script content, deliberately covering the three corrections that reps get wrong: **there is no no-show fee**, **incentives may be alluded to but never named**, and **confirm the date AND the two-hour window out loud**.
- ⚠️ **Authored content today is `foundations:11, dialing:7` — `doors` and `closing` have ZERO steps.** The tracks and the gating are real; the content is not written yet. `test-training.js` prints the per-module counts and explicitly flags the promotion assertion as **vacuous** until module D exists, so a green tick is not mistaken for coverage.
- **`rep_agreements` has no score column** (`rep_id, rep_name, rep_legal_name, signed_at, source, agreement_version`). Pass/fail per module is free; a score visible in reporting needs one small column. Not added.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-training.js` (48 assertions) + `scratchpad/test-training-boot.js`, which **drives the real portal**. **Gated both ways.**

**Steps 4-6 (certification, hard gate, certificate) — 2026-08-11**
- **Certification is per module**, stored as `rep_agreements` rows with `source='training_cert:<mod>'` and `agreement_version=TRAINING_VERSION`. Bumping the version re-opens that module for everyone. Derived from the **same login fetch** that reads the agreement — one round trip answers both.
- ⚠️ **That fetch's `limit(10)` became `limit(50)`.** Certs now share the table, so a rep with several module certs plus a re-cert could push their actual agreement out of the window and read as unsigned.
- ⚠️ **THE LOCKOUT TRAP: a module with no authored steps must be trivially satisfied.** `doors` and `closing` have zero content, so a naive "every module in the track is certified" check locks **every rep out of Doors and Dialing permanently**. `bbModulesOutstanding` filters on `bbModuleHasContent(m)` first. Gated in `test-training-boot.js` — removing that filter reproduces "still gated after certification" exactly.
- **Hard gate** in `salesSetView`: `canvass`/`dialer` route to Training when the track is incomplete, `_bbGateNotice` records which surface they wanted so the tab can say *"Doors is locked"* rather than dumping them somewhere. The mode pills render 🔒 instead of bouncing silently. **Admin is never gated.**
- ⚠️ **The training gate sits DOWNSTREAM of the rep-agreement gate.** Without a signed agreement `loadSalesDashboard` routes to the signing screen and returns *before* it defines `salesSetView` — which is why a harness must stub a fully onboarded rep or it fails with `salesSetView is not defined` and looks like a gate bug.
- **Certificate** (`_bbTourCertify`) renders instantly off `bbTourScore()` with tiers (Perfect run / Passed clean / Certified), the modules earned, and `window.print()` behind a `body.bb-cert-open` print block. ⚠️ The DB write is **fire-and-forget** — a failed insert must never cost a rep the certificate they just earned. It also stamps `team_members.training_completed`, so the Team tab flag is now earned rather than ticked by hand.
- ⚠️ **A harness that re-defines the functions it is testing proves nothing about the shipped code.** `test-training.js` redefines the cert helpers for isolation, and reverting the real exemption did not fail it. The behavioural coverage is in `test-training-boot.js`; the static file now says so and asserts the shipped line by source.

### Live outage targeting — why it looked unbuilt, and the urgency play (2026-08-12)
Reported as *"I don't see that a feature got implemented."* The badge half existed; two things hid it.
- **Bug 1 — the "⚡ Active outages" chip row was deleted by `c78892f`.** **FOURTH thing lost to that one commit**, after the `isPinned` declarations, `canvassAddDoor` and the callback-number search. The `bbOutageHasZip` badges on the cards survived, so the feature half-worked and read as never built. ⚠️ `git show c78892f~1:portal.html | grep -c 'Active outages'` returns 1; at `c78892f`, 0.
- **Bug 2 — a broken feed and a calm night rendered identically** (both = no badges, no chips). Same family as the Regrid `no_key`, Tracerfy 429 and permit-tally entries. `outage-sync.js` also **returned 500 and wrote nothing** on a throw, so a failing feed left the last good snapshot (or no row) in place forever.
  - The catch now writes `failed_at` + `error`, **preserves the previous `zips`** so a transient blip doesn't blank the badges mid-shift, and ⚠️ **does NOT bump `synced_at`** — staleness has to stay visible.
  - `_bbOutageRowHtml(mode)` renders **four distinct states**: active outages · genuinely quiet (*"No active SDG&E outages right now · checked 5m ago"*) · errored · stale (>90 min, against a 20-min schedule) / never-synced. Reps see a neutral "not reporting" line; **only `CURRENT_ROLE === 'admin'` gets the cause** — same rule as `_aiNoteFailText`.
- **Now on BOTH motions** (it was Dialing-only): mounted in `_bbControlsRowHtml`. On Dialing a chip sets `bbDialFocusZip`; on Doors `canvassFocusOutageZip` adds an outage key to `_cvRouteCmp` so those doors float to the front. ⚠️ **Prioritise, never hide** — same rule as Focus SDCP and Focus ZIP; it only reorders.
- **Script branches on both surfaces**, gated on `bbOutageHasZip`. ⚠️ **The outage is a door-opener that WEAVES BACK INTO the normal evaluation pitch — it does not replace it** (per Dennis, correcting the first version, which told the rep to book and hang up). The arc: replace the opening line only → let them vent → **one question, "Did your solar keep anything running?"** → they answer no themselves → one plain sentence on the anti-islanding shutoff → **pivot back to the real reason for the visit** (*"that's not actually why I'm out here — the solar X put on, they're gone, nobody's been checking these"*) → then **Qualify and the Bill Branch exactly as normal**, and the free-eval ask. An outage says nothing about whether their system is healthy, so skipping qualification throws away the actual pitch.
  - ⚠️ **Battery stays the LAST RESORT here, same as every other call/door — only if THEY raise it.** Both branches also forbid quoting a battery, a price or an incentive, and forbid claiming a battery *would* have kept them on: that depends on their panel and loads and is the tech's call after the eval.
  - Gated in `test-outage.js` step [7] — reverting to the "book it and get off the phone" version fails on the missing pivot, the skipped Qualify, and the early terminate.
- ⚠️ **Still unverified against live data:** the ArcGIS field-name guesses in `outage-sync.js` (utility field, zip field) have never been confirmed — `services.arcgis.com` is blocked from the dev sandbox. The new status row is what makes this checkable: if the portal shows "not reporting" with a real reason, the schema guess is wrong. **Check it during an actual outage.**
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-outage.js` (24 assertions). **Gated both ways** — deleting the row reproduces the report, and collapsing the broken state reproduces "a dead feed looks like a quiet night".

### The walkthrough now WALKS THE PORTAL (2026-08-12, per Dennis)
*"I want the new hire tour to also walk them through the Portal… actually see the portal, a real lead, like where to type in notes for their tech."* The tour only ever highlighted whatever happened to be on screen, and a hire starts it **from the Training tab** — so every `kind:'tour'` step fell through to *"this one lives on the other tab"* and the walkthrough was, in practice, a stack of text cards.
- **A step can now name a `view`.** `_bbTourGoToView()` drives `salesSetView()` to that screen, waits a beat for the DOM, then highlights. Re-entry is guarded by `_bbTourPainting` — `salesSetView` calls `renderSales`, which would otherwise recurse.
- ⚠️ **`salesSetView` had to let the tour through the training gate** (`!window._bbTourNavigating`). Without it every portal step bounced straight back to Training and the walkthrough could never show anything. The flag is set and cleared around the one call — gated, and the harness asserts an uncertified rep is *still* bounced normally outside the tour.
- ⚠️ **A brand-new hire has ZERO leads, no route and no GPS — there is nothing to point at.** A demo lead (`__tour_demo__`, obvious filler, never a real homeowner) is injected on `bbStartTour` and removed on finish **and** on early close. Reverting it drops 16 highlights to 6.
  - ⚠️ **The Leads list needed its own injector.** `myFixmyLeads` is computed **once** after the fetch, not per render, so pushing into `adminCustomers` would never surface it — hence `window._salesInjectDemo` / `_salesRemoveDemo` inside the closure.
  - The dialer queue injection also sets `bbDialLoaded = true` so a live fetch can't replace the demo mid-tour, and resets it on cleanup so the rep never dials against tour state.
- ⚠️ **The card / disposition / AI steps run on DIALING, not Doors.** Doors hides its shell behind the "Get My Location" gate and a new hire has not granted GPS — the shell is deliberately identical on both surfaces, so it is explained where it always renders. Scoreboard + mode stay on Doors (both render above the GPS gate).
- **New authored steps:** a real lead card → opening it → **Notes, framed as how you talk to the tech who runs the appointment** → Appts → the Doors shell → the card → dispositions → AI Notes → the same page by phone. Three carry comprehension checks.
- ⚠️ **`_bbTourWasSales` was read in three places and never assigned** — a pre-existing bug meaning closing the tour never restored the view. Now set in `bbStartTour`, and close returns the hire to **Training** rather than leaving them on a screen the gate may still block.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-tour-portal.js` (14 assertions) by walking the real tour end to end as an uncertified setter. **Gated three ways** — stripping `view`, removing the demo lead, or closing the gate bypass each reproduce a distinct failure.

### "Where are the AI transcriptions being logged? I don't see anything here." (2026-08-12)
**They were always being saved.** `_cvAiStop` writes via `bbLogActivity` on Stop & Summarize, and `canvassKnock` harvests a live recording into the note it saves with the disposition — both land in `customers.notes` **and** `lead_activity`. Verified before changing anything; the save path needed no fix.
- **The bug was the door card's DISPLAY.** It rendered `_parseNotes(lead.notes).slice(-1)[0]` truncated to **60 characters** — so a multi-sentence AI summary showed as a fragment, and *any* note logged afterwards (a disposition, a typed note) hid it completely. A rep looking at the card saw `Knock — Dennis Larsen: Not Home: Gated.` and reasonably concluded nothing had been recorded.
- ⚠️ **Dialing has had a proper 4-entry feed (`bbDialFeedHtml`) the whole time.** Another Doors/Dialing asymmetry, and the reason the transcripts "looked" missing on one surface only.
- **`_cvNotesFeedHtml(lead)`** is now on all three door cards (rep route, admin canvass, admin Black Box): collapsed by default with a **`📔 Notes (N)`** header, a **`🎙 N`** badge when any entry is an AI note, and a 160-char preview of the newest; expanded it renders **the same `bbDialFeedHtml`** Dialing uses — one renderer, so the two cannot drift again.
- ⚠️ **Open state lives in `window._cvFeedOpen`, outside the DOM.** The canvass GPS watch re-renders every few seconds and would otherwise slam it shut mid-read — same rule as the note box, the email edit and the booking flow.
- ⚠️ **The AI badge matches BOTH mic glyphs.** `dialer-notes.js` prefixes its summary with **🎙 (U+1F399)** while the AI Notes button renders **🎤 (U+1F3A4)** — counting one would silently miss notes depending on which path wrote them. The badge itself now uses `&#127897;` so it matches the prefix in the note text.
- **No 60/80-char note truncation remains on any card** — asserted.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-notes-feed.js` (16 assertions). **Gated both ways** — restoring the one-line preview reproduces the report exactly.
- ⚠️ **Harness note:** the badge is emitted as an HTML entity, so a regex for the literal emoji fails against correct markup — assert on rendered `innerText`, not the raw string.

### The Session Log listed the same door twice (fixed 2026-08-12)
Reported with **2803 Morningside Trl** showing "Not Home · just now" on two rows and **3033 Via Loma** showing "Warm · 1h ago" on two rows. All three writers (`adminCanvassKnock`, `canvassKnock`, `canvassCommitBooking`) `unshift`ed blind with **no dedupe by id** — and `canvassReopen` exists *precisely* so a rep can pull a knocked door back up and re-disposition it, so this is the normal workflow, not an edge case. The older copy showed a **stale outcome** and both rendered as buttons doing the same thing.
- **`window._cvPushKnockHistory(entry)`** is now the only writer: drops any prior entry with the same id, unshifts, caps at 25. Defined in block 1 so the admin canvass and the rep route share one implementation. Exactly one raw `hist.unshift` remains in the file — inside the writer.
- ⚠️ **Dedupe is by `id`, never by address.** Two genuinely different leads on the same street (`3033 VIA LOMA VISTA, ESCONDIDO` vs `3033 VIA LOMA, SAN DIEGO`) must both survive; collapsing on address would hide a real door. **Duplicate lead *rows* are a separate problem — that is what `bbScanDuplicates`/`bbMergeDuplicates` is for.**
- **The log is "doors I worked", not an audit trail.** `lead_activity` is the audit trail and still records every disposition, so nothing is lost by keeping one row per door.
- Also fixed: a **booked** entry was written without `name` while every other entry carried it.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-session-log.js` (13 assertions). **Gated both ways** — removing the filter reproduces the duplicate exactly.
- ⚠️ **Harness note:** `about:blank` has **no localStorage** (`SecurityError`), and this writer persists there — the harness has to serve a real origin.

### A booked lead was still being worked by BOTH motions (fixed 2026-08-12)
Per Dennis: *"we want to pull them out of the Quarantined Black Box leads when they're booked."* A booking already sets `black_box:false` — but **no Black Box membership query ever filtered on it.** Membership everywhere was `black_box = true OR lead_source = 'orphaned_list'`, and a booking leaves `lead_source` alone, so a booked customer stayed in the pool.
- ⚠️ **It leaked in both directions, and the phone case was the ugly one.** A lead booked at the DOOR kept `sold_type` null and had no `dial_status`, so it sat in the dialer's **Fresh** bucket — a customer with a confirmed appointment at the top of someone's call list. A lead booked on the PHONE has no `knock_status`, so `_cvIsOpen` treated it as untouched and it came back as an **unknocked door**. The door-booked case was partly masked by `knock_status='booked'`, which is why only half of it was ever visible.
- Fixed in **five** places — the dialer's `dialableAnd`, its two pre-migration BASE fallbacks, `_canvassFetchBox`, the rep no-GPS fallback, and `adminCanvassInit`'s raw-param query — all now carry `or(black_box.is.null,black_box.eq.true)`.
- ⚠️ **`is.null` must stay in that clause.** A large share of the pool predates the column; testing `black_box.eq.true` alone would silently empty the queue. Gated — the harness asserts a null-`black_box` legacy lead is still served.
- ⚠️ **The raw-param queries take ONE `and=(…)`, not two `&or=` params.** `adminCanvassInit` had `&or=(archived…)`; a second top-level `&or=` is not a form proven anywhere in this file, so both conditions were folded into a single `and=(or(…),or(…))` — the shape the dialer query has always used.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-booked-exit.js` (13 assertions). It evaluates the predicate as **logic** against six lead shapes rather than string-matching it, so a rewrite that preserves behaviour still passes. **Gated both ways.**
- ⚠️ **Harness trap, second instance:** anchoring on `/_canvassFetchBox[\s\S]{0,900}?\.limit\(1000\)/` failed against perfectly correct code because the function is longer than the budget. **Scope a source assertion to the statement under test** (`client.from('customers').select(SEL)…limit(1000);`), never to the enclosing function by character count.

### Setter → closer routing on a booking (2026-08-12, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
**This did not exist before.** Both surfaces assigned the booked lead to whoever booked it (`rep_id = person.id`), so a Setter's booking sat on the *Setter's* portal and a human had to reassign it. There was no notion of a closer anywhere in the code.
- **The rule, in Dennis's words:** *"If setter is also a Tech they would get it. If the setter is not a Tech then it would go to Dennis."* Everyone is onboarded as a **Setter**; an admin grants the **additional** role of Tech (closer). `bbCloserForBooking(person)` → `person.id` when they can close, else `DEFAULT_CLOSER_ID` (`tech4`, Dennis). Applied identically in `canvassCommitBooking` (Doors) and `bbDialCommit` (Dialing).
- ⚠️ **`setter_name` always records who actually booked it**, never the closer — that is what `edRecordFixmySplit` reads for the 20% setter / 20% closer split, and what the setter's own list matches on.
- ⚠️ **The Doors booking never stamped `setter_name` at all** (the dialer always has). A door-booked deal therefore paid the closer the full 40% and the rep who knocked the door **nothing**. Fixed in the same pass — money logic that existed on only one of the two motions, exactly the standing-rule failure.
- **Storage is `pipeline_state` key `closers` (`{ids:[], names:[]}`), NOT a `team_members` column.** ⚠️ PostgREST **400s the whole query on one unknown column**, several migrations in this repo have sat unapplied for weeks, and a `team_members` select that 400s takes the Team tab down for everyone. This needs no migration and works the moment it deploys. Both id **and** lowercased name are stored so a rep whose id changes still resolves.
- **Day-one fallback:** with no saved row, `CLOSER_SEED_NAMES` covers Dennis Larsen, Christina/Cristina Huang and Christina/Cristina Hackman (**both spellings on purpose** — `TECHS` says "Cristina", Dennis writes "Christina"). The Team panel shows an amber "using the built-in default" line until an admin saves once.
- ⚠️ **`bbSaveClosers` always re-adds `DEFAULT_CLOSER_ID`.** Dennis is the fallback target for every non-closer booking; if he could be unticked, those leads would route to a portal that cannot work them. Gated.
- **Admin control:** a "🎯 Closing (Tech) enabled" panel in Team → Team, checkbox per active rep/tech/setter/dialer, Dennis's box disabled and marked "(always)". A failed save renders "⚠ Not saved: …" — never a success message for a write that did not happen.
- **Setter keeps read-only sight** (per Dennis): `loadSalesDashboard` runs a second query (`setter_name = person.name` and `rep_id != person.id`) **only for non-closers**, rendered as a **"🤝 Booked by me"** section under Leads — name, address, appointment, who is closing it. Deliberately **not** editable: two people editing one appointment is how a customer gets called twice with two different times. The dialer's post-booking editor jump is skipped on a handed-off lead (it would silently no-op, since the lead is not in that rep's `allRecords`) and replaced with a plain confirmation.
- ⚠️ **Only FOUR places may set `black_box:false`** — the two booking commits, the admin activate button, and Doors' "Save to My Leads". `test-warm-booked.js` asserts all four **by name**; an earlier version counted lines and misfired twice (a per-line filter counted the two-line `Object.assign` mirror as a fifth, and stripping `Object.assign` statements swallowed a real one). **Count nothing structural by line.**
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-closer-routing.js` (19 assertions) driving both surfaces. **Gated both ways.**

### Warm stays in Black Box; a booking that silently didn't save (2026-08-12, per Dennis)
Two field reports with opposite symptoms, two different causes.
- **A warm door landed in Admin → Leads.** By design at the time: `canvassKnock` wrote `{step:1, lead_category:'fixmy', black_box:false}` on `warm`/`interested`. **Per Dennis this is wrong — a warm door stays in Black Box / Quarantined until it is BOOKED.** Removed from `canvassKnock` *and* from `adminCanvassKnock`, which carried its own copy and would have let the bug straight back in from the admin card. ⚠️ **Dialing was already right** — `bbDialCommit` only activates on `booked` — so Doors was the outlier and this is now parity, not a new rule. `rep_id` is still stamped so the rep keeps ownership. Nothing else was needed to retire the door: `_cvIsOpen` already excludes any `knock_status` other than `come_back`.
- ⚠️ **Only FOUR places may set `black_box:false`**: the door booking commit, the dialer booking commit, the admin "activate" button, and Doors' explicit "Save to My Leads". The harness counts them. If a fifth appears, something is activating leads that shouldn't.
- **"Booked at the door, never showed up in Leads."** `canvassCommitBooking` ended with:
  ```js
  try { await c.from('customers').update({…}).eq('id', lead.id); } catch(e) {}
  ```
  ⚠️ **supabase-js RETURNS `{error}` — it does not throw** — so a rejected write was invisible twice over: the `catch` was empty *and* nothing read `error`. The rep saw the card disappear, the note logged as "booked", the route re-sort — and nothing reached the database. **The GHL call was checked meticulously while the write that actually creates the lead was not checked at all.**
  - Now `.select('id')` + an explicit `error` check, and **a write matching ZERO rows counts as a failure** (wrong id, or RLS filtering the row out) — "succeeding" on zero rows is exactly how this hides.
