  - On failure it does **not** clear `_cvBk`, **does not** log a `booked` activity row, and **does not** drop the door from the route; the rep gets the real reason and a retry. `_cvBk.ghlOk` makes the retry skip the calendar so it can't double-book.
  - ⚠️ The error must not depend on `#cvBkStatus`, which only exists on step 3 — it falls back to `alert()`. Same trap as `uploadPhotos()` and `#photoStatus`.
- **The diagnostic fingerprint**, for finding past instances: a `lead_activity` row with `outcome='booked'` whose `customers` row still has `black_box = true`. That combination is only producible by this bug.
  ```sql
  select c.id, c.first_name, c.last_name, c.address, c.black_box, c.knock_status, a.created_at
  from lead_activity a join customers c on c.id = a.customer_id
  where a.outcome = 'booked' and c.black_box is true order by a.created_at desc;
  ```
- ⚠️ **A second, older explanation for the same symptom**, fixed the same day: before build `2026-08-12b` a GPS tick rebuilt `#editorSlot` empty mid-booking, so a rep part-way through the 3-step flow could have it vanish with nothing written. Both causes produce "I booked it and it isn't there"; only this one leaves a `booked` activity row behind, which is how to tell them apart.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-warm-booked.js` (18 assertions). **Gated both ways** — restoring the warm activation reproduces the Rios report, and re-swallowing the error reproduces the Wilton fingerprint exactly.

### SDCP badge audit (2026-08-12) — the report was wrong, the audit found two real bugs
Reported from a door at **1136 Via Felicidad, Escondido 92029**: *"this ZIP is SDCP and there's no badge."* **The missing badge was correct.** SDCP's member agencies are the cities of **San Diego, Chula Vista, Encinitas, Imperial Beach, La Mesa, National City** plus the **unincorporated county** (enrolled April 2023). **Escondido is Clean Energy Alliance**, along with Carlsbad, Del Mar, Solana Beach, San Marcos (Apr 2023) and Oceanside/Vista (Apr 2024). Poway, Santee, El Cajon, Lemon Grove and Coronado have no CCA. ⚠️ **Do not add a North County city to `SDCP_ZIPS`** — that is CEA territory and the two are different providers.
- **Bug 1 — there were TWO `SDCP_ZIPS`.** A second identical 69-zip `Set` inside the admin Black Box renderer **shadowed** the module-level one. Byte-identical at the time, with nothing keeping them so. Deleted; there is now exactly one, asserted in the harness.
- **Bug 2 — the whole north/central unincorporated county was missing.** The list only ever carried the east/south unincorporated zips (Alpine, Jamul, Campo, Spring Valley…). **Fallbrook 92028, Lakeside 92040, Ramona 92065, Valley Center 92082** — all genuinely SDCP, all real door territory, all showing no badge. Added with Bonsall 92003, Borrego Springs 92004, Julian 92036, Pala 92059, Palomar Mtn 92060, Pauma Valley 92061, Ranchita 92066, Santa Ysabel 92070, Warner Springs 92086, Jacumba 91934.
- **`SDCP_MIXED_ZIPS` + an "SDCP?" badge.** A zip cannot resolve CCA membership when it straddles a boundary: **92029** is City of Escondido (CEA) *plus* unincorporated Del Dios / Elfin Forest / Harmony Grove (SDCP); 92026/92027 the same; 92019/92021 are El Cajon (no CCA) plus unincorporated Rancho San Diego / Crest. These render an amber **SDCP?** badge whose tooltip says the postal city does not settle it — so the ambiguity is visible instead of looking like a flat "no". Same principle as the Regrid `no_key` rule: never let an unknown render identically to a real negative.
- ⚠️ **A mixed zip must NEVER reach the money or the route.** `SDCP_MIXED_ZIPS` is deliberately a separate set, and the proposal builder's `build.sdcpRebate`, the script tips and the Focus SDCP sort all still test `SDCP_ZIPS.has()` (strict). Quietly applying a rebate to an address that may not qualify is far worse than showing no badge. Gated — promoting 92029 into the strict set fails 4 assertions.
- **`window.sdcpZipStatus(zip)` / `window.sdcpBadgeHtml(zip)`** are now the only way a card decides this; all five surfaces (admin canvass door, admin Black Box chip, dialer call card, rep Doors card, unified lead card) go through them.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-sdcp.js` (14 assertions). **Gated both ways.**

### Manual time override on a door booking (2026-08-12, per Dennis)
*"I need to make the Booked times on Doors more custom."* Step 1 of `canvassStartBooking` offered six fixed 2-hour windows and nothing else, so "I get home at 5:45" or "give me an hour, not two" could not be expressed — the rep wrote down a time the homeowner never agreed to. **Not a new feature: Dialing has had free entry (`bbBookToggleManual`) since it was built.** This is Doors catching up, so the standing "Doors, Dialing, or both?" rule is satisfied by parity rather than by a new build on one side.
- **⚙ Pick an exact time** swaps the preset grid for a `type="time"` start plus a **window length** (1 / 1½ / 2 / 3 hr, `CV_BK_LENGTHS`). Presets remain the default and still mean two hours.
- ⚠️ **The window length is no longer a constant, and three places had to agree.** It is read out loud at confirm (`_cvBkApptLabel`), written to `arrival_end`, and sent to GHL as `endISO` — all now go through **`_cvBkMins()`**. Gated: restoring either hardcoded `2 * 3600000` makes the card say "5:45pm–6:45pm" while GHL receives `17:45→19:45`. **A booking that reads one way and books another is worse than no override at all.**
- ⚠️ **Leaving custom mode drops a non-preset start.** Keeping 17:45 selected while the grid highlights nothing reads as "no time picked" and would book 5:45 anyway. The length resets to 120 with it.
- **A start in the past blocks Next** with a plain reason. Applies to presets too — tapping 8–10am at 2pm was previously bookable.
- ⚠️ **`_cvBk` is now in `_cvUiBusy()`, and this fixed a real pre-existing bug.** The booking renders into `#editorSlot`, which `renderSales()` rebuilds **empty** on every GPS tick — so three steps, a typed time, six confirm ticks and photo uploads could vanish mid-flow with `_cvBk` still set. Same family as the note box and email edit. `renderSales` also repaints an open booking from state at the end, since plenty of other things call it.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-book-time.js` (20 assertions) by driving the real Doors view. **Gated both ways on all four changes independently.**
- ⚠️ **Harness note:** `_cvBk` is closure-private by design — assert through what the rep can see (rendered label, button styling, disabled state), not by reaching into it. Reaching in fails with `ReferenceError` and tempts you to export state purely for the test.

### A door booking could complete with no phone number (fixed 2026-08-11)
Reported with a real lead: booked at the door, activated into the pipeline, name and email present, **phone blank**. Black Box leads usually arrive with no phone — the door *is* where you get it — and the confirm step rendered the script line with a placeholder:
```js
phone: lead.phone || '(no phone yet)'
```
So the rep read *"I have **(no phone yet)** — is that the best number to reach you?"* with a **✓ Confirmed** button underneath, ticked it, and booked an appointment nobody could be called about. ⚠️ **`allOk` counted confirmations, never values** — that is the whole bug.
- **`CV_CONFIRM_ITEMS` entries now carry `required:true`** (name + phone). `_cvBkValueOf` / `_cvBkMissing` are the one place that decides whether a field actually holds something: a blank, a `@pending.fixmy.energy` placeholder email, or a phone that isn't 10 clean digits all count as missing.
- A missing required field renders **"＋ Add phone"** (amber) instead of "✓ Confirmed", **Next stays disabled**, and a line names what is still outstanding. `canvassBkConfirm` also re-routes to the prompt rather than ticking a blank, so a stale render can't slip past.
- The Fix prompt refuses a malformed number and stores clean digits — same rule as `bbSavePhone`.
- ⚠️ **Email is deliberately NOT required.** A door rep who can't get an email must still be able to book; the `@pending.fixmy.energy` placeholder exists for exactly that. Only name and phone block.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-book-phone.js` (18 assertions). **Gated both ways** — neutering `_cvBkMissing` reproduces the report exactly, including the blank phone being confirmable.
- ⚠️ **Harness note:** `"(619) 555-0100 ext"` strips to exactly 10 digits and *is* valid — the real malformed case is an extension carrying digits (`x1234`). A test case that looks wrong but isn't will fail against correct code.

### The REAL cause of "Loading dial queue… forever" — a ReferenceError, not the network (fixed 2026-08-11)
⚠️ **The network-timeout entry below was a wrong diagnosis.** It is a genuine hardening fix and stays, but it was never the cause. The dialer was broken by the **"one shared page shell" refactor (`c78892f`, 2026-08-08)**, which replaced the counters block by line range and **silently swallowed eight declarations** along with the empty-state early return:
```js
var list = buckets[bbDialFilter] || [];
var pinnedLead = bbDialResolveCurrentLead(buckets);
var isPinned = !!(bbDialPinnedId && pinnedLead);
if (!isPinned && !list.length) { …empty state…; return; }
var lead = isPinned ? pinnedLead : list[0];
var _nm, name, ph, phDisplay, ksLabel, cbDue…
```
Everything below reads them, so **`renderBBDialerView` threw `ReferenceError: isPinned is not defined` on every render** — *after* the queue had already loaded successfully.
- ⚠️ **The throw was completely silent.** `dashBody` still held the previous `"Loading dial queue…"` markup, so a ReferenceError looked exactly like a slow network. `bbDialLoaded` was `true` and `bbDialLoading` `false` the whole time — the data was there, the screen just never got redrawn.
- **Second silent deletion from the same commit** — CLAUDE.md already records `canvassAddDoor` being lost the same way. ⚠️ **Never replace a block in `portal.html` by line range.** Anchor on unique markers, and afterwards check that every identifier the surrounding code reads still has a declaration.

#### …and a THIRD: the callback-number lookup (fixed 2026-08-12)
Reported as *"the phone number lookup feature was removed from Dialing options."* It was — `c78892f` cut the search block sitting immediately above the declarations it also deleted. `git show c78892f~1:portal.html | grep -c 'id="bbDialSearchInput"'` returns **1**; at `c78892f` it returns **0**.
- ⚠️ **Every function behind it survived** — `bbDialSearchPhone`, `bbDialFetchByPhone`, `bbDialPinLead`, `bbDialSelectResult`, `bbDialEnsureInQueue`, `bbDialUnpin` were all still defined and still referenced each other. Only the markup that *reaches* them was gone, so the feature went dark while grepping for its names found everything and the page threw **no error**. **A function being defined is not evidence that it is reachable — check for a call site in emitted markup, not just a definition.**
- **What it does, and why it matters:** the rep types the number that just called back and gets that lead's card, pinned above queue order (`📌 Pinned from search` + `← Back to queue`). It matches the loaded queue first with no round trip, then falls back to `phone=like.*<10 digits>` against `customers` — so a callback from a lead **outside the current bucket or not loaded at all** still opens. A single hit auto-pins; multiple hits render a chooser. `bbDialEnsureInQueue` folds an externally-found lead into `bbDialQueue` **before** pinning, so dispositions and `bbLogActivity` find it by normal id lookup and append to its existing note history rather than overwriting it.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-dial-search.js` (18 assertions) by booting the real dialer. **Gated both ways** — deleting the block reproduces the report exactly: no field, no button, and no page error.
- ⚠️ **Harness traps hit here, both new:** (1) the stub routed `/rest/v1/` on a bare `phone=`, but the *queue* load also carries `phone=not.is.null`, so the queue came back empty and the local-match assertions failed against perfectly good code — route on `phone=like.` instead. (2) `button:has-text("Find")` also matches the owner card's **"🔍 Find Owner"** as soon as a lead with no owner is pinned; target `button[onclick="bbDialSearchPhone()"]` — **match the handler, not the label.**
- **`renderBBDialerView` is now wrapped in a try/catch** that paints the error plus a "↻ Reload the queue" button. A render failure must never be indistinguishable from a slow load again.
- **How it was finally found:** by *booting the real view* in Chromium (`scratchpad/test-dialer-boot.js`) instead of reasoning about the source. Three earlier diagnoses — network stall, scope error, cached deploy — were all wrong because the page was only ever *loaded*, never *driven*. ⚠️ **Loading a page proves nothing about a view you have not opened.** The pageerror appeared within one second of actually entering the dialer.
- Verified: queue clears in <1s, real card renders, zero page errors. **Gated both ways** — removing the restored declarations reproduces `ReferenceError: isPinned is not defined` exactly.
- ⚠️ **Harness trap, fourth instance:** the card renders `NEXT CALL` (`text-transform:uppercase`), so a case-sensitive `/Next Call/` against `innerText` failed with the code perfectly correct. **Any assertion against innerText of styled text must be case-insensitive.**

### "Loading dial queue… / Loading your week…" forever (fixed 2026-08-11)
Reported from an iPad on **LTE**: the Dialing view stuck on both placeholders with no error. **Not a regression** — `git diff HEAD~3 -- portal.html` showed neither loader had been touched. The cause is that **neither `bbDialLoadQueue()` nor `bbLoadRepScore()` had a network timeout**, while `loadAdminDashboard` has raced a 12s one since forever.
- ⚠️ **A stalled request neither resolves NOR rejects.** Both loaders already had `.catch` handlers, so a *thrown* error recovered fine — a **hang** is not a throw, so nothing fired. `try/catch/finally` cannot cover this; only a race can.
- ⚠️ **Then the guard latches.** `_bbScoreLoading` / `bbDialLoading` are set *before* the await and cleared *after*, so a hang leaves them `true` and `if (_bbScoreLoading) return _bbScore;` makes **every later attempt return null immediately** — it can never recover, even once signal returns. Only a full page reload escapes. Same family as the `_bbEnrichRunning` stuck-flag bug (2026-08-02), except a hang rather than a throw.
- **Fix:** `_bbWithTimeout(p, what, ms)` + `BB_NET_TIMEOUT_MS` (15s). Both loaders are raced; `_bbScoreLoading` is released in a **`finally`**; the dialer sets `bbDialLoadError` and renders a red "connection timed out · **↻ Try again**" panel; the scoreboard renders its own failure state with a Retry.
- ⚠️ **`out.netFail` exists so a dead connection and a genuine zero week don't render identically** — the same "never let a failing path look like a real negative" rule as the Regrid `no_key` and Tracerfy 429 entries.
- **Field workaround while a rep is stuck:** hard-reload the page; that clears the latched flags.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-stuck-loading.js` (14 assertions) against a supabase stub that never settles. **Gated both ways** — removing the race makes the *harness itself* hang forever, which is precisely the report.
- ⚠️ **Harness trap:** the first version omitted `SUPA_URL`/`SUPA_KEY` stubs, so `createClient` threw *before* any query and the hang test "passed" in 0 ms without the race ever running. The timing assertion now requires it to actually **wait** for the timeout — a pass that arrives too fast is a failed test.

### AI notes lost on disposition — the 2026-07-29 fix only covered the button (fixed 2026-08-11)
Reported: tap 🎙 AI Notes, have a real conversation, tap a disposition — **the note is gone.** The 2026-07-29 entry above says the summary is auto-saved on Stop, and it is; the hole is that **`_bbAiStop()`/`_cvAiStop()` were only ever reachable from the "Stop & Summarize" button.** A rep who dispositions instead — which is how the tool is actually used, not a mistake — left the transcript sitting in `_bbAiFinal`, and the **next** recording's `_bbAiFinal = ''` destroyed it. Nothing in `bbDialCommit` or `canvassKnock` touched the AI state at all.
- **`_bbAiHarvest()` / `_cvAiHarvest()`** stop recognition, return the transcript, and clear state. **Every write path harvests first** — `bbDialCommit` and `canvassKnock` now call it before building their note, so the conversation is written *with* the outcome.
- ⚠️ **The raw transcript is ALWAYS kept.** `_aiNoteFromHarvest` summarizes if the API answers within `AI_SUMMARY_WAIT_MS` (8s) and otherwise saves the raw words prefixed "🎙 Transcript (AI summary unavailable)". An unsummarized note beats a lost call, and this is the assertion that matters most in the harness.
- ⚠️ **The timeout is a `Promise.race`, not just an `AbortController`.** Abort only helps if the transport honours the signal; a hung request that ignores it would park a rep on a spinner mid-shift. The budget has to be a guarantee. (Caught by the harness — the first version relied on abort alone and blocked for 12s.)
- **`_bbAiLeadId` binds a recording to the lead it started on** (Doors already had `_cvAiLeadId`). If the queue moved and the harvest belongs to a *different* lead than the one being dispositioned, the note is written to **the lead it was recorded on** rather than mislabelling another customer or being dropped.
- **Both Stop functions were rebuilt on the same harvest + `_aiSummarize`.** There is now exactly **one** call site for `dialer-notes` — two separate save routines is precisely how this feature lost notes in the first place. Don't add a second.
- **An unsaved typed door note is rescued too** — `canvassKnock` folds `_cvNoteDraft[id]` in, since a rep who typed instead of pressing Save Note lost it the same way.
- **Rep-facing wording** now states the guarantee while recording: *"● recording — saved automatically when you disposition"* (call) / *"…when you pick an outcome"* (door).
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-ainote-save.js` (21 assertions, running the real helper block out of portal.html). **Gated both ways** — reverting the `bbDialCommit` harvest reproduces the report, and dropping the raw-transcript fallback loses the words whenever the AI is down or slow.
- ⚠️ **Harness trap:** a bare `/dialer-notes/` search counts *comments* and reported 3 call sites when there is one. Count the actual `fetch(` call.

### DQ'd disposition + commercial parcels (2026-08-10, per Dennis)
Reported from the field: commercial buildings appearing in the Doors route (`17210 Bernardo Center Dr` — an office block, owner `Caroline Properties LLC`), and no way to permanently remove a property that can't be worked. Per the standing rule, clarified first — **both Doors and Dialing**, and **rep-confirmed rather than auto-filtered**.
- **`disqualified` is one disposition on both surfaces** (`CV_DOOR_OUTCOMES` + `BB_DIAL_OUTCOMES`, same key, same icon, same wording; added to `BB_DIAL_WORKED`).
- ⚠️ **DQ writes `archived = true`, and that is the whole mechanism.** It is the only existing column that removes a lead from *both* motions — the dialer has always filtered `archived`, and the three canvass queries (`_canvassFetchBox`, the no-GPS fallback, `adminCanvassInit`) now do too. **No migration.** The record stays restorable from Admin → 🗄 Archived.
  - **Deliberately NOT `dnc`** — that is a phone-consent flag a door rep must never be able to set (see the existing rule above), and a property being the wrong *type* is not a homeowner asking us to stop calling. **Deliberately NOT `black_box:false`** — that means "activated into the pipeline".
- **A DQ demands a reason before it writes anything** (`_cvDqPickerHtml` → `canvassSaveDq`), same "no silent write" gate as Come Back's time picker: a door removed from every future route with no recorded reason is indistinguishable from one lost to a bug. Reasons: commercial / no solar / apartment-condo / mobile home / vacant / other. The reason lands in the shared note feed via `bbLogActivity`. The picker renders **from `_cvDqFor` state**, so a GPS tick can't wipe it — same rule as the note box.
- **`bbScanCommercial()` — admin bulk pass**, scan → preview → confirm, same shape as Merge Duplicates. Strong signals are pre-ticked, soft ones are not, and **nothing changes until the admin presses the button.**
- ⚠️ **Bare `LLC`, `TRUST` and `INC` are NOT signals and must never become ones.** ~17% of doors are landlord-owned (1,524 measured rentals) and landlords hold property in exactly those wrappers — filtering on them would hide real homeowners, including the trust-owned leads the `_bbOwnerParse`/`_bbTrusteeNote` work exists to serve. Strong signals are: APN `77x` (mobile home) / `76x` (possessory interest), a suite/unit number in the address, and owner names containing PLAZA/COMMERCE/STORAGE/HOTEL/CHURCH/CITY OF/HOMEOWNERS ASSOCIATION/APARTMENTS. `PROPERTIES|INVESTMENTS|HOLDINGS|PARTNERS|MANAGEMENT` is **soft only** — the reported parcel matches on that and is surfaced unticked.
- **There is still no land-use data.** The SanGIS parcel file carries `ASR_LANDUSE`/`NUCLEUS_USE_CD` but the import took only owner + APN, so this is a heuristic by necessity. Importing land use is the real fix and remains open (it would also need the county drive re-run).
- The bulk apply groups by reason and chunks `.in('id', …)` at 200; the per-lead reason isn't written to a column (there isn't one) — a summary lands in `pipeline_state.bb_commercial_dq_last`.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-dq.js` (34 assertions). **Gated both ways** — removing the route's `archived` filter reproduces the "DQ'd door comes back" bug, and promoting bare `LLC` to a strong signal reproduces exactly the false-positive that would hide landlord doors.
- ⚠️ **Harness trap:** an assertion scoped `[\s\S]{0,900}` around `_canvassFetchBox` failed with the code perfectly correct, because the function is longer than the window. **Scope a source assertion to the statement under test, never to a whole function by character budget.**

### Today's Activity dashboard removed (2026-08-08, per Dennis)
*"Remove today's activity and anything job or commissions related. This has evolved now and no longer relevant."* The rep home opened with a FixMy/Solar/Top Tier/Jobs/Commissions tile grid, a 20-knock ring and a day-streak — a third dashboard above the Black Box tabs, from an earlier shape of the business. Deleted along with the `totalEstComm` roll-up that fed it (nothing else read it).
- **The shared Doors/Dialing scoreboard is now the one dashboard**, and it sits on the surface where the work happens rather than on a home screen a rep passes through.
- **The Doors scoreboard now renders above the GPS gate**, so it shows before a rep taps Get My Location — previously it lived inside the route-ready branch and was invisible until GPS was on.
- ⚠️ **Left deliberately:** the Finance tab's 1099 report still has a Commissions column, and the Jobs tab still exists. Both are admin surfaces, not the rep home this removal was about — say so explicitly before removing either.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-home-cleanup.js`. ⚠️ Two assertion traps caught here: a bare `/>Commissions</` search hits the Finance report and must be scoped to the rep-home markup, and comparing `data-tour="mode"` positions matches the **dialer's** anchor first because it is in the earlier script block — cross-render-function ordering checks are meaningless.

### New Hire Walkthrough — training module + portal tour in one (2026-08-08)
Built as ONE feature rather than two, because a new hire learning the pitch and learning the screen is the same fifteen minutes. `BB_TOUR_STEPS` (portal.html) is 18 numbered steps of three kinds:
- **`read`** — why anyone talks to us, and the four standing rules (never talk down the old installer, never sell, never quote a price, confirm date + window out loud).
- **`script`** — renders through `bbScriptBodyHtml` / `cvScriptBodyHtml`, **the same functions the live panel and the one-sheet use**. ⚠️ This is the point: training cannot teach a stale script. Editing the Bill Branch changes the training module in the same edit.
- **`tour`** — highlights a REAL element on the page (`.bb-tour-spot` = green ring + `0 0 0 9999px` scrim cut-out) and explains it in place, so the hire learns the actual screen rather than a picture of it. Anchored via `data-tour="mode|counters|controls|map|card"` on the shared shell rows plus `#bbScoreboard`.
- ⚠️ **A tour step whose element is absent still renders**, as a plain card with "This one lives on the other tab" — a rep on Dialing hitting a Doors step must not get a highlight pointing at nothing.
- Progress is per-rep in `localStorage` (`bbTourDone_<id>` + `_at`), so a hire can stop and resume; the Training tab offers Start / Resume / Replay / Start over, and an admin can replay it to see exactly what a hire sees.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-tour.js`: all three step kinds present, the Bill Branch step renders the live three-beat script, the highlight lands on the real element and clears on close, a missing element still explains itself, progress saves and resumes at the right step, and finishing clears the marker.
- Also removed in this pass: the dialer's legacy "Dials Today / Contacts / Booked" strip, a second differently-styled copy of numbers the shared scoreboard already shows directly above it.

### Doors/Dialing symmetry pass 3 — one shared page shell (2026-08-08, per Dennis)
*"The pages still look so different. Can we mimic them identically (other than the specific nuances of the action)?"* The remaining problem was **layout**, not content: different control placement, different counter styling, different order. Every row of both pages is now ONE renderer, called in the same order, so the two cannot drift apart again:
```
header (title + secondary actions top-right)
_bbRepScoreboardHtml   — identical both modes, compact by default
_bbModeToggleHtml      — 🚪 Doors | 📞 Dialing
_bbCountersHtml        — Doors: Remaining/Knocked/Interested · Dialing: Fresh/Callbacks/Worked
_bbControlsRowHtml     — focus LEFT, script + one-sheet RIGHT
_bbMapBlockHtml        — open by default, remembered
the card, then everything else
```
- **`_bbFocusControlHtml(mode)` finally unifies the focus controls** (the outstanding task): Dialing gets the ZIP input, Doors gets an SDCP checkbox, same row and same position. Both still **prioritise and never hide**.
- **`_bbCountersHtml(tiles)`** renders the same tile either way; Dialing's are `<button>`s because there they are also the queue filter — the one legitimate behavioural difference, and the test asserts the container markup is otherwise identical.
- **Scoreboard is compact by default** (`localStorage.bbScoreOpen`), showing only hours-vs-30h plus a one-line summary; tapping reveals the day bars and the four input tiles. It was the tallest element on the page and sat above everything on every load.
- **Secondary actions moved to the header's top-right** on both — Doors' `+ Door`, Dialing's admin GHL sync.
- ⚠️ **The Doors SDCP block was inside an IIFE.** Cutting it out by line range left an orphaned `})();` and silently deleted `canvassAddDoor` — the parse check caught the orphan, but only because it happened to break syntax. **When replacing a block in `portal.html`, check whether it is wrapped in an IIFE before slicing by markers.**
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-shell.js`: all six rows are shared renderers, counters are structurally identical clickable or not, focus is SDCP on Doors and ZIP on Dialing with script controls to the right of both, the map blocks are identical apart from container id / toggle fn / rep-marker colour (which must differ, since the pins do), and the scoreboard is compact by default and expands.

### Doors/Dialing symmetry pass 2 — script controls + one rep scoreboard (2026-08-08)
Reported as "they're both very different feels" and "the dashboards are very different."
- **Script controls were the same two buttons in two visual languages and two places:** Dialing had a sliding switch buried at the tail of the Focus-ZIP row, Doors had a pill button up in the title row. `_bbScriptControlsHtml(which)` is now the single renderer (Script on/off + 📄 One sheet), mounted in the **header row on both**. `_bbScriptToggleHtml` and `_cvScriptToggleHtml` are **deleted, not hidden** — a surviving per-surface renderer is how these drift apart again. `bbScriptSetEnabled` now accepts an explicit boolean (it previously took a click event and toggled), and both setters repaint the shared strip via `outerHTML`.
- **`_bbRepScoreboardHtml(mode)` replaces the two divergent stat strips.** Per Dennis: *"Doors or Dialing is the same metric to me. 30 hrs is the goal a week."* One component, byte-identical on both surfaces (the test asserts that), showing weekly hours against `WEEKLY_HOURS_TARGET` with a day-by-day Mon–Sun bar, plus four **input** tiles — Doors / Dials / Convos / Booked — counting **both motions together**.
  - **Deliberately input-led.** Hours, knocks, dials and conversations are what a rep controls hour to hour; bookings are shown because they are the point but are *not* what the gauge measures, so a strong-input/slow-outcome week reads as such instead of just looking bad.
  - A "conversation" is any outcome where a human actually engaged (`warm`/`booked`/`not_interested`/`come_back`/`renter`/`already_customer`), counted identically at a door or on a phone.
  - ⚠️ The 16h forgotten-clock-out guard judges the **full** shift before any clipping — same trap as the Dialers dashboard.
  - The Doors route tiles (Remaining/Knocked/Interested) **stay**: they describe the current route, not the week, and the scoreboard sits above them.
  - Degrades without `rep_shifts`: hours show an amber line naming `20260803_setter_database.sql` while the activity counts stay real. Cached 2 min (`BB_SCORE_TTL`) and repainted DOM-locally so a GPS tick can never wipe a typed note.
- **The SolarEdge/Enphase warning was REMOVED (2026-08-08, per Dennis — supersedes the earlier note).** Partnerships **do** exist through the installers Solar Review works with. The script now says so concretely — *"we work through SolarEdge- and Enphase-certified installers, so a warranty claim actually gets filed and the labour actually gets done"* — which is both accurate and more specific than "in conjunction with."
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-scoreboard.js`: the two strips are structurally identical, both old toggle renderers are gone, the week counts both motions and excludes other reps, a 20h shift is dropped whole, the scoreboard is identical across surfaces, and a missing `rep_shifts` keeps the input counts.

### Door approach starts the tech work; incentives may be alluded to only (2026-08-09, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Two decisions after reviewing the Aug 8 AI notes:
1. **The door visit now begins the evaluation, not just books it.** New door-script stage **`7 · Capture On Site`** teaches gathering **electrical photos** (inverter, serial/data plate, MSP, meter, front of house), **production** (a screenshot from mySolarEdge / Enphase-Enlighten) and **consumption** (a bill photo — the true-up page is worth more than the monthly). It points at the existing `✅ BOOKED!` flow, whose last step is `CV_BOOK_PHOTOS` and already labels each item and files it against the lead. ⚠️ Carries a hard consent rule: **never enter a garage, side yard or roof unless invited**; if the homeowner hesitates, stop and book.
2. **Incentives: allude, never specify.** The previous rule was a blanket ban on mentioning them at all, which the field contradicted (the Aug 8 door named a 30% credit, CCA incentives and a $3–4K figure). The rule on **both** scripts is now: you may say incentives **exist**; you may **not** name a programme, a percentage or a dollar figure, and you may **never** say they are guaranteed. Anything specific comes out of the evaluation, in writing.

⚠️ **The three-beat reframe had only landed on the CALL script.** The door script was still running the old battery-pitch Bill Branch — found while making the above change, three weeks of "the scripts match" being untrue. Both now run: utility's problem → what it costs them → the small free step. **When editing one script, check the other; `test-onesheet.js` now asserts the door script's beats, capture stage and incentive rule independently.**

### Bill Branch reframed — free check-up, not a battery pitch (2026-08-08, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
*"I do not want the script to feel like we're selling batteries. I want it to feel like we're going out to check to see if they have any areas of improvement with little to no upfront costs to do so."* The old Bill Branch opened the under-$50 case by proposing storage, which is a product pitch to someone with no problem. Rebuilt around a fixed three-beat arc that **both** bill branches share:
1. **The utility's problem** — peak hours 4–9pm, which is why the credit rules keep changing. Runs first *regardless of their bill*, because it is about SDG&E rather than the homeowner, so nobody gets defensive.
2. **What that costs THEM if nothing changes** — the only branch-specific beat. Under $50: nothing is wrong today, name the *direction of travel* and the fact nobody is watching since the installer folded. Over $50: you bought the system so you wouldn't have the bill and now you have both; plus true-up landing once a year as one big March number.
3. **The small next step** — a free hour, real production numbers next to the real bill, no commitment, "if it's all healthy he'll tell you that." This is the actual ask and it is the whole pitch.
- **Battery is demoted to reactive.** It now lives under an "Only if THEY raise it" heading with a warning that volunteering a product is what turns a free check-up into a sales call. The stage also opens with a red warning: you are not selling a battery, panel or inverter on this call.
- ⚠️ **Two harness traps hit while building this, both worth remembering.**
  1. **An unsubstituted template placeholder still parses.** Writing `" + D + "` inside a Python triple-quoted string produced the literal characters in the JS string — valid JavaScript that renders as garbage on a rep's screen. The `node -e` parse check cannot catch this. `test-onesheet.js` now asserts no `+ D +`, `undefined`, `[object Object]` or `'''` reaches the rendered script.
  2. **`text-transform:uppercase` + `innerText` again.** The beat headings render as "BEAT 1", so a case-sensitive `call.search(/Beat 1/)` returns −1 and the ordering assertion fails for the wrong reason. Third instance of this trap (after `\bDay\b` and "Solar Evaluation") — **any assertion against `innerText` of styled text must be case-insensitive.**
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-onesheet.js`: all three beats present and **in order**, the no-product warning present, battery reactive-only, and the free/no-commitment language intact.

### Sub Sheet acknowledgement re-prompted on every cleared history (fixed 2026-08-08)
Reported alongside the rep-agreement re-prompt, but a **different root cause**: the Sub Sheet ack was **localStorage-only**. `if (!localStorage.getItem(_ackKey))` gated the popup and the button did nothing but `localStorage.setItem` — so clearing iPad history re-prompted every time, and **there was no record anywhere of who had acknowledged which rate sheet.** For a document that sets what subs get paid, that record is worth having independently of the annoyance.
- **Now server-backed with NO migration:** stored in `rep_agreements` with `source='sub_sheet_ack'` and `agreement_version=SUB_SHEET_VERSION`. That table already carries `rep_id / rep_name / signed_at / source / agreement_version`, so it fits exactly and works the moment it deploys — deliberately chosen over a new table given how many migrations already sit unapplied. `_subSheetAcked(person)` checks localStorage first (fast path), then the server; the ack writes both.
- ⚠️ **The rep-agreement login check MUST exclude these rows.** A sub-sheet ack is newer than the signed agreement and carries a *different* version string, so without the filter it would look like the newest agreement at the wrong version and force a needless re-sign. The read now takes `limit(10)` and picks the newest row whose `source !== 'sub_sheet_ack'` **client-side** — a server-side `.neq('source', …)` would silently drop every legacy row where `source` is NULL, because `source <> 'x'` evaluates to NULL in SQL.
- ⚠️ The ack button no longer interpolates the rep's name into its `onclick` attribute — a rep called `O'Brien` broke the markup. The handler reads `_ssAckPerson` instead.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-subsheet-ack.js`, gated both ways: a server ack survives `localStorage.clear()`, the ack writes a correctly-shaped row with an apostrophe-bearing name intact, and an ack row newer than the signed agreement does not trigger a re-sign.
- ⚠️ **Test-brittleness note:** `test-reprompt-blink.js` asserted the agreement read by matching the exact `select('id,agreement_version,signed_at')` string, which broke the moment `source` was added. Assertions about a query should match its *behaviour* (ordered newest-first, not `maybeSingle`), never its literal column list.

### Three field bugs (2026-08-08): agreement re-prompt, blinking UI, missing toggle
- **Rep agreement re-prompted every time iPad history was cleared.** `signRepAgreement` **INSERTs**, so every re-sign adds another `rep_agreements` row for the same `rep_id` — but the login check read it with **`.maybeSingle()`, which ERRORS on more than one row** (`PGRST116`). That error fell through to the "Supabase unreachable" branch, which trusts `localStorage` — empty on a cleared iPad — so the rep was re-prompted, signed again, added a *third* row, and made it permanent. ⚠️ **A compounding bug: each re-prompt worsened the cause.** Fixed by reading `.order('signed_at', {ascending:false}).limit(1)` and taking `[0]` — newest wins, duplicates are a legitimate audit trail rather than an error. **Never `.maybeSingle()` a table that is written with `.insert()`.**
- **QR code and map blinked constantly.** `watchPosition` fires ~1/sec and a *stationary* phone still reports metre-scale jitter; every tick called `renderSales()`, which replaces `dashBody` wholesale — destroying the QR `<img>` (browser re-fetches it) and the `google.maps.Map` (full re-init, pan/zoom lost). Two fixes: (1) `_cvShouldRedraw()` throttles GPS-driven redraws to real movement (`CV_RERENDER_M` 12 m), a `CV_RERENDER_MS` 3.5 s floor, or an actual change in `_cvRouteKey()`; (2) `#cvDoorsMap` and `#cvQrImg` are **detached and reattached around the innerHTML swap**, so the same nodes survive — which also makes `_cvRenderDoorsMap`'s `_cvMapEl !== el` check a no-op and finally makes pan/zoom sticky.
  - ⚠️ **`_cvRouteKey()` must include the heading bucket.** Without it the throttle made the GPS pill show "heading SW" after the rep had stopped — caught by `test-doors.js` step [4]. Bucketed to 45° so ordinary compass wobble doesn't reintroduce the constant redraw.
  - ⚠️ **The throttle is ANDed with `_cvUiBusy()`, never a replacement for it** — dropping the busy check would let a GPS tick destroy a half-typed note again.
- **Script toggle "missing" from Doors** — it was in the code and correctly mounted; the iPad was on an older deploy. Gated anyway (`id="cvScriptToggle"` asserted present) so a real regression would be caught.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-reprompt-blink.js`, gated both ways.

### Automatic shift clock (2026-08-07)
Per Dennis: *"automate the clock function so someone doesn't 'forget to click the buttons so my hours are off' type of excuse."* Hours are now derived from logged work rather than button presses, in two halves:
- **Auto-open (client):** `_bbAutoShiftOnActivity()` runs from `bbLogActivity` on any `dialer`/`door_knock` write. If the rep has no open `rep_shifts` row it inserts one (`source:'auto'`) stamped at that activity. If an open shift has been idle beyond `AUTO_SHIFT_IDLE_MIN` (45m) it is closed **at its last seen activity** and a fresh one opened. Deliberately **not awaited** — a disposition must never wait on, or fail because of, the clock, and the whole thing is try/catch'd so a missing `rep_shifts` table degrades silently.
- **Auto-close (server):** `netlify/functions/shift-autoclose.js`, scheduled `*/15 * * * *`. The client half only fires on the rep's *next* activity, which misses the common case — they shut the laptop and the shift stays open until tomorrow. The sweeper finds open shifts, looks up each rep's most recent `lead_activity` since the shift started, and if that is older than 45 minutes closes the shift **stamped at the last real activity, never at the moment the sweep ran** (otherwise idle time bills as work). A shift with no activity at all closes at its own `started_at` and contributes zero hours; anything beyond `MAX_SHIFT_HOURS` (16) is capped.
- The manual Start/End Shift buttons still work for a rep who wants to clock in before their first dial — nothing depends on them being pressed.
- Verified in `scratchpad/test-autoshift.js`: a logged dial opens exactly one `auto` shift, a second dial on a live shift opens none, and the Phase 3 fallback assertions above.

### Dialer Accountability dashboard (Team → 📢 Dialers, 2026-08-06)
`_teamDialersHtml()` in portal.html — the admin scoreboard for the dialing team, deliberately separate from **1:1s**: that view is qualitative (what reps are hearing, coaching patterns), this one is the numbers. Range pills Today / 7d / 30d (`window._dialRange`, default Today), plus a live "on shift right now" strip, team totals, a per-rep table, and a team-wide outcome mix.
- **Roster = `team_members` (active, role setter/tech/dialer) ∪ everyone with activity in range.** A dialer with no activity renders a row of zeros marked "no activity" rather than disappearing — a scoreboard that only lists people with activity hides exactly the person you need to see. Inactive members and non-dialing roles (ops) are excluded.
- **Stale-rep flag:** anyone clocked in with nothing logged for 25+ minutes shows an amber "nothing logged Nm ago" on their live card.
- **Contact % is coloured against the team average**, not a fixed benchmark — with a brand-new team there is no external number worth measuring against.
- **Degrades without the migration:** `rep_shifts` is optional here. If `20260803_setter_database.sql` hasn't been run, dials/contacts/bookings are still real (they come from `lead_activity`), hours and dials/hr render as `—`, and an amber banner names the exact migration file.
- ⚠️ **Two bugs the harness caught while building this — both worth remembering as metric-design traps:** (1) **channel mixing** — door-knock outcomes were counted as contacts while the denominator (`dials`) excluded knocks, inflating contact rate for anyone who also knocks; contacts/bookings/talk-time are now **dial-only** and knocks stay a separate count. (2) **guard applied after clipping** — the 16h forgotten-clock-out guard ran on the range-*clipped* duration, so a shift left open two days ago clipped to a plausible-looking ~6h inside "Today" and sailed straight past it; the guard now judges the **full** shift duration before clipping to the range.
- **Admin seat = Dennis, one identity (2026-08-06):** `bbRepName()` returned the literal string `'Admin'` whenever `CURRENT_ROLE === 'admin'`, so Dennis's own dialing split into two rows on every per-rep report — "Dennis Larsen" when he dialed from the sales portal, "Admin" when from the admin portal (46 + 33 dials shown separately). Fixed at both ends: `bbRepName()` now stamps `ADMIN_DIALER_NAME` ('Dennis Larsen') going forward, and `_repCanonical(name)` folds the historical aliases (`admin`, `solar review admin`, `dennis`, `tech4`) into that one name **at read time**, so no data migration is needed and old rows merge immediately. Applied at all six per-rep grouping points (dialer dashboard roster/activity/shifts, 1:1s note grouping + metrics + shifts). Add to `REP_ALIASES` if another generic label ever appears in `lead_activity.rep_name`.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-dialer-dash.js`) with four dialers including a deliberate no-show: roster completeness, metric math, stale detection, a shift straddling midnight clipped to the range, the forgotten clock-out dropped, range widening picking up prior-day dials, and the no-migration fallback.

## Jobs Tab: Commission Dashboard + "Installed" Step (2026-08-25, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
The admin Jobs tab previously showed zero financial information on a job card — no commission, no company margin, nothing beyond deposit/milestone pills. Reported as the page "not being functional yet." Built after three rounds of clarifying questions (scope kept expanding correctly each round — this is the final, confirmed spec):
- **Visibility:** Admin Jobs page AND each rep's own portal Jobs list show commission info. **Ops partners see none of it** — they keep exactly what they already had (their own M1/M2/M3 payout), never the rep's split or the company's margin. This was explicitly confirmed after a first-pass answer accidentally included Ops partners; re-asked and corrected before building.
- **Potential vs Earned is a disposition change, not a date.** Per Dennis: *"Monitoring online makes sense for Axia but not for fixmy pipeline yet since that service doesn't exist. Let's change that disposition to Installed."* — followed by the higher-risk of two options offered: **actually renumber the FixMy pipeline** (not a separate independent field bolted on the side) to insert step 9 = **Installed**, pushing Monitoring Online from step 9 → step 10. The 9-step pipeline documented under "FixMy.Energy (diagnostic / battery retrofit)" above is now 10 steps.

### The renumbering itself
A full read-only audit (see the exact grep sweep this was based on — every `step` comparison in `portal.html` and every Netlify function) found **nothing anywhere does a literal `step === 9` or `step >= 9` comparison** — every terminal-stage check already used `step >= 8`, `step >= 7`, or explicit dropdown arrays. This made the renumbering far lower-risk than it looked from the outside. What actually changed:
- **Four hardcoded step-label arrays**, previously able to drift independently, all updated together: the admin lead editor's `edStep` dropdown, the tech/sales editor's `salesEditLead` dropdown, the lead-card pill label+color arrays (`buildLeadCard`), and the customer-portal's own progress tracker (`loadCustomerDashboard`) — homeowner-facing, labeled plainly "Installed."
- **Every `step >= 8` site** (admin "Complete" filter/stat tile, `socials-agent.js`'s "installing" detection, `calcRepCommission`'s $500 fallback-base eligibility) now also matches the new step 9 by construction — reviewed each one; in every case sweeping "Installed" into the existing "8-and-up" bucket is the correct behavior, not a side effect to guard against.
- ⚠️ **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed in the same pass, unrelated to the renumbering:** the lead-card progress-dot row (`buildLeadCard`) was hardcoded to render only 8 dots (`[1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8]`) for what was already a 9-step pipeline — short by one *before* this change even started. Now renders all 10.
- **`adminQuickAdvanceStep`'s existing hard stop at `step >= 7`** (the one-click arrow button) is unchanged — it already couldn't quick-advance past "Not Sold/Dead" before this change, so steps 8/9/10 have always required the full editor dropdown or a dedicated action button. Not a new limitation.
- ⚠️ **Asymmetry found, not fixed (flagged to Dennis, not silently patched):** the admin editor's `saveLeadEditor` recomputes `install_date`/`diagnostic_date` based on `step >= 7`, but the tech/sales editor's `setLeadStep` never touches either field — it only ever writes `{step: newStep}`. A tech advancing a lead to Install Booked or beyond from their own portal does not populate `install_date`. **This is why Earned gates on `step` directly, never on `install_date`** — the date field can't be trusted to be populated depending on which editor was used, but `step` always can.

### The commission engine — one computation, three surfaces
`_loadFixmyJobCommissions(ids)` / `_fixmyCommCache` (portal.html) is the single source of truth, reused by the admin Jobs card, the new Jobs-tab dashboard, and each rep's own Jobs card — deliberately **the same formula `edLoadAccounting`/`edRecordFixmySplit` already use** (Revenue − Sub Sheet Costs = Net; 40% pool; same-person-sets-and-closes keeps the full 40%, a distinct setter splits 20/20), not a second implementation of it. Prefers the real locked-in `commissions` row once one exists (`edRecordFixmySplit` has been clicked); falls back to a live-computed estimate (labeled "est.") otherwise — exactly the "accruing" language `edLoadAccounting` already used, extended to work in bulk across many jobs at once instead of one at a time.
- **Earned = `step >= 9`.** Potential = sold (`sold_type` set) but still earlier than that.
- `_jobCommLineHtml(cid)` — **admin-facing**, shows every party's slice at once (closer, setter if one exists, and the company's margin) plus a POTENTIAL/EARNED badge, rendered under each FixMy job card via a `#jobComm_<id>` placeholder div filled in after the async fetch resolves (no full re-render — same pattern as the New Solar summary tile).
- `_jobCommLineHtmlForRep(cid, person)` — **rep-facing**, shows ONLY that person's own slice: their closer amount if `person.id === job.rep_id`, or their setter amount if their name matches `setter_name` — never both, never the other party's number, never the company margin. Mounted on the rep's own Jobs card (`renderSales()`, FixMy jobs only — Top Tier is excluded, New Solar has its own separate estimate mechanism already).
- Any write that can flip `step` or lock in a commission (`markJobInstalled`, `setLeadStep`, `saveLeadEditor`, `edRecordFixmySplit`) deletes that customer's `_fixmyCommCache` entry so the next render recomputes rather than showing a stale Potential/Earned badge.

### "Mark Installed" — the one-tap action that makes step 9 reliable
Per Dennis's own commission math depending on this being set accurately, and having just watched `job_status` (a similar hand-maintained dropdown) go stale on every single job in production (see the Jobs-page audit that kicked this whole build off — every job showed "Pending Assessment" regardless of real progress, because nothing made updating it easy): `markJobInstalled(id)` is a single confirm-and-go button on the admin Jobs card (shown when `step === 8`, i.e. Install Booked), mirroring `markOpsMilestonePaid`'s exact shape — one tap, no need to open the full lead editor and hunt for the Progress Step dropdown. This is the actual fix for the "will this go stale like job_status did" risk, not just documentation telling someone to remember.
- ⚠️ **`job_status` itself was NOT fixed in this pass** — flagged to Dennis as a separate, real, currently-live bug (every job shows "Pending Assessment" regardless of real progress, because nothing besides a manual dropdown edit ever changes it), but fixing it wasn't part of the agreed scope for this build. Still open.

### Jobs-tab dashboard (2026-08-25 version — superseded below, 2026-08-29)
`_jobsCommDashHtml()` / `_loadJobsCommDashboard()` — collapsed by default (mirrors the Dialer/Doors scoreboard pattern Dennis referenced), Day/Week/Month/Quarter/Year/Lifetime range picker (`_jobsCommRange`), two tiles (Potential, Earned) each showing job count + combined rep total + company total. **Scoped by when the job was created**, not by when it was sold or installed — i.e. "of the jobs that entered the pipeline in this window, how much is sitting in each bucket right now" — labeled explicitly in the panel caption so this isn't mistaken for a payout-date report. Mounted only on the FixMy tab (`adminJobCat !== 'new_solar'`) — New Solar keeps its own existing `_nsCommSummaryHtml` tiles, a separate, already-shipped mechanism.
- Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit.

## Jobs Tab Rebuild — Axia/QCell PPW commission formula + unified pipeline reporting (2026-08-29, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Full rebuild requested with an explicit "ask all necessary questions" instruction — two rounds of `AskUserQuestion` were run before writing any code, since the Axia/QCell formula and the source-based commission-rate rule both determine real dollar payouts and could not be safely guessed. This section supersedes the "Jobs-tab dashboard (2026-08-25 version)" note directly above it — that dashboard shape (Potential/Earned tiles, FixMy-only, New Solar had its own separate `_nsCommSummaryHtml`) is gone, replaced by the unified one described here.

### Steve Silverthorn — the bug that started the audit
Reported missing from FixMy Jobs despite a real $299 diagnostic charge. Root cause found in Supabase directly: his record had **`lead_category:'new_solar'`** even though everything else about him was a FixMy diagnostic (`payments` row: $299.99, category `revenue`, `source:'ghl'`; `agreement_status:'signed'`; `solar_status:'ns_welcome_scheduled'` — a lead-phase New Solar status he never actually progressed past, confirming the New Solar tag was never real). He was invisible in **both** pipelines: wrong category for FixMy, and `sold_type` still null so New Solar's own job-phase gate never picked him up either. A companion audit (`join`ing real `payments` revenue against `lead_category`/`sold_type` mismatches) found **no other cases** — Antoinette Kruse, the other row the audit surfaced, is a fully legitimate, correctly-categorized New Solar job (`sold_type:'new_solar'`, `solar_status:'ns_pto'`) and needed no fix.
- **Fixed directly:** `lead_category='fixmy'`, `sold_type='diagnostic'`, plus the missing $250 job cost inserted into `job_costs` (`status:'pending'`).
- This is also **why** the Jobs-tab inclusion rule changed — see `_isRealJob` below.

### "All sold jobs (jobs that have generated revenue)" — the new Jobs-tab inclusion rule
`renderJobsView()`'s job list was gated on `!!c.sold_type` alone — exactly the gap that hid Steve Silverthorn. **`_isRealJob(c)`** (portal.html, right above `renderJobsView`) is now `!!c.sold_type || _jobsRevenueIds.has(c.id)` — `_jobsRevenueIds` is a `Set` of every `customer_id` with a real `payments.category='revenue'` row, fetched once (`_ensureJobsRevenueIds()`) and re-rendering the view when it lands. `sold_type` stays the fast, synchronous path for the common case; the payments-ledger check is the fallback that catches anything `sold_type` missed, matching Dennis's own definition of what belongs in the tab.

### Axia/QCell commission — the PPW/Redline formula (replaces the old manual `prompt()` estimate)
```
Base PPW − (Redline − Domestic Content Bonus) = Net PPW
Net PPW × System Size (Watts) = Gross Commission        [shown as "Revenue" on the card/dashboard]
Gross Commission × pool% = combined Setter/Tech pay      [shown as "Costs" / COGS]
Gross Commission − that pay = Solar Review Net Commissions
```
- **Base PPW, Redline, and Domestic Content Bonus are manual per-job fields** (Dennis's explicit choice over a centrally-maintained lender rate table), entered on the Axia/QCell job's accounting panel exactly like FixMy's editable Dealer Cost — `ns_base_ppw` / `ns_redline_ppw` / `ns_dc_bonus_ppw`, three new nullable numeric columns on `customers` (migration `20260829_ns_ppw_commission_fields.sql`, ✅ applied). Redline's own two components, confirmed by Dennis from the Victoria Seacombe worked example ($2.20 Redline − $0.45 Domestic Content Bonus = $1.75 net): the raw per-watt lender fee (EnFin is the field's hint label, but the field works for any lender/cash deal — leave at 0 and Net PPW just equals Base PPW) minus a federal ITC domestic-content credit that reduces it.
- **System Size (Watts) = `customers.system_size` (kW, the field's existing unit everywhere else in the app) × 1000.**
- **The pool percentage is source-dependent — this is new logic, Axia/QCell only, FixMy's existing flat 40%/20-20 rule is explicitly untouched** (confirmed directly: *"Axia/QCell only"* when asked). `_nsIsBlackBoxSourced(c)` = `black_box===true || lead_source==='orphaned_list'`:
  - **Black-Box-sourced** (or, per Dennis, the not-yet-built "Lead Locker" source once it exists): pool = **40%**, split exactly like FixMy — same person sets & closes keeps the full 40%, a distinct setter splits 20/20.
  - **Self-Sourced** (everything else — "not from Black Box," the rep found/onboarded the account themselves): pool = **70%**, same-person-vs-distinct-setter split logic applies here too, so a distinct setter splits **35/35** rather than getting nothing.
  - ⚠️ **The test had to be "not from Black Box," never a narrow positive match on `lead_source==='self_generated'`.** Checked live: every existing Axia/QCell lead today has `lead_source` either `null` (9 of 10) or `'referral'` (1 of 10) — **zero** carry a literal Black Box marker. A narrow `self_generated`-only match would have misclassified the entire current book at the wrong (lower) rate.
- **`_nsCalcCommission(c)`** (portal.html) is the one formula implementation — pure, synchronous, takes a customer record, returns `{netPpw, watts, revenue (=gross), costs (=closer+setter pay), companyAmt, isBlackBox, poolPct, closerPct, setterPct, hasSetter, ...}`. `_axiaCommCache` / `_loadAxiaJobCommissions(ids)` wraps it the same way `_fixmyCommCache`/`_loadFixmyJobCommissions` already does — the only round trip needed is checking which payouts (if any) are already locked into `commissions`, since every formula input already lives on the `customers` row.
- **`edRecordAxiaSplit(cid)`** — the Axia/QCell "Lock in as Sold → Payroll" button, a direct mirror of `edRecordFixmySplit`: recomputes at click time, inserts whichever of closer (`rep_commission`), setter (`setter_commission`), and company (`override`, payee `solar_review_corp`) rows aren't recorded yet, `line:'new_solar'`. Once locked, the real row always wins over the live estimate (same "accruing" vs "sold" language FixMy already uses).

### One shared card/dashboard shape for both pipelines
Per Dennis: *"they're both different and different formats based on the Portal access level — clean all this up to be synonymous."*
- **`_commLineHtml(d, statusBadgeHtml)` / `_commLineHtmlForRep(d, person, statusBadgeHtml)`** (portal.html) are now the ONE rendering core for both pipelines — `_jobCommLineHtml`/`_jobCommLineHtmlForRep` (FixMy) and the new `_axiaCommLineHtml`/`_axiaCommLineHtmlForRep` are thin wrappers supplying their own status badge (FixMy: POTENTIAL/EARNED off `step`; Axia: ESTIMATE/LOCKED off whether the `commissions` rows exist, plus a BLACK BOX/SELF-SOURCED + pool% tag) over a normalized cache-entry shape (`closerAmt/setterAmt/companyAmt/*Locked/*Paid/hasSetter/setterName/repId/revenue/costs`) both `_fixmyCommCache` and `_axiaCommCache` now share.
- **Every job card — both pipelines, admin view — shows Revenue → Costs → Net Commissions** (`_revCostLineHtml`, folded into `_commLineHtml`) before the per-party split. Confirmed directly with Dennis using the Victoria Seacombe numbers: Revenue = Gross Commission ($13,932, what Axia/QCell owes Solar Review) · Costs = combined Setter/Tech pay owed out ($5,572.80) · Net Commissions = the difference ($8,359.20). For FixMy this is unchanged: Revenue = collected `payments`, Costs = `job_costs` Sub Sheet total.
- **Rep-side cards (both pipelines) show ONLY that person's own commission slice** — no Revenue, no Costs, no company margin — via `_commLineHtmlForRep`. This is a real fix, not just cleanup: the Axia/QCell rep card previously showed **no commission information at all** (`_jobCommLineHtmlForRep` was only ever wired into the FixMy/Top-Tier branch of the rep Jobs view; the `isNSJ` branch had nothing). Both `renderJobsView` (admin) and `renderSales()` (rep) now call the matching pipeline's line function identically in structure.
- **The `job_status` badge (Pending Assessment/In Progress/Ready/Approved) is deleted, not fixed** — per Dennis, choosing the "Drop it" option over "fix it for real": it never advanced off "Pending Assessment" in production regardless of actual progress (flagged as a known-broken field in the 2026-08-25 entry above), and the pipeline-step badge plus Net Commissions already convey real progress without it. The underlying `job_status` column and the lead editor's own dropdown for it are untouched — only the card badge is gone.

### Unified dashboard — Revenue / Costs (COGS) / Net Commissions, Pending by role, one range picker
`_jobsCommDashHtml(cat)` / `_loadJobsCommDashboard(cat)` replace both the old FixMy-only Potential/Earned dashboard and the separate New Solar `_nsCommSummaryHtml` estimate strip (deleted) with one function taking `cat` (`'fixmy'` | `'new_solar'`):
- **Three tiles: Revenue, Costs (COGS), Net Commissions** — summed from the same per-job cache each pipeline already computes (`d.revenue`/`d.costs`), so the dashboard total and the sum of what each card shows can never quietly disagree.
- **`JOBS_COMM_RANGES` trimmed to Total / Year / Quarter / Month / Week** (per Dennis's exact wording) — dropped the old "Day" option and renamed "Lifetime" → "Total"; the range key `'life'` is unchanged internally, only its label.
- **Pending Payouts row — Setter / Tech, plus Ops for FixMy only.** "Pending" = locked-but-unpaid *or* still-accruing-estimate, either way money not yet paid out. **Ops pending is FixMy-specific** — read from the existing `ops_milestone1_amount`/`_status` + `milestone2_amount`/`_status` + `milestone3_amount`/`_status` fields already on `customers` (Solar Review pays Ops partners directly via the Sub Sheet). Axia/QCell has no Ops role in this sense — Axia IS the installer, never a sub-contracted partner Solar Review pays — so its pending row is Setter/Tech only.
- `_jobsCommOpen`/`_jobsCommLoading` are now per-category objects (`{fixmy:false, new_solar:false}`) instead of a single shared boolean, so toggling one pipeline's dashboard open doesn't affect the other's collapsed state.
- Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit.

### Axia/QCell Commission Setup popup — replaces passive panel data-entry (2026-08-29, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Reported off a screenshot of the accounting panel's old-looking "Commission & Revenue Estimates" 3-button UI: *"Is this how the commissions are becoming calculating? This isn't ideal."* Grepping the live source for that exact heading found nothing — it no longer exists anywhere in `portal.html` (it was already replaced by the Base PPW/Redline/DC-Bonus fields earlier the same day, see above), so the screenshot was very likely a stale/cached client view, not a live regression. Regardless, Dennis's underlying ask was a real, separate UX improvement: *"I want a pop up (similar to the fix my diagnostic pricing tool) when the disposition changes to Welcome Call Closed asking System Size, Base PPW, and TPO. This will tell the calculation of the commissions and if there's a setter it will split that commission accordingly."*
- **Fires the instant `solar_status` transitions INTO `ns_welcome_closed`** (`NS_JOB_KEYS[0]` — job phase start), in **both** the admin editor (`saveLeadEditor`) and the tech/sales editor (`savesSalesEdit`) — mirroring exactly where the FixMy Diagnostic Fee popup intercepts the step-3 transition in each editor. Only fires on the actual transition (`cRec.solar_status !== 'ns_welcome_closed'` beforehand) — re-saving a lead already at that status does not re-open it.
- **One shared implementation, not two** (`openAxiaCommModal` + helpers, portal.html, declared at the top-level script scope right after `confirmDiagFee`) — unlike the FixMy modal's deliberate admin/sales duplication, there is no portal-specific difference in this math, so a second copy would just be a second place to drift out of sync with `_nsCalcCommission`. Reachable from the sales-portal closure the same way `_nsCalcCommission`/`_axiaCommCache` already are (plain global function declarations in a non-module script share one `window`).
- **TPO ("Third-Party Ownership" — a lease/PPA financing type, confirmed with Dennis) is the gate for whether Redline/Domestic Content Bonus apply at all**, not a fourth dollar field: a Cash/Loan deal has no redline, so those two inputs only appear once **TPO — Lease/PPA** is picked, and are forced to `0` whenever Cash/Loan is selected (never left stale from an earlier draft). This is why Dennis's own description named only 3 fields (System Size, Base PPW, TPO) even though the underlying formula still needs Redline/DC Bonus — TPO answers "does a redline apply," and if so the two dollar fields appear inline in the same popup. Stored as new `customers.ns_is_tpo boolean` (migration `20260829b_ns_is_tpo.sql`, ✅ applied) — purely informational/UI-gating, `_nsCalcCommission` itself is untouched and still just reads whatever `ns_redline_ppw`/`ns_dc_bonus_ppw` values are on the row.
- **The popup writes the SAME columns the accounting panel's manual fields already read/write** (`system_size`, `ns_base_ppw`, `ns_redline_ppw`, `ns_dc_bonus_ppw`, plus the new `ns_is_tpo`) — confirmed with Dennis ("popup feeds the same fields") — so the panel isn't replaced, it's a second, always-available way to correct these numbers after the fact. Live preview inside the popup (Net PPW, Gross Commission, Solar Review Net Commissions, and the Setter/Closer split) is computed with the exact same `_nsCalcCommission(c)` the dashboard and job cards use — one formula, read everywhere, never a parallel calculation.
- **Cancel discards the whole intercepted save**, same as the FixMy diagnostic-fee modal — the disposition change itself doesn't commit until the popup is confirmed, so a rep can't move a lead into job-phase without the numbers that drive its payout.
- ⚠️ **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed while wiring this: `saveLeadEditor`'s `updates.system_size` was reading `#edSys` unconditionally** (`(document.getElementById('edSys')||{}).value||null`) — but `#edSys` is hidden for Axia/QCell leads (`isNSEdit ? '' : edField('System Size (kW)','edSys',...)`), so `getElementById` returned `null` and **every single admin save of an NS lead — a note, an auto-save, anything — silently wiped `system_size` back to `null`**, even one this new popup had just correctly set moments earlier. Same trap `nem_status` right below it already guards against (`document.getElementById('edNem') ? … : (cRec.nem_status||null)`); `system_size` now uses the identical guard. Without this fix, the popup's own numbers would have been erased by the very next unrelated save.
- Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit.

## Ops Milestone 1 retired + job-card simplification pass (2026-08-30, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Started from a Thomas Hart correction, which surfaced that the CC-surcharge math and the M1 payout mechanism both needed a real look, not just a data fix.

### Thomas Hart — CC surcharge belongs to company margin, not the rep's 40% pool
Dennis gave exact numbers for two transactions (Diagnostic Sold $499.99 / COGS $300 / Cristina Huang's comm $80.00 / Solar Review's comm $119.99, and On-Site Diagnostic Service $301.00 / COGS $100 / comm $80.00 / Solar Review $121.00) that didn't match the real `payments` rows ($312.74 + $519.49 = $832.23, not $499.99 + $301.00 = $800.99). The $31.24 gap is exactly the Sign & Pay **3.9% credit-card surcharge** on his two stated base prices ($499.99×1.039=$519.49, $301.00×1.039=$312.74, both exact) — confirming the real payments are correct and the surcharge itself is real revenue Dennis wants landing in **Solar Review's margin, not blended into the rep's 40%-of-net split** ("we may want to reduce the service fees for CC considering it's collecting additional revenue above the costs" — a future consideration, not built here).
- **Fixed as a direct data correction, not a formula change:** inserted 2 `job_costs` rows ($300 + $100) and 2 `commissions` rows (`rep_commission`, Cristina Huang/tech5, $80 + $80, status `sold`) on Thomas Hart's job. Left the real `payments` rows untouched. Since FixMy's company margin has always been the *computed remainder* (Net − rep − setter, never its own stored row — unlike Axia/Top-Tier/New-Solar's `override` company row), the surcharge lands in Solar Review's share automatically: Revenue $832.23 − Costs $400 − Rep $160 = **$272.23**, which is exactly his stated $240.99 base company split + the $31.24 surcharge.
- ⚠️ **Not automated going forward.** Dennis confirmed *"Keep the 40%-of-net auto-formula"* for future jobs — this was a one-off manual override, not a new per-transaction entry model. Automatically excluding CC surcharge from every job's rep-commission pool would need a reliable way to tell a Sign&Pay-surcharged card payment apart from an unsurcharged one, and `payments` has no column for that today (the `method` JSON shape doesn't distinguish it). **Flagged as a real, well-scoped follow-up** (e.g. stamp `payments.surcharge_amount` at `sign-complete.js` collection time) rather than guessed at now, since it touches every rep's payroll math.

### Ops Milestone 1 removed from the FixMy job card, editor, and Sub Sheet
Per Dennis: *"I don't want them to show M1s any longer. We can remove all M1 logic in the code."* — chosen over a cards-only option specifically so the lead editor and Sub Sheet wording came along too.
- **Removed:** the "Ops M1 Paid/Pending" badge and "M1 Paid" button from the admin FixMy Jobs card (`renderJobsView`); the "Ops Milestone 1 Amount/Status" fields from the admin lead editor's Deposit & Milestones section (`edOpsMile1Amt`/`edOpsMile1St`, and the matching lines in `saveLeadEditor`'s `updates` object — no more silent write to those columns from any save); the M1-specific "Payment Terms" wording on both Sub Sheet copies (standard sheet + per-partner sheet); the M1 branch in `markOpsMilestonePaid`'s column-lookup ternary (only ever called with M2/M3 now); M1's contribution to the admin Jobs dashboard's "Ops pending" total.
- **M2/M3 (bigger retrofit/install milestone billing) are untouched** — the admin card's "M2 Paid" button no longer gates on M1 having been paid first (dropped the `ops_milestone1_status==='paid'` prerequisite).
- **The underlying `ops_milestone1_status`/`ops_milestone1_amount` DB columns are NOT dropped** (no destructive migration, standing convention) — just unused by every write path now.
- ⚠️ **A real pre-existing bug found and fixed while doing this:** the customer portal's own battery-retrofit "Completion (M2)" milestone display (`brHtml` in `loadCustomerDashboard`, `sold_type==='battery_retrofit'` only) was reading `ops_milestone1_status`/`ops_milestone1_amount` — but the admin Jobs card's own "M2 Paid" button has always written `milestone2_status`/`milestone2_amount` (a different, still-alive pair of columns). **The customer's own Completion status could never actually update when admin marked M2 paid** — two different columns, one write path, one read path, never wired together. Now reads `milestone2_status`/`milestone2_amount`, the columns the admin action actually writes. This is also what freed `ops_milestone1_*` up to be fully retired with zero loss of the legitimate customer-facing feature.
- ⚠️ **Deliberately left untouched, scoped decision:** the Ops-partner portal's own job-detail view (Cosmic/Jon/John's own "Payment 1" line, `loadOpsDashboard`-area code around portal.html:26761/26798/26940) still reads `ops_milestone1_status`/`ops_milestone1_amount`. Nothing writes those columns anymore for new jobs, so this will read as permanently "Awaiting Payment" going forward for anything created after 2026-08-30 — historical jobs that already had a value still display correctly. Left in place because removing an Ops partner's own visibility into their payment status is a different, not-yet-confirmed decision from "stop showing admin an M1 badge" — flag to Dennis before removing.
- **Also removed (job cards, not the M1 pass but bundled the same day per Dennis — "I don't need the magic links listed here"):** the "🔗 Link" (magic-link) button from both the admin FixMy Jobs card and the rep-portal FixMy Jobs card. The lead editor's own quick-actions Magic Link button (`openLeadEditor`/`salesEditLead` header row) is untouched — a different surface, not what was shown in the screenshot.
- **Not yet built, needs Dennis's input:** making the admin Jobs tab "more dynamic w/ reflection to any unearned or unpaid commissions," tied to the described Friday weekly-payout cadence for the existing Finance → Commissions sub-tab — Dennis is still working out the shape of this ("I am going to simplify the processes moving forward too but not sure how yet"), so nothing was guessed at beyond what's described here. The Cosmic-link piece flagged above is now built — see the entry directly below.
- Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit.

### Diagnostics/RMA/Service Fee order of operations, formalized (2026-08-30, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Dennis laid out the real 7-step flow this pipeline actually follows: (1) disposition moves Photos Uploaded → Diagnostic Booked, (2) price is selected, (3) customer is charged, (4) admin manually schedules the visit with Cosmic/the ops partner over a text thread, (5) once that's derived it gets updated in the portal so the customer sees it — "we don't currently have a calendar used for this (don't want it blocking new sales)", (6) a link pops up to pay Cosmic through `https://www.cosmicsolar.com/account`, (7) it's marked complete in the CRM once processed. Also: **"We don't need M2 or M3 either"** for this pipeline.
- **Steps 1–3 were already built** — `openDiagFeeModal`/`confirmDiagFee` (the step-3 transition intercept) already covers price selection + the Sign & Pay charge. No change needed.
- **Step 4 is manual/outside the portal by design** — the text thread with Cosmic isn't something to build around.
- **Steps 4–5 — real simplification, not just documentation:** `confirmDiagSchedule` (fired from `openDiagSchedulingModal`, the "Diagnostic Job Converted" popup) used to ALSO attempt to book the visit onto the GHL Diagnostic Calendar (`GHL_DIAG_CALENDAR_ID`) after saving the manually-entered date. That calendar isn't the real scheduling mechanism (scheduling happens over text with Cosmic, per Dennis's own description) — the attempt is now removed entirely. The flow is now exactly what's described: admin types the date/arrival window, it saves to `diagnostic_date`/`arrival_window`/`arrival_end` (which the customer portal already reads to display their appointment), and the existing `GHL_DIAG_SCHEDULE_WEBHOOK` customer-notification fires — no calendar dependency anywhere in this path. ⚠️ `GHL_DIAG_CALENDAR_ID` is still read elsewhere (`saveLeadEditor`'s reschedule-an-existing-GHL-appointment path, gated on `cRec.ghl_appointment_id` already being set) — left alone since it only fires for appointments that predate this change; nothing new creates a `ghl_appointment_id` via this flow anymore, so it self-limits to legacy data.
- **Steps 6–7 — built on the EXISTING `job_costs` Sub Sheet mechanism, not a new one.** Rather than reviving anything shaped like the just-retired Ops Milestone 1 (a status column on `customers`), the "💳 Pay via Cosmic" link is a plain new-tab anchor to `https://www.cosmicsolar.com/account`, rendered next to any **pending** `job_costs` line item on the lead editor's Sub Sheet panel (`edLoadAccounting`) whenever `assigned_ops === 'ops3'` (Cosmic Solar) — gated via a new `data-ops` attribute on `#edAcctPanel`, read alongside the existing `data-cid`/`data-cat`/`data-rep`. Step 7 ("marked on CRM as completed once it's processed") is the Sub Sheet's **pre-existing** pending → paid toggle (`edToggleCost`) right next to it — clicking the Cosmic link to actually pay, then flipping that toggle, is the whole step-6/7 sequence. No new table, no new status field.
- **"We don't need M2 or M3 either" needed no code change.** The Milestone 2/3 editor fields ("Powerwall 3 — 3 Milestone Payment Schedule") are already gated `c.sold_type === 'battery_retrofit'` only — they were never reachable for a Diagnostic/RMA/Service Fee job in the first place, so the Jobs card's M2/M3 buttons (which only render when `milestone2_amount`/`milestone3_amount` are actually set) could never have appeared there either. Battery Retrofit's own M2/M3 milestone billing — a different job type, not part of this order-of-operations — is untouched.
- Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit.

### Ops Portal sync — job_costs COGS only, NOT the customer's Diagnostic Fee (2026-08-30, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE, corrected same day)
First pass, per Dennis: *"The synchronization needs to happen on Ops Portals view as well (when a Diagnostic Fee is paid it should show on their end. They should have access to the same report as well)."* Built as two things: a Diagnostic Fee (customer `invoice_status`/`invoice_amount`) badge, plus this partner's own `job_costs` Sub Sheet entries.
- ⚠️ **The Diagnostic Fee badge was WRONG and was removed the same day.** Corrected instruction: *"ops shouldn't see anything except their COGs. IE: Diagnostic is $300. RMAs are $350. Service Fees are based on Scope of Work. That's the only thing that should be visible by Ops."* The customer's payment to Solar Review is revenue, not the ops partner's cost — `_opsFeeBadgeHtml` and all three call sites (job card header, Revenue tab ledger, job detail Payment Status block) are deleted entirely, not just hidden.
- **What's actually correct and stays: this partner's own `job_costs` Sub Sheet entries only** — `loadOpsDashboard` fetches `job_costs` scoped to `customer_id in (this partner's own job ids)`, never another partner's (the existing "Ops must not see the shared Sub Sheet rate card" restriction is untouched — this is a partner reading their own jobs' cost lines, nothing else). `opsCostsFor(jobId)` is the one lookup shared by:
  - **Revenue tab (Job Ledger + YTD Summary):** the old "Payment 1" row and totalEarned/totalPending calc used the retired `ops_milestone1_status`/`amount` — now iterate this partner's real `job_costs` rows instead (each its own labeled row, e.g. "Diagnostic — COGS", not a generic "Payment 1"). M2/M3 (battery retrofit milestone billing, still a valid COGS-to-ops concept) are untouched — still summed in alongside the job_costs total.
  - **Job card detail (isOpen):** relabeled "💰 Your Cost (COGS)" — just the list of this job's own cost lines with pending/paid status, nothing else.
- ⚠️ **Real data leak found and fixed while correcting this:** Thomas Hart's two `job_costs` rows (entered earlier the same day, see the correction above) were labeled `"Diagnostic Sold — COGS ($499.99 txn)"` / `"On-Site Diagnostic Service — COGS ($301.00 txn)"` — the parenthetical embedded the CUSTOMER's revenue figure directly in a label that now renders verbatim on Cosmic's own portal (Thomas Hart's `assigned_ops` is Cosmic).
