## Proposal builder — manual System Price was being clobbered (fixed 2026-08-06)
Reported on Erik Starkey's proposal: *"after I typed out the price and the squares it wasn't adding appropriately."* Root cause in `_propRecalcRedline()`: it recomputed **`build.price = lineSum + reroofCharge` unconditionally**, so a manually typed System Price was discarded on the very next recalc — and every square/charge/line-item edit calls `_propUpdateTotal` → `_propRecalcRedline`. Typing $28,000 then entering the roof area produced $30,250 (the catalog sum + reroof), silently throwing the $28,000 away. Catalog `default_price` values are **dealer cost**, so the manual System Price *is* the retail number — losing it is losing the whole quote.
- **Fix:** the manual price is stored as `build.basePrice` **excluding** add-on charges, with a `priceManual` flag (mirrors the existing `partPriceManual` pattern). `price = base + _propAddonCharge(build)`, where base is `lineSum` until the rep types one. The field therefore redisplays exactly what was typed, and squares/panels edited afterwards **add on top** instead of replacing it. `_propRestoreExisting` sets `priceManual` + a back-computed base so a reopened proposal shows the price actually sent, not one re-derived from the catalog.
- **⚠️ Anything quantity-driven must go through `_propAddonCharge`/`_propAddonCost`, never straight into `build.price`** — that is the mistake this fix undoes.
- **Remove & Reinstall card (new, same pass):** `RR_COST_PER_PANEL = 225` (Sub Sheet), `build.rr = { on, panels, chargePerPanel }`, handlers `_propToggleRR`/`_propRRPanels`/`_propRRCharge`, persisted as `opt.remove_reinstall` + a customer-visible `id:'remove_reinstall'` line item, and filtered back out on restore so it can't double-count. It is a **builder card, not a `proposal_catalog` row**, because it is quantity-driven per-panel pricing — the same shape as Reroof, and it needs no migration. **Deliberately no default retail** ($225 is dealer cost; inventing a charge would quietly set customer pricing) — the card warns while $/panel is 0.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-proposal-pricing.js`) across six scenarios: price-then-squares (the reported order), squares-then-price, bumping squares after pricing, R&R alone, both add-ons plus the resulting 40% pool, and toggling an add-on back off. Gated both ways — restoring the unconditional recompute reproduces the $28,000 → $13,250 loss exactly.

### A custom line item made Rep commission show $0 no matter the System Price (fixed 2026-08-20)
Reported on Joe Medina's proposal: *"$15,500 Total Price − $12,500 Redline = $3,000 Gross Commission = $1,200 Net Commission. Why is this showing zero?"* Root cause: `_propRecalcRedline()`'s `lineSum` — the only thing that ever fed `build.redline` — explicitly **excludes custom line items** (`.filter(function(li){ return !li.isCustom; })`), because a typed custom item carries no known dealer cost from the catalog. There was no way to tell the builder what a custom item's real dealer cost was, so any proposal built wholly or partly from a custom line (rather than a picked `proposal_catalog` row) redlined at $0 — and the commission line is gated `build.redline > 0 ? … : 0`, so it forced itself to **exactly $0** regardless of a perfectly real System Price. The dead giveaway this had existed before and been dropped: `document.getElementById('propRedline_'+optIdx)` was still being *read* in two places (`sendProposal`, and an orphaned, never-rendered `propCalcComm` left from a prior builder UI) — an editable Dealer Cost input clearly used to exist and was lost in a rewrite.
- **Fix:** a real, live **"Dealer Cost / Redline ($)"** input next to System Price in the builder (`propManualRedline_<optIdx>`, `window._propSetRedlineManual`), mirroring the existing manual-System-Price pattern exactly — `build.dealerCostManual` + `build.baseDealerCost` store the typed figure **excluding** add-on (reroof/R&R) cost, so `_propRecalcRedline` no longer needs a catalog item to have a real redline. A **"↻ Auto"** button clears the override back to the catalog-summed value. `_propRestoreExisting` now also flags a reopened proposal's saved `opt.redline` as manual (same reload-authoritative treatment `priceManual` already got) so it can't silently zero out on the next edit.
- **Also added:** an explicit red warning under the field when no catalog item is selected and no override is set (*"dealer cost is $0 and Rep commission will show $0"*) — the old behavior had no way to tell a rep this was happening short of noticing the number. The waterfall preview also gained a **"Gross commission (price − dealer cost)"** line between Dealer cost and Rep commission, matching how Dennis actually reasons about it out loud.
- Verified with a logic-level harness reproducing the exact reported numbers: a proposal built as one $15,500 custom line item redlines at $0 and shows Rep commission $0 (matches the report exactly); typing Dealer Cost $12,500 makes it $1,200 (matches Dennis's hand math exactly); a normal catalog-only build is unchanged (price=redline=$12,500, no regression); Auto-reset correctly falls back to the catalog sum.
- ⚠️ Same lesson as the 2026-08-06 manual-price fix directly above: **anything a rep can type a real number into must be stored as an explicit override that survives the next recalc, not silently re-derived and discarded.**

### SDCP battery incentive eligibility — wasn't tied to eligibility, it was tied to which SKU got clicked (fixed 2026-08-21, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Reported while reviewing an email draft for Arturo Ma (battery-only retrofit, no new panels): the builder's SDCP rebate line only ever populated when the rep happened to click the **"TPW3 + (5) 420W Tesla Panels"** catalog bundle — the plain **"Tesla Powerwall 3 (Retrofit)"** option (`option1`, the one that fits a battery-only retrofit onto an *existing* system) carried `sdcp_rebate: null` in the catalog, i.e. $0, with nothing in the UI explaining why. A battery-only quote — the exact case a FixMy retrofit customer is — silently priced the incentive at zero for no reason connected to actual eligibility.
- **The real eligibility rule, per Dennis, is not "which SKU":** (1) SDCP territory (zip — already automatic), (2) **the system is close to net-zero or net-positive to the grid** — a fact about that customer's usage a rep has to judge, not something a catalog row encodes, and (3) **upon SDCP's own qualification** — an external approval this tool can never confirm in advance. Zip alone was being treated as sufficient; it isn't.
- **Data fix:** `proposal_catalog.sdcp_rebate` set to `3375.00` on the plain `option1` battery-retrofit row too (previously only the panels-bundle row had it) — same incentive amount, since it's one SDCP program gated by conditions, not a different dollar figure depending on whether panels are also being sold. Direct SQL, no migration file (a data update, not a schema change): `update proposal_catalog set sdcp_rebate = 3375.00 where option_key='option1' and service_type='battery_retrofit' and sdcp_rebate is null;`. **Scoped to the two `battery_retrofit` rows only** — the `inverter_swap` bundles that happen to bundle a PW3 with new panels were deliberately left untouched, since Dennis's ask was specifically about the "PW3 retrofit," not those bundles; extend if he confirms they should carry it too.
- **New qualification gate, `build.sdcpQualified`** (default **false**): the dollar amount still auto-computes from catalog + zip exactly as before (nothing changed about *how much*), but it is no longer applied by default. The builder shows an explanatory line **and an explicit checkbox** ("Confirmed close to net-zero/positive — include as pending SDCP qualification") the rep must tick before the amount (a) shows as a deduction in the pricing waterfall, or (b) is ever written into a sent/previewed proposal (`sendProposal`/`previewProposal` both gate `opt.sdcp_rebate` on this flag now, and stamp `opt.sdcp_qualified` alongside it). The customer-facing line is labeled **"SDCP incentive (pending SDCP qualification)"**, not a flat "SDCP rebate" that reads as already secured. `_propRestoreExisting` treats a reopened proposal that already carries a saved `sdcp_rebate` as previously-confirmed, so it doesn't silently un-check on reopen.
- ⚠️ Same family as the manual-price and manual-redline fixes directly above: **a number a rep can only include after an explicit action must be stored as an explicit flag, never inferred from which button happened to get clicked.**

### Participate Program was zeroing out commission (fixed 2026-08-27, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
Reported with two screenshots of the same proposal: Participate off showed Gross commission $4,000 / Rep commission $1,600 (correct); toggling Participate **on** dropped BOTH to $0 and wrongly triggered "Below $1,000 minimum — admin approval required to send." Per Dennis: **"Participate is a lease to own program so the 30% discount does not impact the commission table."** Commission must always be computed on the true System Price, never on what the customer ends up paying through the financing program.
- **Root cause, in two places, both reading the Participate (discounted) price instead of the System Price for commission math:**
  1. **The live waterfall preview** (`_propRenderBuilds`) — `_oComm` and the displayed "Gross commission" line both used `_custPays` (`build.participate && build.partPrice ? build.partPrice : build.price`) instead of `build.price`. On this proposal, System Price $20,179 − Dealer cost $16,179 = $4,000 gross correctly; with Participate on, `_custPays` becomes the discounted $14,125, and $14,125 − $16,179 is negative, clamped to $0.
  2. **`sendProposal`'s actual persistence** — `effCommPrice = partPrice != null ? partPrice : price` had the identical bug **one level more serious**: this is what gets written to `opt.rep_commission`/`opt.gross_commission` in the saved `customers.proposal` record and to `customers.commission_estimate`. Any proposal actually **sent** with Participate on would have persisted `rep_commission: 0`, not just displayed it wrong.
  - **A third site already had it right** and was the reference used to fix the other two: the button that swaps "Send" for "Admin Approval Required" below the $1,000 minimum (`_anyBelowMin` in `_propRenderBuilds`) was already computing off `b.price` alone, never touched by Participate — which is exactly why the Send/Approval button behavior didn't match the broken number shown next to it.
- **Fix:** both buggy sites now use `build.price`/`price` (the true System Price) unconditionally for `rep_commission`/`gross_commission`, matching the already-correct third site. The "Customer pays" and "Participate savings" lines in the waterfall are untouched — those are deliberately still `_custPays`-based, since that's genuinely what the homeowner pays; only the commission math was wrong.
- ⚠️ **What this did NOT corrupt:** `edRecordFixmySplit` (the function that actually locks in a paid `commissions` row at sale time) recomputes net authoritatively from real `payments`/`job_costs` tables — it never reads the proposal's stored `rep_commission`/`gross_commission` at all. Those saved fields are a pre-sale **estimate** only (`commission_estimate` is written once on send and never read back anywhere else in the file). So no historical actually-paid commission was ever wrong from this bug — only what a rep saw while quoting, and what a *sent-but-unsold* proposal's estimate field said.
- ⚠️ Same family as the manual-price/manual-redline/SDCP-qualification fixes directly above: **a discount or override applied for the customer's benefit must never silently flow into the internal commission math** — the two have to be computed from clearly separate figures (`build.price` for commission, `_custPays`/`partPrice` for what the customer owes), not one variable reused for both.

## "$NaN deposit" on every accepted proposal — the deposit field was never actually settable (fixed 2026-08-31, per Dennis)
Reported vaguely as "not working right with saving and the customer/rep accepting the proposal terms." Investigated by pulling recent `customers.proposal` rows directly rather than guessing — found the real defect in the customer-facing proposal page itself, `loadCustomerDashboard`'s "Payment Milestones" block (the one place `previewProposal` also renders, since Preview opens the real customer magic-link view rather than a separate admin renderer).
- **Root cause: the milestones panel rendered unconditionally**, reading `opt.milestones.deposit` with no guard. `opt.milestones` is only ever populated by `sendProposal`/`previewProposal` when `build.deposit > 0` — and **`build.deposit` has no rep-editable input anywhere in the builder UI**; it's set exactly once, from `row.deposit_amount` (a `proposal_catalog` default) when the item is picked. For any item whose catalog row has no `deposit_amount` — the common case for the dealer-cost/redline-driven jobs this pipeline has moved to over the past month — `opt.milestones` stays `{}`, and `parseFloat(undefined).toLocaleString()` renders the literal string **"$NaN"** as the "Deposit (today)" line, positioned directly above the Accept button on every real proposal a customer opens.
- **Fix:** the milestones panel now only renders when `opt.milestones && opt.milestones.deposit > 0` — no deposit configured means no milestones section at all, not a fabricated NaN one. Same guard applied to `acceptProposal`'s post-accept confirmation message, which had the identical unguarded `opt.milestones.deposit` read in its "collect your $X deposit" line — a customer who just successfully accepted would see "$NaN deposit" in their own confirmation.
- **Not a "saving" failure in the sense of a lost write** — `sendProposal`/`acceptProposal` both already verify their DB writes and surface real errors. The report's "not working right" was accurate as a description of what the customer/rep actually saw (a broken-looking number at the exact moment of accepting), not a sign the accept itself silently failed.
- ⚠️ **The deposit/milestone billing concept is effectively unused in the current pipeline** — with no way for a rep to set it, only proposals whose catalog item happens to carry a `deposit_amount` ever get a real milestones panel. If Dennis wants milestone billing (deposit + remaining, or deposit + permit/inspection split) to actually be usable again, it needs a real rep-facing input in the builder, not just this rendering fix — flagged as a follow-up, not built here since it wasn't asked for.

## Reroof category box (proposal builder, 2026-07-20)
Per-option **🏠 Reroof** card in the builder's catalog grid (`_propRenderBuilds`, after the RMA card). Toggle on → two inputs: **Surface area (sq ft)** (auto-filled from the Google Solar `roofAreaMeters2 × 10.7639` when a site map exists, editable) and **$ / square charged** (editable retail, default $850). `_reroofSquares(build)` = `ceil(sqft / 100)` (100 sq ft = 1 square).
- **Cost is fixed `REROOF_COST_PER_SQ = $750`/sq.** `_propRecalcRedline` now computes `redline = lineSum + squares×750` (dealer cost, → commission math) and `price = lineSum + squares×chargePerSq` (retail auto-added to the System Price). Non-reroof options are unchanged (squares 0).
- State: `build.reroof = { on, sqft, chargePerSq }` (added to `_propInitBuild`). Handlers `_propToggleReroof` / `_propReroofArea` / `_propReroofCharge`.
- `sendProposal` persists `opt.reroof = { squares, sqft, cost_per_sq:750, charge_per_sq, cost, charge }` and pushes a customer-visible line item `Complete Reroof — N squares` (price = charge). `_propRestoreExisting` rehydrates `build.reroof` from `opt.reroof` and **filters the `id:'reroof'` line item** out of `build.lineItems` so it never double-counts.
- **Sub Sheet (Rate Sheet):** a "Reroofing → Reroof (per square)" row (1 square = 100 sq ft; dealer cost, retail set per proposal). **Rate updated 2026-08-06: $750 → $700/sq**, and a new "Other Services → Remove and Reinstall (per panel) $225.00 / panel" row was added. A rate change must be made in **three** places or the sheet and the money disagree: the displayed standard sheet (`_renderSubSheetHTML`), the per-partner editable defaults (`OPS_RATE_ITEMS`), and — for reroof only — `REROOF_COST_PER_SQ` in the proposal builder, which feeds redline → the 40% commission pool. `SUB_SHEET_VERSION` is bumped on any rate change so every rep re-acknowledges the sheet at next login (same pattern as `REP_AGREEMENT_VERSION`). Gated in `scratchpad/test-subsheet.js`.

## Proposal Tool Intelligence (upgraded 2026-07-16, Erik Starkey proposal)
Production math lives in `_propCalcBaseProduction()` (portal.html) — shared by the builder's Projected Savings preview (`propShowScenario`), stored proposals (`sendProposal`), and AI insight (`generateProposalInsight` → `generate-proposal-insight.js`). Three intelligence upgrades:
- **Measured inverter output %** replaces the old hardcoded 25%: builder has a full-width "Restore Existing Array" card (toggle + "% output measured" input, per option; `build.restoreArray` / `build.arrayOutputPct`, persisted as `opt.restore_array`/`opt.array_output_pct` and restored by Load Previous). Recovery = `system_size × 1,600 × (1 − pct/100)`. String inverters often fail PARTIALLY (one MPPT/string down → 40–65% output, not 25%) — always derive pct from measured production: `current kWh/day ÷ expected kWh/day`. The toggle also means recovery counts when the "new inverter" is a **Powerwall 3's integrated 11.5 kW / 6-MPPT solar inverter (20 kW DC input)** — existing strings land directly on the PW3, so a dead string inverter needs NO standalone inverter purchase and per-MPPT tracking replaces legacy optimizers. This is the default cost-reduction play on inverter-failure retrofits.
- **Battery arbitrage scales with storage**: `13.5 kWh × (PW3 units + Expansion Packs) × 1.5 cycles/day`, capped at 60% of the home's daily usage when the bill is on file (a battery can't shift more than the evening load). Old model was fixed 13.5 × 1.5 regardless of quantity.
- **NEM true-up honesty** (`_propSplitByUsage()`): production beyond annual usage (derived `bill × 12 ÷ $0.453`) is valued at ~$0.04/kWh wholesale true-up, not $0.453 retail; surfaces in methodology lines + `prod_data.surplusKwh`. Frame surplus as EV/electrification headroom, not bill savings.
- `prod_data` now stores `outputPct, storageKwh, batteryUnits, batteryCycledDaily, surplusKwh, annualUsageKwh` — customer-facing methodology renders from these (falls back to legacy text for old proposals).
- **Erik Starkey draft proposal**: `supabase/seed/erik_starkey_proposal.sql` (⚠️ not yet applied — run in SQL Editor/MCP; direct DB access was blocked in the build session). Status `draft` → invisible to customer until Dennis loads it in the builder ("Load Previous"), sets retail prices (line items are dealer cost + DRAFT labor/reroof estimates), confirms reroof squares + real monthly bill, and Sends. Full worked analysis in the SQL header comments (21× SunPower 2013 strings at ~51% output + 7× Enphase IQ7 2019 healthy; 63% blended; usage ≈ 18,235 kWh/yr).
- **Proposal JSON round-trips faithfully (2026-07-17)**: `line_items[]` persist their `id` (option_key) and `_propRestoreExisting` prefers it over the option's primary id, plus rehydrates the PW3/Expansion/420W stepper counts from ×N in restored names — battery/panel detection and steppers survive save → Load Previous. The Erik seed carries per-line ids.
- **Tesla Visa gift card promo EXPIRED 2026-07**: all $500/$1,000 Visa references removed from index.html; `_propAddCatItem` and the PW3 stepper no longer copy `tesla_rebate` from the catalog (SDCP rebate logic untouched); customer render shows a neutral "Tesla Rebate" label only for legacy proposals that already carry a value.

## Orphan Partial Captures (fixed 2026-07-17)
A mid-typed web partial ("3335 Laurashawn", no phone/email, `partial_capture=true`) never string-equals the completed full address ("3335 Laurashawn Ave, La Mesa, CA"), so `ghl-inbound.js`'s exact-address match couldn't absorb it — the completion made its own record and the partial lingered as a duplicate flagged "Booking Incomplete — Call Back" + surfaced in the Black Box hot-lead popup. Two fixes: (1) `ghl-inbound.js` now, on any real completion (`!isPartial` + has email/phone + address), archives contactless partials whose address shares the booking's house-number + first street word (`address=ilike.'<num> <street>%'&partial_capture=eq.true&phone=is.null&email=is.null`) — sets `partial_capture=false, archived=true`. (2) `buildLeadCard`'s incomplete badge now requires the explicit `partial_capture===true` flag (+ no sold_type/diagnostic_date, step≤1) instead of merely a missing `diagnostic_date`, so a completed booking whose date didn't stamp (GHL AppointmentCreate webhook not wired) never false-flags. Pre-existing orphans self-clean on the next booking to that address, or Archive them manually.

## `/booked` — the booking confirmation has its own URL (2026-08-10, per Dennis)
`/book` used to confirm **in place**: on success it hid `captureCard`, showed `confirmedWrap`, and 3s later sent the customer to the portal magic link. The URL never changed, so there was **no page load to hang a conversion on** — the only trigger available was a JS event. Dennis asked for a real URL so Google Ads / GTM can fire on a page view instead. The `/book` form itself is unchanged.

**Flow is now `/book` → `/booked` → `/portal?email=…&code=…`.**
- **`booked.html`** (+ a `netlify.toml` 200-rewrite for `/booked`) renders the confirmation the customer used to see on `/book` — same markup, same copy, same `Congratulations!` / `Request Received!` split on `_appointmentBooked`.
- ⚠️ **The payload rides in `sessionStorage` (`fmBooking`), never in query params.** Email/phone in the URL would leak into GA4 page paths, Google Ads reports, `Referer` headers and browser history. Keeping `/booked` clean and param-free is also what makes a URL-based conversion trigger trivial — match the path exactly, no wildcards.
- **`book.html` falls back to the old in-page confirmation** if `sessionStorage` throws (locked-down private mode). The `confirmedWrap` markup is deliberately still there for this; it is not dead code.
- ⚠️ **The booking payload is NOT cleared after render.** An earlier version removed it, which meant a customer tapping **back** from the portal saw "No booking found". Dedupe is handled by `fmBookingCounted` (keyed on the booking id) instead.
- **Landing on `/booked` with no booking in session** (bookmark, shared link, new session) renders an honest "No booking found" + a link back to `/book`, and **fires no conversion**. It must never invent a confirmation.

### ⚠️ Pick ONE conversion mechanism
`booked.html` fires the Ads conversion inline (`AW-18121666624/u8R7COLKt7AcEMDgisFD`) **and** pushes a `booking_confirmed` dataLayer event for GTM. **If a URL-based conversion on `/booked` is created in Google Ads, the inline block must be deleted** — it is marked with a `⚠️ REMOVE THIS BLOCK` comment. Otherwise every booking counts twice. Also set the Ads action's **Count = One** so a refresh can't double-count server-side (the JS guard only protects the client).

### `gbraid`/`wbraid` were never captured (fixed in the same pass)
The conversion is gated on the session carrying a real paid click, so organic bookings don't inflate the campaign — correct, and deliberate. But the gate only checked **`gclid`**, and Google sends **`gbraid`/`wbraid` instead** for iOS and other privacy-restricted clicks. Those bookings recorded nothing. Both keys are now captured (`book.html`, `index.html`, `contact.html`, `careers.html`) and honoured by every gate (`booked.html`, `book.html` fallback, `thank-you.html`). This matters most for Performance Max, which is heavily mobile.
- ⚠️ **A `0.00` conversion count is NOT proof of a broken tag here** — the gclid gate means zero is the *expected* output until paid clicks actually land. Same family as the Regrid `no_key` / Tracerfy 429 lesson: check whether the path ran before concluding it failed.
- **The homepage flow still goes `index.html` → `/thank-you`** (a separate unique URL, same conversion label). Two flows, two confirmation URLs. Unifying them onto `/booked` is an open option, not done.
- Verified in Chromium/Playwright (`scratchpad/test-booked.js`, 20 assertions): the handoff happens, the URL is clean and PII-free, the conversion fires exactly once on `gclid` **and** on `gbraid`, an organic booking fires none while still showing the customer their confirmation, a refresh doesn't re-count, a direct visit is honest and silent, the magic link is built correctly, a booking with no email doesn't follow a broken portal link, and a GHL-rejected appointment says "Request Received". **Gated both ways** — reverting the `gbraid` widening drops step 4, and removing the no-booking guard makes the page invent a confirmation.
- ⚠️ **Harness trap:** these pages declare `function gtag(){dataLayer.push(arguments)}` at top level, which **overwrites any `window.gtag` stub** an init script installs. Counting conversions from a stub silently reports zero for *both* the fires-correctly and never-fires cases — count them out of `dataLayer` instead.

## Formatting / Branding SOP (2026-07-17, per Dennis — AUTHORITATIVE)
**Whenever a formatting/branding/layout change comes up, ASK clarifying questions first** (AskUserQuestion) so branding, flow, and formatting stay synonymous across ALL portals (Admin, Tech/Sales, Ops, customer). Don't unilaterally restyle one portal — changes should keep every portal consistent.

## Unified Lead Card (2026-07-17)
All lead cards render through the single `buildLeadCard(c)` function in portal.html — used by both the Admin Leads view and the Tech/Sales Leads view, so every portal shows the **same** card. Contents: name + disposition/step pill, meta line, address **+ SDCP badge** (San Diego Community Power territory by zip via `SDCP_ZIPS`/`extractZip`), assignment line, a badges row (INV/AGR/OPS badges, Incomplete-Booking warning), then action buttons: **Edit · Call · Text · Nav (Apple Maps) · Archive** (icon **and** word). Edit routes by role (`openLeadEditor` for admin, `salesEditLead` otherwise). Top Tier cards append confirm/reminder + Open Solar / Service Finance quick-links via `_ttCardExtras`.
- **Magic Link + Proposal moved OFF the card and INTO both editors (2026-07-17), then Magic Link was restored to the card too (2026-08-24, per Dennis — the editor-only version wasn't enough).** `buildLeadCard(c)`'s action row now has a quick **🔗 Link** button (copies the magic link via the same `sendMagicLink()` used in both editors), gated on the lead having an email — same gating as the pre-existing Jobs-card Link button. Proposal Tools stays editor-only (`openLeadEditor`/`salesEditLead` header action row) — it's a closer-facing cluster, not a quick action every card needs. The tech leads list still has no inline "Update Status" dropdown (dispositions are changed inside the editor, not from the card list).
- **Lead temperature REMOVED from the UI (2026-07-18):** the Hot/Warm/Cold gauge on cards (`_leadTempBadge`) and the "TEMP" segmented control in both editors (`_leadTempControl`) were deleted per Dennis — unused noise. The `customers.lead_temp` **column is retained** (still set server-side by `ghl-inbound.js`/`ghl-book.js`, still feeds bizdev/chat agents); saves no longer write it from the editor.
- **Proposal Tools cluster (2026-07-18):** the single "Proposal" pill in both editors is now a labeled **"PROPOSAL TOOLS"** row built by `_proposalToolsRow(id, address, isNS)`: **Internal Tool** (`openProposalBuilder`, FixMy/Top-Tier only — hidden on New Solar), **Aurora** (`v2.aurorasolar.com/new-project`), **SolarGraf** (Cosmic's tool, `app.solargraf.com/dashboard`), **Imgur** (`imgur.com/upload` — grab a roof-image URL to paste into the proposal builder's image field). Aurora/SolarGraf copy the lead address to the clipboard via `window._propOpenExternal(url, addr)`. **Gated out of the Setter and Ops portals:** the tech editor only renders it when `!config.setterMode` (Setter + rep/Ronda run setterMode); Ops never opens these editors. Admin editor always shows it.
- **Step-5 "Proposal Sent" no longer auto-opens the proposal builder** (both `saveLeadEditor` and tech `setLeadStep`) — the Proposal Tools cluster is the entry point now. The step-3 diagnostic-fee popup (`openDiagFeeModal`/`openSalesDiagModal`) is unchanged.
- **Diagnostic auto-conversion relaxed** (`saveLeadEditor`): a lead converts to a `sold_type='diagnostic'` Job when **invoice_status='paid'** alone (no longer requires agreement_status='signed' too). Jan Burrell paid by check + signed outside Sign & Pay — the strict "paid AND digitally signed" rule left her stuck as a Lead. Invoice = Paid IS the sale.

## Persistent Staff Auth (2026-07-17)
Staff sessions moved from `sessionStorage` (which iOS Safari wipes on backgrounding — the cause of constant iPad re-logins) to **localStorage with a 30-day sliding expiry**. Helpers `_persistUser` / `_readUser` / `_clearUser` in portal.html; `signOut()` clears both. Active use renews the 30 days. Keys: `fmUser`, `fmUserExp`.

## Sales Rep Agreement — versioning & forced re-sign (2026-07-30)
`buildRepAgreementHTML()` (portal.html) is the actual legal text reps sign on first login (`signRepAgreement()` → `rep_agreements` table). Section 3.6 (Setter/Dialer Commission Split — matches the 40%-pool math already live in `edRecordFixmySplit`: same person sets & closes keeps the full 40%, a distinct setter gets 20%/20% with the closer) was added 2026-07-30, and per Dennis existing reps must **re-sign** before their next payout — the split itself is **forward-only**, no retroactive recalculation of past deals.
- **`REP_AGREEMENT_VERSION`** (portal.html, near `SUB_SHEET_VERSION`) is a plain version string — bump it whenever the agreement text next materially changes.
- **`rep_agreements.agreement_version`** (migration `20260730_rep_agreement_version.sql`) records which version a rep signed. The single gate computed once per login in `loadSalesDashboard` (`agreementSigned`) now requires `agreement_version === REP_AGREEMENT_VERSION`, not just "a row exists" — so every rep who signed an older version is automatically routed back to the signing screen on next login, exactly like a brand-new hire, with no separate re-sign flow needed. The `localStorage` offline-fallback cache (`repAgreement_<id>`) carries the version too (as its last `|`-delimited segment) so a stale pre-change cache can't bypass the re-sign requirement.
- **Admin visibility:** `agStatus()` in the Team view's rep list now shows a third state — amber **"⚠ Needs re-sign"** — for anyone whose `agreement_version` doesn't match current, alongside the existing green "Signed" / red "Not signed".
- ✅ `20260730_rep_agreement_version.sql` is applied (verified 2026-08-08) — it's non-destructive (`add column if not exists`), existing rows land `NULL` which correctly fails the version check and triggers re-sign.

## Sign & Pay — payment methods (2026-07-17)
`sign.html` now has a pill row: **Credit Card (3.9%) · Bank/ACH (1%) · Check (0%)**. Fee (surcharge) and the Stripe PaymentIntent are recomputed per method; `sign-init.js` takes a `method` param → `SURCHARGE_PCT` {card:0.039, ach:0.01, check:0} and `payment_method_types` (`card` / `us_bank_account`); check returns `offline:true` with no PI.
- **Card** (3.9%): existing Card Element + `confirmCardPayment` → `sign-complete` (paid). 
- **ACH** (1%): `stripe.collectBankAccountForPayment` (Financial Connections) + `confirmUsBankAccountPayment`; ACH settles in 1–2 days so on `processing`/`succeeded` it records signature + invoice `sent` (pending) via `sign-fallback` `agreement_only`, not immediate paid. ⚠️ **Requires ACH + Financial Connections enabled on the Stripe account** — errors surface inline if not.
- **Check** (0%): fully offline — records signature + pending invoice via `sign-fallback` `agreement_only`; admin marks Paid when the check clears (which then auto-converts to a Diagnostic Job per the relaxed rule above).

## Tablet (iPad) zoom (2026-07-17)
Lead views bumped ~5% on tablet: `@media(min-width:768px) and (max-width:1099px)` sets `html{font-size:16.8px}`, `#dashboard{max-width:none}`, wider `.dash-body` padding — less wasted screen, easier navigation.

## Photo Upload (rep/admin paths — fixed 2026-07-17)
- `uploadPhotos()` no longer hard-requires `#photoStatus`: it falls back to the Tech/Sales editor's `#sePhotoStatus` (or a dummy). Previously a missing element silently aborted the entire upload while `salesUploadPhotos` still reported "✓ N photos uploaded" — the "saved 6 photos but nothing shows" bug. The fabricated success message is removed; the real per-photo status from `uploadPhotos` is shown instead.
- Rep/admin uploads now auto-advance an unsold FixMy lead from step 1 → 2 (Photos Uploaded) on first successful upload, matching the customer-portal upload path.

## Photo Upload — job_photos schema fix + decoupled AI sync (2026-07-22)
Two stacked bugs behind reports of uploads being slow or failing outright ("All uploads failed — see error above" with nothing useful shown):

1. **Schema mismatch (the real root cause of outright failures):** `job_photos` only ever had legacy quoted mixed-case columns `"Path"`/`"Label"`, not the lowercase `path`/`label` every upload path (`uploadPhotos`, `handleUpload`, the storage-scan fallback) actually inserts. Confirmed with a direct `INSERT` test — `column "path" of relation "job_photos" does not exist` (42703). Every DB-record save for an uploaded photo was failing at this step: the file lands in Storage, but the row insert silently errors, so the photo never really saves. Fixed by `supabase/migrations/20260722_job_photos_schema_fix.sql` (renames `"Path"`→`path`, `"Label"`→`label`, non-destructive). **⚠️ Not yet applied — MCP writes were denied in the session that built this; run it manually in the SQL Editor, before `20260722_quoya_async_sync.sql` below.**
2. **AI call blocking the upload:** every photo ran a synchronous Claude vision call (`quoyaAutoAssess`/`quoyaAssessPhoto`) before the DB insert, so a multi-photo upload was as slow as N sequential API calls, and one flaky call could stall the batch. Also found and fixed in the same pass: the "all failed" fallback message check (`status.innerHTML.includes('&#9888;')`) never actually matched, because reading `.innerHTML` back after assigning an HTML entity like `&#9888;` returns the rendered character, not the entity text — so the real per-photo error always got silently overwritten by a generic "see error above" with nothing above it. Replaced with a plain tracked `hadError` boolean.

**New architecture — upload now saves instantly, AI categorization happens after:**
- `uploadPhotos()` (admin/tech) and `handleUpload()` (customer portal) insert the photo row immediately with `quoya_status='pending'` — no blocking AI call. The customer-portal upload cards already know their target category (Utility Bill/Inverter/Main Panel) from which card was tapped, so `label` is set to that immediately; only the quality-check pass (blurry/dark/cut-off) waits.
- **Manual "🔄 Sync with Quoya" button** — added to all three Photos panels (admin job detail, admin lead editor, tech/sales editor). Calls `netlify/functions/quoya-sync.js` (regular/sync function, ≤25 photos, scoped to one lead) and refreshes the grid with real results immediately.
- **Nightly sweep** — `netlify/functions/quoya-sync-background.js` (background function, scheduled `0 10 * * *` ≈3am PT in netlify.toml), processes up to 60 pending/failed photos per run across all leads. A large backlog just takes a few nights to fully drain rather than spiking Anthropic usage in one shot; anyone who needs a specific lead done sooner uses the manual button.
- Shared categorization logic lives in `netlify/functions/lib/quoya.js` (fetch pending rows → fetch image bytes from the saved Storage URL → call Anthropic vision directly, server-side → PATCH the row). Failed attempts retry up to 3 times (`quoya_attempts`) before being marked `skipped` so a permanently broken image URL doesn't keep burning tokens forever.
- **Net effect on AI spend:** roughly neutral to lower — same one Claude call per photo as before, just moved off the upload critical path; the retry cap and nightly batch cap actually bound worst-case cost tighter than the old unbounded per-upload retries.
- New `job_photos` columns (migration `20260722_quoya_async_sync.sql`, ✅ applied — verified 2026-08-08): `quoya_status` (pending|done|failed|skipped), `quoya_attempts`, `quoya_synced_at`, `expected_label`.
- `quoyaAutoAssess`/`quoyaAssessPhoto` (the old client-side blocking-call functions) were deleted from portal.html — no longer called from anywhere.
- Pending photos render with a "⏳ Pending"/"Analyzing…" badge in all three grids until synced.

### A booking's photo slot silently uploaded with the WRONG label, or none at all (fixed 2026-08-20)
Reported: uploaded the Utility Bill during a Site Evaluation, and it never showed as completed on the lead (Joe Medina). Confirmed via SQL: 5 `job_photos` rows on his job, 3 with `label` and `expected_label` both `null`.
- **Root cause: `uploadPhotos()` read the slot's category off a single shared global, `window._evForceLabel`, INSIDE its per-file loop — not once, at the top of the call.** Both callers that force a category — `evUpload` (Eval wizard) and `canvassBkUpload` (Doors booking's photo-capture step, `CV_BOOK_PHOTOS`: Inverter/Serial/MSP/Production/Utility Bill — exactly Joe Medina's 5 slots) — set `window._evForceLabel = label` before `await uploadPhotos(files)` and reset it to `null` in a `finally` after. Each slot renders its own independent `<input type="file" onchange="canvassBkUpload(this,'<label>')">`, so a rep tapping through slots at a door — pick the Inverter photo, then immediately tap MSP before the first upload's Storage round-trip finishes — has two overlapping calls in flight. Whichever call's `onchange` fires second overwrites the global before the first call's loop reaches its read, and whichever call's `finally` runs first nulls it out from under the other. Photos land with the wrong label, or `null`.
- **Fix:** `uploadPhotos(files, forcedLabel)` now takes the category as an explicit parameter, captured into a call-local variable (`_forcedLabelForThisCall`) at the top of the function — never re-read from the global per file. `evUpload`/`canvassBkUpload` now pass `label` directly instead of writing `window._evForceLabel`; nothing writes that global anymore (a harmless fallback read remains for defense only).
- **Recovering photos already saved with a lost label (no code needed):** every affected row was inserted with `quoya_status:'pending'`, and Quoya's categorization pass determines `label` from the actual image content, independent of `expected_label` — so tapping **"🔄 Sync with Quoya"** on the lead (or waiting for the nightly sweep) correctly labels a photo that lost its category to this race, including backfilling a genuine Utility Bill. No SQL fix was needed or applied for Joe Medina's lead.
- ⚠️ **Same bug class as the `_bbEnrichRunning`/`isPinned` family documented elsewhere in this file: a single shared mutable global written by more than one call site that can legitimately be in flight at once.** Any future forced-category upload path must pass the label as an argument, never through `window._evForceLabel`.

## Aurora Solar Integration
- New Solar leads at job-phase statuses show an Aurora card with Launch + copy-name + copy-address buttons — now the single shared renderer `window._auroraCardHtml(c)` (Jobs list, Jobs detail, lead editor all called it separately before and had drifted).

### True one-click Aurora hand-off (2026-08-20, per Dennis — "make it one step faster")
Every "Launch Aurora" button used to just copy the address to the clipboard and open Aurora's blank `/new-project` page — the rep still had to click the address field and paste. **Researched and confirmed: Aurora has no URL query parameter to prefill that field** (it's a live autocomplete Aurora's own frontend controls) — the only real way to skip the paste is Aurora's authenticated **Create Project API**, which creates the project server-side and returns an id to deep-link straight into it.
- **`netlify/functions/aurora-create-project.js`** — `POST { address, first_name, last_name, email, customer_id }` → calls `POST https://api.aurorasolar.com/tenants/{tenant_id}/projects` with the lead's info (`external_provider_id` = our customer id, for a clean CRM↔Aurora mapping) and returns `{ ok:true, url }` pointing straight at the new project, or `{ ok:false, code }` on any failure.
- **Needs two Netlify env vars Dennis has to generate himself** (only an admin on the Aurora account can, from **Aurora → Settings → API Settings**): `AURORA_API_TOKEN` and `AURORA_TENANT_ID`. Optional `AURORA_ENV=sandbox` to test against `api-sandbox.aurorasolar.com` before pointing at production. **Not yet set** — until they are, `_propOpenAurora()` degrades silently to the old copy-address-then-open-blank-page flow for every rep, so this shipped ahead of the credentials existing without breaking anything.
- ⚠️ **The exact response field carrying the new project's id couldn't be verified from this dev sandbox** (`api.aurorasolar.com` is blocked by its network policy) — the function defensively checks every field name Aurora's docs/tutorials reference (`id`, `project_id`, `project.id`, `data.id`) and logs the raw response when none match, so the first live call's Netlify function log confirms or corrects it. Same "self-discovers and logs everything" pattern as `outage-sync.js`/`zip-boundaries.js` above.
- **`window._propOpenAurora(opts)`** (portal.html) is the one call path every Aurora button now goes through — Proposal Tools row, the Eval wizard, the proposal builder's waterfall, and the New Solar job Aurora card. ⚠️ **The blank tab is opened synchronously inside the click handler, before the `await`** — iOS/iPadOS Safari (this crew's actual devices) blocks `window.open()` called after an async gap as a popup; opening blank first and navigating that same window handle once the fetch resolves keeps it inside the user-gesture grace period. Falls back to the old clipboard-copy flow on any error, `not_configured`, or missing address — silently, no error shown to the rep.
- Not built: writing the resulting Aurora `project_id` back onto the customer row (would let the portal show "Aurora project already started" instead of creating a duplicate on a second click). Worth doing once the credentials are live and the response shape is confirmed — deliberately deferred rather than guessing a schema for a table that doesn't exist yet.

## SMS Tooling Decision — GHL LC Phone over Twilio
**Recommendation: Use GHL's built-in LC Phone (Lead Connector) for all SMS — do not set up Twilio.**

Rationale for slim-budget operation:
- GHL subscription already paid — LC Phone costs per message (~$0.008/segment) but no additional monthly fee or account setup
- Twilio adds a second vendor: account, billing, API keys, monitoring — unnecessary overhead
- GHL SMS integrates natively with GHL workflows, so photo-upload notifications and other alerts can be GHL workflow triggers (no Netlify function code needed)
- Exception: only use Twilio if you need programmatic SMS outside GHL's control flow (e.g., real-time alerts from a Netlify function that can't reach GHL) — not the case here

**Action needed:** Build a GHL webhook-triggered workflow that fires when a customer uploads a photo (via the portal's `notify-photo-upload.js`) → sends SMS to assigned rep via LC Phone. Remove the Twilio path from the Netlify function once the GHL workflow is live.

## Future Services

### Golf Course Solar Panel Protection
**Status: Concept — not yet scoped or offered.**

Solar panels installed near golf courses are at elevated risk of impact damage from errant golf balls. This is a niche but recurring need in San Diego County (Torrey Pines, Rancho Bernardo, Carmel Mountain, Eastlake, etc.).

**Scope of work (to be developed):**
- Site assessment: identify exposure angle, distance from fairways/driving ranges, typical ball trajectory
- Protection options to evaluate:
  - Polycarbonate ballistic shield panels mounted above array on standoff frame
  - Heavy-gauge wire mesh / expanded metal screens (angled to deflect, not catch)
  - Sacrificial tempered glass overlay on high-exposure panels
  - Array repositioning / tilt adjustment during re-roof or retrofit (prevents future exposure)
- Framing: likely custom aluminum unistrut or steel angle — no off-the-shelf product exists for this
- Documentation: before/after photos, warranty language for protection gear vs. panel warranty

**Ops partner:**
- Cosmic Solar's fit is uncertain — primarily a panel swap / install crew, not custom fabrication
- Need to identify a partner with light metal fabrication + roofing experience
- Possible leads: local awning/shade structure contractors, solar racking fabricators, or a roofing/metal shop willing to sub on jobs
- **Action needed:** Ask Cosmic if they're comfortable with custom screen/shield installs; if not, source an alternate ops partner before offering this service

**Lead identification:**
- Filter `address` field in portal for proximity to known golf courses (manual today; could automate with geocoding + golf course polygon overlay)
- Add `golf_course_exposure` boolean to customers table when this service launches

| Lumio | Ch. 11 bankruptcy | Mar 2024 | Multi-state (CA included) | "Lumio Inc", "1st Light Energy" |
| Freedom Forever | Ch. 11 bankruptcy | Apr 15, 2026 | Multi-state | "Freedom Forever LLC" |
| Mosaic Solar Loans | Ch. 11 (LENDER only) | Jun 6, 2025 | 500,000+ (financed) | N/A — lender, not installer |
| Infinity Energy | Ceased operations | 2023 | CA only (Rocklin HQ, one of the largest CA installers, PPA-focused) | "Infinity Energy Inc", "Infinity Energy" |
| Suntuity Renewables | Bankrupt/shut down | ~2024 | Multi-state (NJ/CA/TX) | "Suntuity Renewables", "Suntuity" |

### Priority 2 — Acquired/Managed (lower priority — successor handling service calls)

| Company | Status | Successor |
|---|---|---|
| Vivint Solar | Acquired by Sunrun 2021 | Sunrun |
| SolarCity | Acquired by Tesla 2016 | Tesla Energy |
| Freedom Forever | Ch. 11 Apr 2026 — now in Priority 1 | — |
| Complete Solar | Acquired SunPower assets 2024, rebranded | Active as "SunPower" |

### PermitStack Pull Notes
- SunPower is rarely listed as "SunPower" on permits — use "Complete Solar Inc" or "BRS Field Ops" as primary search terms
- SD County zip filter: 919xx (El Cajon, La Mesa, Santee, Lakeside) + 920xx (San Diego, Chula Vista, Poway)
- System size kW extracted from permit description text via regex when not a dedicated field
- Mosaic is a LENDER not an installer — leads from Mosaic are homeowners with outstanding loans, not necessarily broken systems
- Portal Import tab → "Pull Live from PermitStack" button fires `permitstack-pull.js` for each selected installer, deduplicates by address, auto-populates CSV textarea
- `PERMITSTACK_KEY` — Netlify env var, gates nightly `bb-auto-pipeline-background.js` Phase 1c (city-by-city PermitStack pull for all 16 SD cities). ✅ Set in Netlify.
- **Phase 1c rotation cursor (fixed 2026-08-01):** Phase 1c has no server-side GROUP BY, so it queries city-by-city per installer within a single 5-minute-per-run budget. The nested loop originally always started fresh at SunPower (6 name variants) × San Diego (highest-volume city) every night, so cities late in the list — Carlsbad (#9 of 16), Encinitas, National City, Vista, San Marcos, Lemon Grove — could go unqueried indefinitely even with the key set, since nothing ever advanced past the first installer/city. Root-caused from a "why does 92008 (Carlsbad) show 0 Black Box leads" report. Fixed by flattening `installer × city` into one ordered array and persisting a rotation cursor in `pipeline_state` (key `phase1c_cursor`, same pattern as `expansion_index`) — each run starts where the previous run left off and wraps around, so every combination gets queried within a few weeks instead of the same handful monopolizing every night. Check the Netlify function logs' `Phase 1c: ... covered N/TOTAL units (cursor X→Y)` line to confirm rotation is progressing.

## CPUC / SDG&E NEM Data — Public Records Request Draft

**Status: Drafted, not yet sent.**

### Option A — CPUC (send to `public.records@cpuc.ca.gov`, ~10 business days)
**Subject:** `Public Records Act Request — NEM Interconnection Data (SDG&E Service Territory)`

---

California Public Utilities Commission  
Attn: Public Records Office  
505 Van Ness Avenue, San Francisco, CA 94102

Re: California Public Records Act Request — Net Energy Metering (NEM) Interconnection Data, SDG&E Service Territory

To Whom It May Concern,

Pursuant to the California Public Records Act (Gov. Code § 7920.000 et seq.), I respectfully request all NEM interconnection application and approval data for residential solar installations within the SDG&E service territory, filed between January 1, 2015 and December 31, 2023. Specifically:

1. Service address (street address and ZIP code — census tract or block group acceptable if address is withheld)
2. Interconnection approval date
3. Installed system size (kW-DC or kW-AC)
4. Installing contractor or solar company name as reported on the application
5. NEM tariff type (NEM 1.0, NEM 2.0, NEM-A)

**Purpose:** Solar Review Corp (operating as FixMy.Energy) provides independent solar diagnostic, repair, monitoring, and battery retrofit services to San Diego County homeowners. A significant number of residential solar customers in the SDG&E territory were left without support when their original installers — including SunPower (Chapter 11, 2024), Sullivan Solar Power (closed 2019), Petersen Dean (closed 2020), and Sungevity (closed 2017) — ceased operations. We are using this data solely to identify affected households so we can offer them a path to restore full system performance. We have no intention of publishing, reselling, or sharing this data.

**Format:** CSV, Excel, or pipe-delimited text preferred.

**Fee Waiver Request:** Disclosure primarily benefits the public by facilitating the repair and continued operation of existing distributed energy resources — directly supporting California's clean energy goals. If a waiver is not granted, please provide a cost estimate before proceeding. If any portion is denied, please specify which exemption applies and provide all non-exempt portions.

Dennis Larsen  
Solar Review Corp / FixMy.Energy  
[phone] · dennis@fixmy.energy

---

### Option B — SDG&E Direct (faster, send to `regulatory@sdge.com`)
**Subject:** `CPRA Request — Residential NEM Interconnection Records, 2015–2023`

SDG&E files quarterly NEM data with the CPUC and maintains it in structured form. Cite the same CPRA authority (Gov. Code § 7920.000 et seq.) and request the same five fields. Use the same purpose and fee waiver language as Option A. SDG&E's regulatory affairs team typically responds faster than the CPUC's public records office.

## Internal Proposal Tool audit — Save Draft, Record Acceptance, battery-only Site Map fix (2026-09-02, per Dennis)
Reported as three separate confusions while using the builder: *"There's not a save feature. There's no accept button on the Tech's end. It's asking for a site map (for a battery only sale)."* Full audit confirmed all three as real gaps, not user error. Two design questions were resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before building (both picked the recommended option).

### 1. Save Draft
The builder had no way to persist in-progress pricing short of actually sending it to the customer — closing the modal lost everything. **`sendProposal(id, approvalRequest, mode)`** now takes a third `mode` param; `mode==='draft'` writes `customers.proposal` with `status:'draft'` (never `'sent'`), skips the customer-facing send/notify side effects, and leaves the modal open instead of closing it. A new **"💾 Save Draft"** button (`propDraftBtn`) sits in the modal's action row next to Send. A draft is invisible to the customer (magic-link view only renders `status==='sent'`/`'accepted'` proposals) and reopens exactly where it was left via the existing "Load Previous" restore path — no new restore logic needed, `_propRestoreExisting` already keys off the newest `proposal` row regardless of status.

### 2. Record Acceptance (rep-side accept)
There was exactly one path to `acceptProposal` — the customer's own magic-link click. A deal closed in person or over the phone (common on FixMy/battery-retrofit calls, per the audit conversation) had no way to be marked accepted; reps were either fabricating a customer click or leaving real sold jobs stuck at `status:'sent'` forever.
- **`acceptProposal(id, optionId, financing, loanTerm, recordedBy)`** — new 5th param. When present, stamps `accepted_via:'rep_recorded'`, `accepted_by_rep_id`, `accepted_by_rep_name`, `accepted_recorded_at` on the accepted option alongside the normal accept fields, and returns early (skips the customer-only `#proposalWrap` DOM rewrite, which doesn't exist in the admin/tech context). The GHL acceptance webhook payload carries `accepted_via`/`recorded_by_rep` too, so downstream automations can tell a rep-recorded acceptance apart from a real customer click.
- **`window.openRecordAcceptance(id)`** — a modal listing every real `status:'sent'` option on the lead with per-financing-term Record buttons (mirrors the customer's own `selectLoanTerm` financing branches exactly: `cash`/`prepaid` vs. per-term loan options — this is where an earlier draft of the fix had wrongly hardcoded `financing:'participate'` for all Participate terms; corrected to match).
- **`window._recordAcceptConfirm(custId, optionId, financing, loanTerm, optIdx, repId, repName)`** — confirms, then calls `acceptProposal(..., {id:repId, name:repName})`. `repId`/`repName` come from the ambient `person` global in tech/sales context; admin context wraps the lookup in the same defensive `try{}catch(e){}` pattern already used elsewhere in the file for admin-without-`person` fallback.
- **"✅ Record Acceptance" button** added to `_proposalToolsRow`, gated on `!isNS && c.proposal && c.proposal.status==='sent'` — appears only once a proposal has actually been sent, disappears once accepted (nothing to record twice).

### 3. Site Map required for a battery-only sale
`_propCalcBaseProduction`'s battery-arbitrage math (`13.5 kWh × PW3 units × 1.5 cycles/day`) is entirely independent of the Google Solar Site Map/roof data — it only reads `pw3_retrofit`/`pw3_expansion_pack` line-item counts. But the builder always rendered the "🛰️ Site Map" button and, if no `solarData` existed, `propShowScenario` printed a generic "Missing system data — run Site Map" warning even for a pure battery deal with zero panels, where Site Map is genuinely irrelevant.
- **Site Map button now conditionally hidden.** `_isBatteryOnlyBuild = _hasBatteryItem && !_hasPanelOrInverterItem` (computed from the build's line items in the "workflow action buttons" block of `_propRenderBuilds`) wraps the button — it only renders when the build actually includes a panel or inverter item that could benefit from roof/yield data.
- **The warning message is now context-aware.** `propShowScenario`'s missing-data branch distinguishes "nothing selected yet" (`_noItemsYet`) from "items selected but still missing panel/inverter or battery data" — a battery-only build with real `pw3_*` counts no longer gets told to go run Site Map, since `hasData` is already true from the arbitrage math alone.
- **The warning no longer fires prematurely on builder-open.** The initial `setTimeout(...propShowScenario('moderate', bill, ...))` call that primes the Projected Savings preview the instant the modal opens was gated only on `bill > 0` — so it fired (and showed a "missing data" warning) before the rep had picked any catalog item at all. Now also requires `_propBuilds[0] && _propBuilds[0].lineItems.length > 0`, so the preview — and any warning it might show — only ever appears once there's actually something to preview.

Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit.

### A dead photo URL took down the whole Quoya Assist insight (fixed 2026-09-02)
Reported live off a screenshot: generating a proposal insight for a lead with uploaded photos returned "Could not generate insight: Claude API error 400: {...\"Unable to download the file. Please verify the URL and try again.\"}" — nothing generated at all, including the plain customer-facing paragraph that has nothing to do with photos.
- **Root cause:** `generate-proposal-insight.js` sends every uploaded photo as an `image` content block with `source:{type:'url', url:p.url}` — Anthropic fetches each URL server-side. All photo blocks ride in the SAME message as the customer paragraph request, so if even ONE photo's Storage URL is unreachable (a deleted file, a row whose object never actually finished uploading, a bucket-policy hiccup), Anthropic 400s the ENTIRE request and the rep gets nothing — not even the text-only insight the photos were never required for.
- **Fix:** `callClaude(withPhotos)` is now a retryable helper. On a 400 with photos attached, the function retries once with photos dropped entirely (a 400 on this endpoint is essentially always a request-shape problem — most commonly one bad image URL — never billing/auth/rate-limit, which come back as 401/403/429/529 instead, so this retry is bounded and cheap). The retry's result still returns a normal customer paragraph; a `photo_warning` field tells the rep photos couldn't be read this pass. `generateProposalInsight()` in portal.html prepends that warning as a bracketed, editable line at the top of the (already rep-reviewed-before-send) insight textarea — no new UI element, matches the existing "Rep reviews and edits before sending" contract.
- **Not fixed here, flagged:** WHY a specific photo's URL is unreachable wasn't diagnosed — the sandbox this was built in can't reach `supabase.co` to test the actual failing URL, and doing so wasn't necessary for the fix (the retry makes the failure mode survivable regardless of cause). If this recurs on the same lead, check whether that specific `job_photos` row's `url` actually resolves (deleted Storage object vs. a genuinely malformed URL) before assuming the retry-fallback is masking a real, fixable data problem.
- Same family as the Regrid `no_key`/Tracerfy 429/permit-tally lesson repeated throughout this file, one level removed: **a single failure inside a batched request must not be allowed to take the whole batch down when the rest of the request doesn't depend on it.**

Verified via `node --check` on `generate-proposal-insight.js` and the standard portal.html parse-check.

## Internal Proposal Tool — the real end-to-end SOP (2026-09-02, per Dennis)
Follow-up after the Save Draft/Record Acceptance/Site Map audit above: Dennis walked the full flow (Send to Customer → Copy Link → customer's magic link → Tech confirming Option 1) and found it dead-ends at three points. Two are now fixed in code; one needs a GHL Workflow only Dennis can build (same "portal fires a webhook, a GHL Workflow does the sending" pattern already used everywhere else in this file — diagnostic-fee flow, agreement flow, no-automation tagging).

**The corrected SOP, end to end:**
1. Tech/admin builds pricing in the Internal Proposal Tool (`openProposalBuilder`).
2. **Send to Customer** (`sendProposal`, non-draft) saves `customers.proposal` with `status:'sent'` AND now fires `GHL_PROPOSAL_WEBHOOK` with `type:'proposal_sent'` (email, phone, full_name, magic_link, top_option_title/price, rep_name). ⚠️ **This alone does not send anything** — a GHL Workflow bound to this webhook trigger has to exist to actually SMS/email the customer their link. See "Still needs Dennis" below.
3. **Copy Link** (`copyProposalLink`) is the rep-controlled fallback that works today with zero GHL setup — copies `https://fixmy.energy/portal?email=...&code=...` to the clipboard for the rep to text/email manually.
4. Customer opens the magic link → `loadCustomerDashboard` — **now auto-scrolls to `#proposalWrap`** the instant the page renders, whenever a real `status:'sent'`, not-yet-accepted proposal exists. Previously the proposal card rendered several sections down a long dashboard (info-grid → progress timeline → required-documents uploaders all came first) with nothing indicating it was even there — this is what Dennis meant by "clicking the link doesn't take them to Option 1."
5. Customer taps **Accept Option N** on their own device → `acceptProposal(custId, optionId, financing, loanTerm)` with no 5th arg, stamps `accepted_via:'customer_self_service'`, fires `GHL_PROPOSAL_WEBHOOK` with `type:'proposal_accepted'` (newly added, alongside the existing untyped payload fields — was previously the one payload on this webhook with no `type` at all).
6. **If the deal closes in person or by phone instead** (no customer magic-link click) — the "Record Acceptance" button in `_proposalToolsRow` (gated `c.proposal.status === 'sent'`, so it appears in the lead editor's Proposal Tools row only after step 2 has actually run) opens `openRecordAcceptance(id)`, listing every sent option with per-financing-term Record buttons → `acceptProposal(..., {id:repId, name:repName})`, stamping `accepted_via:'rep_recorded'` instead. **This is the "Tech can't confirm Option 1" fix** — it existed from the prior audit pass but wasn't discoverable from inside the builder/Preview modal, which is deliberately a dead-end mockup (labeled "(preview)", disabled Accept button) and was never meant to be the real accept path. Record Acceptance lives on the lead editor screen, one level out from the builder.

**Still needs Dennis — a GHL Workflow, not code:** build a Workflow triggered on the same webhook URL as `GHL_PROPOSAL_WEBHOOK` (`.../hooks/gXWwbOVymY0iRfj7c1It/webhook-trigger/224ee5c6-...`), branching on `type`:
- `type == proposal_sent` → send the customer an SMS + email with `{{trigger.magic_link}}`, using `{{trigger.full_name}}`/`{{trigger.top_option_title}}`/`{{trigger.top_option_price}}` in the message — this is the piece that makes "Send to Customer" actually send something.
- `type == proposal_accepted` → whatever post-acceptance automation Dennis wants (internal notification, contract kickoff, etc.) — new, previously had no `type` to branch on at all.
- `type == proposal_approval_request` → the pre-existing low-commission admin-notification branch (unchanged, already working).
Until that Workflow exists, "Send to Customer" is honest about it — the status message now reads "an SMS/email trigger fired (needs a GHL workflow to actually deliver it)" instead of implying delivery, and the modal stays open longer (2.6s) so the rep actually reads it before it auto-closes.

Verified via the standard `node -e "new Function(...)"` parse-check across all 4 inline `<script>` blocks after every edit.

## Common Patterns
```js
// Null guard for conditionally-rendered fields (prevents TypeError on NS leads)
(document.getElementById('edSys')||{}).value||null

// NS vs FixMy guard in saveLeadEditor
var isNSSave = cRec.lead_category === 'new_solar';
if (!isNSSave) { updates.deposit_status = ...; }

// Supabase client
var client = supabase.createClient(SUPA_URL, SUPA_KEY);
var { data, error } = await client.from('customers').update(updates).eq('id', id);
```
